# Mistakes found in published sources

The atlas's workers read papers and books line by line to extract and plan their mathematics, and they record every mistake they find in them: misprints (slips whose intended meaning is clear), errors (false statements or steps that fail) and gaps (steps asserted without an adequate proof). A finding counts as confirmed once an independent reviewer has checked it at its locator. Each finding says where its worker looked for an existing correction; a new one is not corrected in any erratum or later version they found. Nothing here has been sent to the authors.

Collected by `scripts/errata.py` from the paper extractions and blueprint packets (research/blueprint/PROTOCOL.md, section 18); the data is in `data/source-issues.json`.

0 new mistakes confirmed · 706 awaiting review · 93 already corrected in print · 0 rejected on review · 8 extractions and packets not yet checked.

## New mistakes, confirmed

None yet.

## New mistakes awaiting review

### Alpöge–Bhargava–Shnidman, "Rank stability in quadratic extensions and Hilbert's tenth problem for the ring of integers of a number field", Inventiones Mathematicae (2026) (`PAPER-ALPOGE-BHARGAVA-SHNIDMAN-26`)

- **Error** at Proof of Proposition 2.5, case 𝔪 ≠ 1, p. 1137 (published version); the same text in arXiv v1 p. 7 (it affects the proof). The source says `"We need only check that the equation t_1x² + 2rγ^{nℓ}t_2y² = t_3z² has a solution modulo 𝔪 for some choice of units t_1, t_2, t_3 modulo 𝔪. Since we are free to assume t_1 = t_3, there is no local obstruction at 2-adic primes 𝔭 | 𝔪."`; it should be `With t_1 = t_3 there can be an obstruction at a 2-adic 𝔭 | 𝔪, whenever v_𝔭(2rγ^{nℓ}) < v_𝔭(𝔪). The conclusion still holds. Repair: replace r by rδ^ℓ, where δ ∈ 𝒪_F is divisible by 𝔭^{v_𝔭(𝔪)} for each 2-adic 𝔭 | 𝔪 and by no other prime (it exists because the class group is finite). Then C_{q^ℓr²δ^{2ℓ}} ≅ C_{q^ℓr²} (§2: C_n ≅ C_{nλ^{2ℓ}}), so Lemma 2.3 and the sets S′, S, Σ are unchanged. The clause r ∉ 𝔭 still holds at the odd ramified primes, where the smooth-conic argument uses it. Now β = 2rδ^ℓγ^{nℓ} ≡ 0 modulo the 2-adic part of 𝔪, so x = y = z = 1 solves the congruence there with t_1 = t_3. Alternatively, keep r and choose t_1 ≠ t_3.`. Take F_0 = ℚ, K_0 = ℚ(i) and ℓ = 3, which does not divide Disc = −4. §4 replaces these by F = ℚ(ζ_3) and K = F(i) = ℚ(ζ_12), and K/F is unramified above 3. The prime (2) is inert in F/ℚ, so F_(2) = ℚ_4 is unramified over ℚ_2 with residue field 𝔽_4. F_(2)(i)/F_(2) has conductor exponent 2, so 𝔪 = (4). Since (2) ramifies in K, (2) ∉ S and γ ∉ (2); and r ∉ (2) by Lemma 2.3. Hence v_2(β) = 1. Suppose t x² + βt_2y² ≡ t z² (mod 4) with x, y, z units. Then (z/x)² ≡ 1 + βt_2y²/(tx²) (mod 4), which is ≡ 1 mod 2 but ≢ 1 mod 4. But a unit w with w² ≡ 1 (mod 2) has w ≡ 1 (mod 2), since squaring is bijective on 𝔽_4, and then w² ≡ 1 (mod 4). This is a contradiction. An exhaustive search over the 12 units of ℤ[ζ_3]/4 confirms it. Inertness of a principal prime (p) in K is detected by χ(p) = (−1)^{(N(p)−1)/2}, where N is the norm to ℚ; this is the Hilbert symbol (p, −1) at (2), trivial on global units. For every unit value of β/2 there is no solution with t_1 = t_3 and χ(t_1) = χ(t_2) = −1. Yet admissible residues exist with t_1 ≠ t_3: for β ≡ 2, u_1 = u_2 = 1 + 2ζ_3 and u_3 = u_1 + βu_2 ≡ 3 + 2ζ_3 (mod 4), with norms 3, 3, 7 ≡ 3 (mod 4). Recorded as `PAPER-ALPOGE-BHARGAVA-SHNIDMAN-26/E3`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2501.18774: only v1 exists (30 January 2025), read in full; the published version, Invent. Math. 243 (2025), 1129–1139 (open access, CC BY 4.0), read in full and compared with v1; Crossref metadata for 10.1007/s00222-025-01392-3: no update, erratum or correction relation (a filter for works updating this DOI returns none); the authors' research pages (A. Shnidman, W. Ho): no revised version or correction listed; the published version repeats the arXiv v1 wording of this step verbatim (p. 1137).
- **Misprint** at Proof of Proposition 3.1 (sketch), p. 1136 (published version); arXiv v1 p. 6. The source says `"The finite part of the singular series is (ignoring small primes) ∏_𝔭 lim_{n→∞} Nm(𝔭)(Nm(𝔭) − 1)^{−3} #{(p_1, p_2) : p_1, p_2, p_1 + βp_2 ∈ (𝒪_F/𝔭^n)^×}"`; it should be `∏_𝔭 lim_{n→∞} Nm(𝔭)^{3−2n}(Nm(𝔭) − 1)^{−3} #{(p_1, p_2) ∈ (𝒪_F/𝔭^n)² : p_1, p_2, p_1 + βp_2 ∈ (𝒪_F/𝔭^n)^×}: the count needs the normalization Nm(𝔭^{n−1})^{−2}. For 𝔭 ∤ β the local factor is Nm𝔭(Nm𝔭 − 2)/(Nm𝔭 − 1)² = 1 − (Nm𝔭 − 1)^{−2}.`. The condition is a condition modulo 𝔭, so the set counted has Nm(𝔭)^{2(n−1)} times as many elements as its reduction mod 𝔭. For 𝔭 ∤ β with q = Nm𝔭, the reduction has (q − 1)(q − 2) elements. The printed n-th term is therefore q^{2n−1}(q − 2)/(q − 1)², which tends to infinity for every 𝔭 with q ≥ 3, so the product as printed diverges. With the normalization the factors are 1 − (q − 1)^{−2} and the product converges. Shnidman, W. Ho): no revised version or correction listed; the published version prints the same formula (p. 1136).
- **Misprint** at Proof of Proposition 3.1 (sketch), p. 1135 (published version); arXiv v1 p. 6. The source says `"1/(Nm_{F/ℚ} C)^3 Σ_{α_1, α_2, α_3 ∈ 𝒪_F/(C)} e(−tr_{F/ℚ}(α_1u_1 + α_2u_2 + α_3u_3)/C) ∫_{ξ ∈ (𝔡_{F/ℚ}I)^{−1} ⊗_ℤ ℝ/ℤ} dξ S_N(ξ + α_1/C) S_N(βξ + α_2/C) S_N(−ξ + α_3/C)"`; it should be `To impose p_i ≡ u_i (mod C) the α_i must run over 𝔡_{F/ℚ}^{−1}/C𝔡_{F/ℚ}^{−1} (with the same normalization). With α_i ∈ 𝒪_F/(C), as printed, the expression counts solutions with p_i ≡ u_i modulo C𝔡_{F/ℚ}^{−1} ∩ 𝒪_F.`. For x ∈ 𝒪_F, Σ_{α ∈ 𝒪_F/C𝒪_F} e(tr(αx)/C) = Nm(C)·1[x ∈ C𝔡^{−1}], since α ↦ e(tr(αx/C)) is trivial on 𝒪_F exactly when x/C lies in the inverse different. C𝔡^{−1} ∩ 𝒪_F strictly contains C𝒪_F whenever C and 𝔡 share a prime. That happens in the application: C must be divisible by the primes of S above ℓ, and ℓ ramifies in F ⊇ ℚ(ζ_ℓ). - **Gap** at End of the proof of Proposition 2.5, p. 1137 (published version), with the statement of Proposition 3.1, p. 1135; arXiv v1 pp. 5–7 (it affects the proof). The source says `"For each of the infinitely many triples (p_1, p_2, p_3) produced by the above construction, the ideal 𝔱_{p_1,p_2,p_3} := (p_1^{(ℓ−1)/2} p_2 p_3^{(ℓ−1)/2}) is such that any large enough prime factor occurs with multiplicity strictly smaller than ℓ. Moreover the collection of such ideals has infinite support, else we would be producing infinitely many solutions to an 𝒮-unit equation for a finite set 𝒮 of primes, contradicting the theorem of Siegel and Mahler."`; it should be `The argument needs infinitely many pairwise non-proportional triples. Proposition 3.1 should say so; its proof gives it, since the circle-method count (or Kai's asymptotic) of triples of height ≤ N is ≫ N^{2d}/(log N)^3 with d = [F : ℚ], against O(N^d) triples proportional to a fixed one. The multiplicity claim should be stated for non-proportional triples.`. Suppose ε_1 + βε_2 = ε_3 has a solution in units ε_i ∈ 𝒪_F^×. Then for every I-prime π satisfying the congruences, (ε_1π, ε_2π, ε_3π) is a triple as in Proposition 3.1. For it 𝔱 = (π)^ℓ, up to a power of I, so its large prime factor has multiplicity exactly ℓ, contrary to the first sentence. It also yields the same (a, b) = (ε_1/ε_3, γ^{nℓ}ε_2/ε_3) for every π. Proposition 3.1 as stated ('infinitely many triples (p_1, p_2, p_3) (mod 𝒪_F^×)') does not exclude that all but finitely many of its triples are of this form. The paper does not check whether such unit solutions exist for the chosen β. So the step from Proposition 3.1 to the infinitude of X is incomplete as written, though it is repaired by the count in its proof. ### Anschütz–Le Bras, "Prismatic Dieudonné Theory", Forum of Mathematics, Pi (2023) (`PAPER-ANSCHUTZ-LEBRAS-23`)

- **Misprint** at Theorem 4.9.5 (published: Theorem 4.90), the statement; present in arXiv v4 and in the published version. The source says `Then the prismatic Dieudonné functor M_Δ(−) : BT(R) → DM^adm(R) from the category of p-divisible groups over R to the category of admissible prismatic Dieudonné crystals over R is essentially surjective.`; it should be `… to the category of admissible prismatic Dieudonné modules over R …`. R is assumed quasi-regular semiperfectoid and the displayed functor is the non-calligraphic M_Δ(−), which Definition 4.42 (arXiv Definition 4.2.8) defines as Ext^1_{(R)_qsyn}(G, O^pris) with values in prismatic Dieudonné modules over R; DM^adm(R) is used for both categories by the deliberate overloading of Definitions 4.8 and 4.19 (arXiv Definitions 4.1.8 and 4.1.15), but the words in the statement name the wrong one. The proof settles it: it begins 'let M ∈ DM^adm(R)' and speaks of 'a descent datum for expressing that it comes from an admissible prismatic Dieudonné module over R (namely, M)'. The crystal version of the same statement is Theorem 4.74 (arXiv Theorem 4.6.10), for a general quasi-syntomic ring. Recorded as `PAPER-ANSCHUTZ-LEBRAS-23/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: the arXiv listing and submission history of arXiv:1907.10525, read 22 September 2026: v1 (24 July 2019), v2 (6 February 2020), v3 (14 May 2021) and v4 (11 October 2022), the version read, whose comment records that the notion of filtered prismatic Dieudonné crystal was replaced by the equivalent notion of admissible prismatic Dieudonné crystal and that the proof of full faithfulness was corrected and simplified following a suggestion of Akhil Mathew; there is no later preprint version and no version carrying the erratum; the published version, doi:10.1017/fmp.2022.22 (Forum of Mathematics, Pi 11 (2023), e2, open access on Cambridge Core), read 22 September 2026, including its 'A correction has been issued for this article' notice; the erratum itself, Prismatic Dieudonné Theory – ERRATUM, doi:10.1017/fmp.2024.30 (Forum of Mathematics, Pi 13 (2025), e3, published 7 February 2025), read 22 September 2026: it is the only correction listed for the article; the numbering of the preprint against the numbering of the published version, statement by statement, in all six sections and the appendix; the two agree except that arXiv Remark 5.1.7, on Cartier duality for finite locally free group schemes, has no counterpart in the published version.
- **Misprint** at the sentence introducing Definition 5.2.4 (published: the sentence introducing Definition 5.11); present in arXiv v4 and in the published version. The source says `Recall the following definition, which already appeared in Proposition 4.26 before.`; it should be `Recall the following definition, which already appeared in Definition 4.24 before.`. The definition that follows is the definition of a Breuil–Kisin module over a prism and of the minuscule ones, repeated verbatim; it appeared as Definition 4.24 (arXiv Definition 4.1.24). Proposition 4.26 (arXiv Proposition 4.1.26) is the Cais–Lau equivalence between windows over the frame A_d and BK_min(A), which uses the definition but does not state it. The LaTeX source of arXiv v4 confirms the slip: the reference is \Cref{sec:abstr-divid-prism-remark-cais-lau-principal}, the label of Proposition 4.1.26, while the label of Definition 4.1.24, sec:comp-case-mathc-definition-breuil-kisin-module, is attached to both copies of the definition and is never referred to. Recorded as `PAPER-ANSCHUTZ-LEBRAS-23/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: the arXiv listing and submission history of arXiv:1907.10525, read 22 September 2026: v1 (24 July 2019), v2 (6 February 2020), v3 (14 May 2021) and v4 (11 October 2022), the version read, whose comment records that the notion of filtered prismatic Dieudonné crystal was replaced by the equivalent notion of admissible prismatic Dieudonné crystal and that the proof of full faithfulness was corrected and simplified following a suggestion of Akhil Mathew; there is no later preprint version and no version carrying the erratum; the published version, doi:10.1017/fmp.2022.22 (Forum of Mathematics, Pi 11 (2023), e2, open access on Cambridge Core), read 22 September 2026, including its 'A correction has been issued for this article' notice; the erratum itself, Prismatic Dieudonné Theory – ERRATUM, doi:10.1017/fmp.2024.30 (Forum of Mathematics, Pi 13 (2025), e3, published 7 February 2025), read 22 September 2026: it is the only correction listed for the article; the numbering of the preprint against the numbering of the published version, statement by statement, in all six sections and the appendix; the two agree except that arXiv Remark 5.1.7, on Cartier duality for finite locally free group schemes, has no counterpart in the published version.
- **Misprint** at Example 6.0.4 (published: Example A.4), the displayed chain of equalities; present in arXiv v4 and in the published version. The source says `p^{i}x_{i,0}=p^{i}fx_{i,1}=\ldots = f^{i}x_{i,i}\neq 0`; it should be `p^{i}x_{i,0}=p^{i−1}fx_{i,1}=\ldots = f^{i}x_{i,i}\neq 0`. The relations imposed are p x_{i,j} = f x_{i,j+1}. Substituting once gives p^i x_{i,0} = p^{i−1}(p x_{i,0}) = p^{i−1} f x_{i,1}, and each further substitution trades one p for one f, so that the chain is p^i x_{i,0} = p^{i−1}f x_{i,1} = p^{i−2}f^2 x_{i,2} = … = f^i x_{i,i}: the total degree in p and f is i throughout. As printed, the second term has degree i+1 and is in fact equal to f^{i+1}x_{i,i+1} = 0, since x_{i,i+1} = 0, which would contradict the assertion that the chain is non-zero. The next sentence, p^{i+1}x_{i,0} = f^i p x_{i,i} = 0, is correct. - **Misprint** at Definitions 1.3.1 and 1.3.5 (published: Definitions 1.7 and 1.11); present in arXiv v4 and in the published version. The source says `whose linearization φ^*M → M has its cokernel is killed by I^pris`; it should be `whose linearization φ^*M → M has its cokernel killed by I^pris`. A stray 'is'. ### Antieau–Mathew–Morrow–Nikolaus, "On the Beilinson fiber square", Duke Mathematical Journal (2022) (`PAPER-ANTIEAU-MATHEW-MORROW-ETAL-22`)

- **Misprint** at Example 7.3, the two stated ranges in the computation of the cyclic homology of k over W(k); in arXiv v2, the version read (the published version could not be consulted). The source says `Using the filtrations of [antieauperiodic], we conclude for i ≤ 2p−1, π_i HC(k/W(k)) ≅ W(k)/p^{s+1} if i = 2s ≥ 0, 0 otherwise. … Thus, in the range 0 ≤ i ≤ 2p−2, we deduce that π_i HC((W(k),(p)/W(k)) ≅ p^{s+1}W(k) if i = 2s, 0 if i is odd.`; it should be `The two ranges should be i ≤ 2p−4 and 0 ≤ i ≤ 2p−4 (equivalently i ≤ 2p−3, since the odd groups vanish).`. Both formulas are read off from the sentence immediately before them, that LΩ_k/LΩ_k^{≥s} ≅ W(k)/p^s for s ≤ p−1, applied with s+1 in place of s: π_{2s}HC(k/W(k)) = LΩ_k/LΩ_k^{≥s+1}. That needs s+1 ≤ p−1, i.e. i = 2s ≤ 2p−4. At the first excluded value, i = 2p−2 and s = p−1, the formula would give W(k)/p^p, but LΩ_k here is the p-completed divided power envelope of (p) ⊆ W(k) — that is, W(k) itself — and its Hodge filtration is the divided power filtration, whose p-th step is generated by γ_p(p) = p^p/p!, of p-adic valuation p − 1 since v_p(p!) = 1. So LΩ_k/LΩ_k^{≥p} = W(k)/p^{p−1} and π_{2p−2}HC(k/W(k)) ≅ W(k)/p^{p−1}, not W(k)/p^p; correspondingly the relative group is p^{p−1}W(k), not p^pW(k). The conclusion of the example is unaffected: it is drawn in the range 1 ≤ i ≤ 2p−5, where the odd degrees used are i = 2s+1 ≤ 2p−5, so s ≤ p−3 and s+1 ≤ p−2, well inside the range where the de Rham formula holds. Recorded as `PAPER-ANTIEAU-MATHEW-MORROW-ETAL-22/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: the arXiv listing and submission history of arXiv:2003.12541, read 22 September 2026: v1 (27 March 2020) and v2 (29 September 2021), the version read, whose comment records many minor updates from the referee reports and a new, simplified proof of the integral comparison theorem for syntomic cohomology; there is no later preprint version; the published version, Duke Mathematical Journal 171 (2022), no. 18, doi:10.1215/00127094-2022-0037: Duke is not open access and the published text could not be read here, so the findings below are recorded against arXiv v2, the version read, and it is not known whether they were corrected in print; the Crossref record for the article lists no correction or erratum; the authors' home pages linked from the issue, and a search for an erratum or corrigendum attached to the article: none found.
- **Misprint** at Example 7.3, the displayed formula for the relative cyclic homology; in arXiv v2, the version read (the published version could not be consulted). The source says `\pi_i\HC((W(k),(p)/W(k))\simeq\begin{cases}p^{s+1}W(k) & \text{if }i=2s, \\ 0 & \text{if }i\textrm{ is odd}\end{cases}`; it should be `π_i HC(W(k), (p)/W(k)) — one opening parenthesis too many.`. The relative cyclic homology of the pair (W(k), (p)) relative to W(k) is written HC(W(k), (p)/W(k)) everywhere else in the same example, for instance two sentences earlier in 'the cyclic homology term may be replaced by the cyclic homology τ_{≤2p−5}ΣHC(W(k),(p)/W(k)) over W(k)' and in Remark 7.4, which writes τ_{≤2p−5}ΣHC(O_F,(p)/W(k)). The extra parenthesis is unmatched in the LaTeX source and prints as such. Recorded as `PAPER-ANTIEAU-MATHEW-MORROW-ETAL-22/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: the arXiv listing and submission history of arXiv:2003.12541, read 22 September 2026: v1 (27 March 2020) and v2 (29 September 2021), the version read, whose comment records many minor updates from the referee reports and a new, simplified proof of the integral comparison theorem for syntomic cohomology; there is no later preprint version; the published version, Duke Mathematical Journal 171 (2022), no. 18, doi:10.1215/00127094-2022-0037: Duke is not open access and the published text could not be read here, so the findings below are recorded against arXiv v2, the version read, and it is not known whether they were corrected in print; the Crossref record for the article lists no correction or erratum; the authors' home pages linked from the issue, and a search for an erratum or corrigendum attached to the article: none found.

### Bakker–Klingler–Tsimerman, "Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci", Journal of the American Mathematical Society (2020) (`PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20`)

- **Error** at Theorem 4.1, p.13 of the author copy (= arXiv 1810.04801v2, p.13); the JAMS typeset version was not collated (publisher PDF not obtainable here, verification gap G1) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Theorem 4.1. Let V be a polarized variation of pure Hodge structures of weight k over the punctured polydisk (∆*)^n, with period map Φ: (∆*)^n → S_{Γ,G,M}. Then Φ is R_an,exp-definable." (∆ is the unit disk, p.4.)`; it should be `Definability holds only after shrinking. For some radius 0 < ρ < 1, the restriction of Φ to (∆*_ρ)^n is R_an,exp-definable, and then for every ρ < 1 by compactness of the remaining annular pieces. The reduction in §4.1 must use charts whose shrunken polydisks still cover the compactification.`. Counterexample (n = 1, trivial monodromy). Let f(z) = exp(−(1+z)/(1−z)) on the unit disk, so |f| < 1, and τ = 2i + εf with ε small, so that τ(∆) lies in a disk on which π: H → Γ\H is injective (Γ torsion-free, e.g. Γ(3)). τ is the period map of a polarized weight-one integral VHS. But Φ^{−1}(π(2i + ε/e)) contains the points z_n = 2πin/(2 + 2πin), n ≠ 0, where f = 1/e. These form an infinite discrete set accumulating at 1, which is impossible for a fibre of a definable map. The proof itself allows for this: it says 'after maybe shrinking the polydisk' (p.14), and its Siegel set S_H (y > 1) covers only |q| < e^{−2π}. Theorem 1.3 is unaffected, since §4.1 can use finitely many shrunken charts. Checked numerically: |f(z_n) − 1/e| < 1e−8 for 0 < |n| ≤ 2000. Noted by the extraction (item local-outer-boundary-obstruction). Recorded as `PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: The official erratum (JAMS 36 (2023), DOI 10.1090/jams/1025), read in full: it corrects only the maximal-compact dependence, morphisms and the Siegel-set pullback citation; arXiv 1810.04801 v2 (2020-06-22, the latest version): same wording at each locator; Benjamin Bakker's web page (author copy and erratum).
- **Gap** at Proof of Lemma 4.5, p.15 of the author copy (= arXiv v2 p.15); the JAMS typeset version was not collated (publisher PDF not obtainable here, verification gap G1) (it affects the proof). The source says `"Consider for each positive integer m and real number c the function f_{m,c} which we obtain from f by setting z_1 = mz_2 + c. Note that the assumptions of the lemma still apply to f_{m,c}, and it follows by induction on n that f_{m,c} ≪ 1 on all of Σ_{n−1}"`; it should be `State the lemma, and verify its curve hypothesis, on the wider sectors Σ_n^R = {|x_i| < R, y_1 ≥ ⋯ ≥ y_n > 1} for every R, with constants depending on R. The substitution then maps Σ_{n−1}^{R′} into Σ_n^{mR′+c}, and the induction goes through unchanged. In the application in §4.5, the curve bounds come from Schmid's one-variable Corollary 5.29 and the norm estimates of Theorem 4.8, which hold for real parts in any bounded interval; Theorem 1.5 is already stated on H^n_{R,η} for all R.`. Σ_n is defined with 0 < x_i < 1 (Definition 4.4). The proof uses c = 0, 1, …, r_1 and m ≥ 1. For c ≥ 1, x_1 = mx_2 + c > 1, so Σ_n ∩ {z_1 = mz_2 + c} is empty. For c = 0 it is only the strip x_2 < 1/m. So the hypothesis gives no information about f_{m,c} on Σ_{n−1}. The functions extend there (the coefficients in O are periodic in x and f is polynomial in x), but the bound is assumed only on Σ_n. With the R-uniform hypothesis, the finite difference in c isolates the top x_1-degree, the Vandermonde step in m isolates each y-power, and |x_1| < 1 on Σ_n finishes the proof, as printed. Noted by the extraction (verification gap G4); the repair is given by this continuation. Recorded as `PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: The official erratum (JAMS 36 (2023), DOI 10.1090/jams/1025), read in full: it corrects only the maximal-compact dependence, morphisms and the Siegel-set pullback citation; arXiv 1810.04801 v2 (2020-06-22, the latest version): same wording at each locator; Benjamin Bakker's web page (author copy and erratum).
- **Error** at Proof of Theorem 1.5 (§4.5), p.18 of the author copy; the JAMS typeset version was not collated (publisher PDF not obtainable here, verification gap G1). The source says `"Moreover, if b is (e′, C′)-reduced and e is a basis for which condition (3) of Definition 4.11 holds for some C > 0, then b will also be (e, C″)-reduced for some C″ = C″(e, C, e′, C′) > 0."`; it should be `Condition (2), the ordering b(e_i) < C b(e_j) for i < j, transfers only up to a permutation of e. Conclude that b is (σe, C″)-reduced for some permutation σ, which is one of finitely many.`. Take e′ = (e_1, e_2), e = (e_2, e_1) and b = diag(1, T) with T ≥ 1. Then b is (e′, 2)-reduced and satisfies condition (3) for e (b(e_2)b(e_1) = T = det b), but condition (2) for e would need T = b(e_2) < C″b(e_1) = C″ for all T. The proof immediately adds 'we may assume (2) as there are only finitely many orderings of the basis', which is the corrected form, so Theorem 1.5 is unaffected. Noted by the extraction (item basis-change-reducedness). Recorded as `PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20/E3`; looked for an existing correction in: The official erratum (JAMS 36 (2023), DOI 10.1090/jams/1025), read in full: it corrects only the maximal-compact dependence, morphisms and the Siegel-set pullback citation; arXiv 1810.04801 v2 (2020-06-22, the latest version): same wording at each locator; Benjamin Bakker's web page (author copy and erratum).
- **Misprint** at Appendix A.1, p.20 of the author copy; the JAMS typeset version was not collated (publisher PDF not obtainable here, verification gap G1). The source says `"Given X a real-analytic n-manifold with corners, one defines its boundary ∂X (cf. [Joy12, def. 2.6]. This is a real-analytic n-manifold with corners for n > 0"`; it should be `∂X is a real-analytic (n − 1)-manifold with corners.`. The boundary of an n-manifold with corners in Joyce's sense is (n − 1)-dimensional; for [0,∞) it is a point. The appendix itself later says 'the compact real-analytic (n − 1)-manifold with corners ∂X' (proof of Proposition A.2). Noted by the extraction (item corner-boundary). Recorded as `PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20/E4`; looked for an existing correction in: The official erratum (JAMS 36 (2023), DOI 10.1090/jams/1025), read in full: it corrects only the maximal-compact dependence, morphisms and the Siegel-set pullback citation; arXiv 1810.04801 v2 (2020-06-22, the latest version): same wording at each locator; Benjamin Bakker's web page (author copy and erratum).

The source says `irreducible iff transitive`; it should be `Require separability and positive degree for the iff on the distinct root set; irreducible characteristic-zero polynomials meet these hypotheses.`. ### Bennett–Siksek, "A conjecture of Erdős, supersingular primes and short character sums", Annals of Mathematics 191 (2020), no. 2 (`PAPER-BENNETT-SIKSEK-20`)

- **Error** at Lemma 3.2, p.361, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 191 (2020), 355–392 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 3920a752…). The source says `"Its discriminant is Δ_a = 64(a_a b_a c_a)² = (2⁸/g⁶)(n+id)²(n+jd)²(n+(2j−i)d)²."`; it should be `Δ_a = 16(a_a b_a c_a)² = (2⁶/g⁶)(n+id)²(n+jd)²(n+(2j−i)d)².`. For y² = x³ + a₂x² + a₄x, Δ = 16a₄²(a₂² − 4a₄). For E_a: Y² = X(X−a)(X+c), a₂ = c − a and a₄ = −ac, so Δ = 16a²c²(a+c)² = 16(abc)² since a+b+c = 0; with b_a = −2(n+jd)/g this is 2⁶/g⁶ times the product. The extraction found this (item 16, report D1); the review re-derived it. ### Benoist–Wittenberg, "On the integral Hodge conjecture for real varieties, I", Inventiones Mathematicae (2020) (`PAPER-BENOIST-WITTENBERG-20`)

- **Error** at Theorem 3.16(ii), p.55, and Theorem A(ii), p.4, in the published version, Inventiones mathematicae 222 (2020), 1–77 (author-hosted copy with journal pagination, SHA-256 daeb43ec…46a9) (it affects a stated result). The source says `Theorem A: "Let X be a smooth, proper and geometrically irreducible real variety, of dimension d ≥ 1. Assume that X satisfies the real integral Hodge conjecture for 1-cycles and that H²(X, O_X) = 0. … (ii) There exists a geometrically irreducible curve of even geometric genus in X if and only if the natural map Pic(X)[2^∞] → Pic(X_C)^G[2^∞] is onto." Theorem 3.16 assumes "dim(X) > 0".`; it should be `Add the hypothesis d ≥ 2 (or X(R) = ∅); the proof, which goes through Proposition 3.9, needs d ≥ 2 when X(R) ≠ ∅.`. Counterexample in dimension one: X = E, the real elliptic curve y² = x³ − x. Here H²(E, O_E) = 0 and the Hodge conjecture for 1-cycles (codimension 0) holds. The only geometrically irreducible curve in E is E itself, of geometric genus 1, so the left side fails. Since E(R) ≠ ∅, Br(R) → Br(E) is injective and Pic(E) → Pic(E_C)^G is an isomorphism, so the right side holds. Proposition 3.9 (a curve of odd geometric genus exists) is stated for dimension ≥ 2, as the introduction says (p.4). The surface and higher-dimensional cases, which are the paper's applications, are unaffected. Found by the extraction (items printed-even-genus, main-even-genus). Recorded as `PAPER-BENOIST-WITTENBERG-20/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: O. Wittenberg's publications page https://www.math.univ-paris13.fr/~wittenberg/ lists this paper and its sequel (part II, J. Éc. polytech. Math. 7 (2020)) with no erratum for part I, while it does list an erratum for another Benoist–Wittenberg paper (checked 22 September 2026); arXiv 1801.00872: v1 (2018), v2 (2019), v3 (11 March 2020); v3 has the same Theorem 3.16; web search for an erratum or corrigendum to part I, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Misprint** at §2.2, paragraph before Example 2.5, p.37, in the published version, Inventiones mathematicae 222 (2020), 1–77 (author-hosted copy with journal pagination, SHA-256 daeb43ec…46a9). The source says `"Namely, let X be a smooth and proper variety over a real closed field R and let k ≥ 0 be even, so that Z(2k) = Z(k). We can view ω^{2k} … as an element of H^{2k}_G(X(C), Z(k)). This class is Hodge if R = R, being torsion, and it belongs to H^{2k}_G(X(C), Z(k))_0 if (and only if) X(R) = ∅."`; it should be `"let k > 0 be even".`. For k = 0, ω^0 = 1 is not torsion, and it is the class of X itself, hence algebraic and in the subgroup cut out by the topological condition even when X(R) ≠ ∅; the "if and only if" fails. For even k > 0 the statement is as printed. Noted by the extraction (item constant-omega). Recorded as `PAPER-BENOIST-WITTENBERG-20/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: O. Wittenberg's publications page https://www.math.univ-paris13.fr/~wittenberg/ lists this paper and its sequel (part II, J. Éc. polytech. Math. 7 (2020)) with no erratum for part I, while it does list an erratum for another Benoist–Wittenberg paper (checked 22 September 2026); arXiv 1801.00872: v1 (2018), v2 (2019), v3 (11 March 2020); v3 has the same Theorem 3.16; web search for an erratum or corrigendum to part I, 22 September 2026: none found.

### Bhargava–Gross–Wang, "A positive proportion of locally soluble hyperelliptic curves over Q have no point over any odd degree extension", Journal of the American Mathematical Society (2017) (`PAPER-BHARGAVA-GROSS-WANG-17`)

- **Error** at Proof of Theorem 28, p.19 (arXiv v2) (it affects the proof). The source says `"When z_0 ≠ 0, we have α = (x − T)(Q) = x_0 − β. Then x′ = Span{(1, 0, 0), (β, 0, 0), . . ., (β^{g−1}, 0, 0), (β^g, 1, −½(x_0 + f_1/f_0))} is isotropic with respect to both bilinear forms." and, for z_0 = 0, "x′ = Span{(h_1(β) − h_1(x_0), 0, 0), (β − x_0, 0, 0), . . . , ((β − x_0)^{g−1}, 0, 0), ((β − x_0)^g, 1, −½((2g + 1)x_0 + f_1/f_0))}"`; it should be `For g ≥ 1 neither plane is isotropic for B′: the (g − 1)-st basis vector pairs with the last one to −1 in the first case (+1, or h_1(x_0), in the second). The fix is to give that vector the third coordinate that cancels the pairing through ab′ + a′b: - z_0 ≠ 0: replace (β^{g−1}, 0, 0) by (β^{g−1}, 0, 1); - z_0 = 0, g ≥ 2: replace ((β − x_0)^{g−1}, 0, 0) by ((β − x_0)^{g−1}, 0, 1); - z_0 = 0, g = 1: replace (h_1(β) − h_1(x_0), 0, 0) by (h_1(β) − h_1(x_0), 0, −h_1(x_0)). The corrected planes are isotropic for A′ and B′ and avoid the radical of A′, so the solubility claim and Theorem 28 stand.`. With ⟨λ, μ⟩_{B′} = [β^{n−1}](αβλμ) + ab′ + a′b and α = x_0 − β: ⟨(β^{g−1}, 0, 0), (β^g, 1, −c)⟩_{B′} = [β^{2g+1}]((x_0 − β)β^{2g}) = −1 ≠ 0. All the other pairings of the printed plane vanish. In the Weierstrass case, h_1(β)(β − x_0) = f(β, 1) = 0 in L gives the same pairing −[β^{2g+1}](β(β − x_0)^{2g}) = −1 for g ≥ 2. The Gram matrices were computed symbolically: the printed planes fail and the corrected ones are isotropic in random examples with g = 1, 2, 3 in both cases. - **Misprint** at §8, second example, p.23 (arXiv v2). The source says `"… its Jacobian E, which is an elliptic curve with equation y² + xy = x³ − x² − 929x − 10595 and prime conductor 571."`; it should be `y² + y = x³ − x² − 929x − 10595 (Cremona's 571b1).`. With PARI: the printed curve [1, −1, 0, −929, −10595] has conductor 43792070 and analytic rank 1. The curve [0, −1, 1, −929, −10595] has conductor 571, rank 0, trivial torsion and analytic Ш of order 4 = |(Z/2)²|. It is isomorphic to the Jacobian y² = x³ − 27Ix − 27J of the quartic (minimal model computed from I, J). The other numbers of both examples check: Δ = −2⁸·571 and −3⁵·11²·37²; the field of discriminant −2⁴·571 with O_L = Z[θ]; N(θ³ − θ) = −36; F(1), F(0), F(−1) = −2, −3, −6; the first quartic's Jacobian is y² = x³ − 3⁶·1221; its analytic Ш has order 16. - **Gap** at Theorem 44, pp.34–35 (arXiv v2) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Suppose F is a large congruence family … for which there exist two primes p, q neither of which is a quadratic residue modulo the other such that … 1. f, pf, qf, pqf all lie inside F and the hyperelliptic curves have points over Q_p and Q_q. 2. … J has split semistable reduction of toric dimension 1 at p and good reduction at q."`; it should be `Add the conditions at 2 whenever 2 has a unique prime above it in Q(√p, √q). That happens exactly when one of p, q is ≡ 3 mod 4 and the other ≡ 5 mod 8, e.g. (p, q) = (5, 3). The condition needed is that J has good reduction at 2 and the four twists have Q_2-points. Alternatively, restrict to p ≡ q ≡ 1 mod 4, or to the prime ≡ 1 mod 4 being ≡ 1 mod 8.`. The natural proof (none is written) applies Theorem A.3 with F = Q(√p, √q) and p_0 = p. Theorem A.3 requires good reduction and local points of all four twists at every prime with a unique prime above it in F. The primes p and q have this property, as the hypothesis makes each inert in the other's quadratic field. So does 2 when it ramifies in one quadratic subfield and is inert in another. For p = 5, q = 3: 2 is inert in Q(√5) and ramified in Q(√3). The hypotheses of Theorem 44 say nothing at 2. Recorded as `PAPER-BHARGAVA-GROSS-WANG-17/E3`; looked for an existing correction in: J. The source says `"Define g(x, y) = x^n + f_1x^{n−1}y + f_0f_2x^{n−2}y + · · · + f_0^{n−1}f_ny^n." "By the Weil conjectures, the curve y² = l(x) has at least q + 1 − n√q > n rational points over F_q. So there is at least one whose x-coordinate is not a root of f." "[33] A. Skorobogatov, Torsors and rational points, Cambridge Tracts in Mathematics 114, 2007." "[34] M. Stoll, … Algebra and Number Theory 1 (1997), 349–391."`; it should be `"f_0f_2x^{n−2}y²"; "> 2n + 2 rational points" (which q > 4n² also gives); "Cambridge Tracts in Mathematics 144, 2001"; "Algebra and Number Theory 1 (2007)".`. g must be homogeneous of degree n for g(f_0x, y) = f_0^{n−1}f(x, y). The points whose x-coordinate is a root of f, or at infinity, number up to 2n + 2, so "> n" does not by itself yield a good point, though q + 1 − n√q > 2n² does. Crossref gives Skorobogatov's tract as no. 144 (2001, DOI 10.1017/CBO9780511549588), and Algebra & Number Theory began in 2007 (DOI 10.2140/ant.2007.1.349). Recorded as `PAPER-BHARGAVA-GROSS-WANG-17/E5`; looked for an existing correction in: J. Amer. Math. ### Bhargava–Shankar–Taniguchi–Thorne–Tsimerman–Zhao, "Bounds on 2-torsion in class groups of number fields and integral points on elliptic curves", Journal of the American Mathematical Society (2020) (`PAPER-BHARGAVA-SHANKAR-TANIGUCHI-ETAL-20`)

- **Misprint** at Bombieri–Pila, The number of integral points on arcs and ovals, Oxford author preprint, Lemma 7 proof, printed p.14 (PDF index 13). (it affects the proof). The source says `lambda = ell A^(-1/k)/N`; it should be `Use lambda = ell A^(1/k)/N, where ell is the interval length. Then Taylor gives lambda^k≤lambda^(k−1)+...+lambda+2.`. Divide the Taylor inequality by N. The k-th-order term is ell^k A/N^k, the k-th power of the corrected dimensionless quantity. The polynomial identity in supplement BP2 proves lambda≤2, retaining the stated interval bound. Recorded as `PAPER-BHARGAVA-SHANKAR-TANIGUCHI-ETAL-20/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Web search on 2026-09-22: Bombieri Pila "arcs and ovals" erratum; Web search on 2026-09-22: "Bombieri" "Pila" "Since p"; Read the Oxford-hosted author preprint at https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/pila/Ovals.pdf, especially printed pp.14–17; no existing correction was located in these searches..
- **Misprint** at Bombieri–Pila Oxford author preprint, Theorem 4 proof, printed p.17 (PDF index 16), just before the estimate for H+K. (it affects the proof). The source says `Since p ≤ q`; it should be `Since q ≤ p.`. For the selected monomials X^i Y^j, q=Σj while p=Σ(i+j), and i≥0. Since A>1 this gives A^q≤A^p, the direction needed in the next estimate. Supplement BP4 writes out the entire subsequent bound. - **Gap** at Bombieri–Pila Oxford author preprint, Theorem 4 proof, printed p.17 (PDF index 16), final choice of the monomial cutoff δ. (it affects the proof). The source says `δ = 2 sqrt(log N/(d log log N))`; it should be `Take the floor of the displayed real number and prove the threshold and error inequalities; supplement BP5 proves δ≥2d and 4 log N/δ+9dδ log δ≤11 sqrt(d log N log log N).`. The monomial cutoff used to define D and p must be an integer. The printed real choice alone does not supply an admissible cutoff or justify the exact constant. Monotonicity of the objective between its floor and that real choice proves that rounding down preserves the estimate; the stated theorem and threshold need not change. The source says `sqrt(pi) |Disc(K)|^(1/(2n)) |alpha|_v^(-1/m)`; it should be `For every complex place, square the whole displayed factor: its disc contributes pi R_v^2, not sqrt(pi) R_v. The body volume is 2^r pi^s sqrt(D_K)/N(I).`. A complex coordinate is two real dimensions. With K=Q(i), I=O_K and alpha=1, the radius is sqrt(2); the disc area is 2pi rather than sqrt(2pi). The latter is below the Minkowski threshold 4 in the ordinary metric, whereas the correct area is above it. The existing balanced-body item already uses the corrected formula, so the theorem and its route are unchanged. ### Bhargava–Shankar–Wang, "Squarefree values of polynomial discriminants I", Inventiones Mathematicae (2022) (`PAPER-BHARGAVA-SHANKAR-WANG-22`)

- **Error** at §1, p.4 (arXiv v3). The source says `"Then p² strongly divides ∆(f) if and only if f modulo p has at least two distinct multiple roots in F̄_p, or has a root in F_p of multiplicity at least 3; and p² weakly divides ∆(f) if p² | ∆(f) but f modulo p has only one multiple root in F_p and this root is a simple double root."`; it should be `This holds for odd p. For p = 2, 4 always divides Δ(f) strongly when 2 | Δ(f), so W^{(2)}_m is empty for even m.`. By Stickelberger's congruence, the discriminant of a monic integer polynomial is ≡ 0 or 1 mod 4, so 2 | Δ(f + 2g) implies 4 | Δ(f + 2g) for every g. For example f = x² + 1 ≡ (x + 1)² mod 2 has a single simple double root, yet Δ(x² + a_1x + a_2) = a_1² − 4a_2 ≡ 0 mod 4 whenever a_1 is even. A brute-force check over all f mod p confirms the criterion for p = 3 (n ≤ 5) and p = 5 (n ≤ 4), and finds failures at p = 2 for every n = 2, …, 5. The proofs only use the implication "two multiple roots or a triple root ⇒ strong", which holds at p = 2 as well (in Proposition 2.2), and the tail estimates concern large primes, so nothing else changes. Recorded as `PAPER-BHARGAVA-SHANKAR-WANG-22/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Invent. Pi 13 (2025)) does not revisit these points.
- **Error** at Lemma 5.1 and proof, p.24 (arXiv v3). The source says `"By the uniqueness of Minkowski-reduced bases for these rings, φ must map Minkowski basis elements to Minkowski basis elements, i.e., φ(h_i(θ)) = h*_i(θ*) for all i. In particular, this is true for i = 1, so φ(θ) = θ* + c for some c ∈ Z … It follows that g(x) = g*(x), a contradiction."`; it should be `A Minkowski-reduced basis is unique at best up to the signs of its vectors: with α_1, …, α_n, every ±α_1, …, ±α_n is also Minkowski-reduced. So φ(h_1(θ)) = ±h*_1(θ*), φ(θ) = ±θ* + c, and the correct conclusion is g*(x) = g(x) or g*(x) = (−1)^ng(−x). Lemma 5.1 holds as stated only for pairs not related by x ↦ −x. In Corollaries 1.3 and 1.4 each field then comes from at most two of the counted polynomials, so the lower bounds are unchanged. Remark 5.6 already counts θ ↦ ±θ + c.`. Counterexample: g = x³ − 50x + 7 and g* = x³ − 50x − 7 are distinct, have vanishing x²-coefficients and are strongly quasi-reduced. For both, an exhaustive search gives the Minkowski-reduced basis 1, θ, θ² − 33, unique up to sign, with squared lengths 3, 100, 1667. Yet x ↦ −x is an isomorphism Z[x]/(g) ≅ Z[x]/(g*). If "unique" were meant literally, including signs, no lattice would be strongly quasi-reduced and Lemma 5.3 would fail instead. Each class meets the region {H < X} in Vol/m^{κn} + O(X^{n(n+1)/2−1}/m^{κ(n−1)}) points, the error coming from the a_1-direction, of length ≍ X. An error X^{n(n+1)/2−n} per m cannot hold: already for m = 1 the count #{H(f) < X} = 2^nX^{n(n+1)/2} + O(X^{n(n+1)/2−1}) of the introduction has an error of exact order X^{n(n+1)/2−1}, since the number of a_1 in (−X, X) fluctuates by O(1). The source says `"When n ≥ 4 is even and the polynomial factors as g(x)ḡ(x) over a quadratic extension, the splitting field has degree at most 2(n/2)! and so the index of the corresponding Galois group in S_n is again at least n(n−1)/2."`; it should be `The splitting field has degree at most 2((n/2)!)², so the index is at least C(n, n/2)/2. That is ≥ n(n − 1)/2 only for n ≥ 8. For n = 6 the index can be 10, but n/[S_n : H] ≤ 3/5 < 1 still gives the stated bound. For n = 4 the index can be 3 (Galois group D_4), and the argument yields O_ε(X^{6+4/3+ε}) = O_ε(X^{22/3+ε}) for these quartics. So Proposition 4.3 is proved for n = 4 only in the weaker form #V_4(Z)^red_X = O_ε(X^{22/3+ε}) = O(X^{n(n+1)/2−8/3+ε}).`. Over K = Q(√d) the splitting fields of g and ḡ have degree up to (n/2)! each, and their compositum up to ((n/2)!)². The Galois group of f lies in S_{n/2} ≀ S_2, of order 2((n/2)!)². Example: x⁴ − 2 = (x² − √2)(x² + √2) over Q(√2) has Galois group D_4 of order 8 > 2·2! = 4. With [S_4 : D_4] = 3, Dietmann's bound for |a_4| ≤ X⁴ is X^{4/3+ε} per (a_1, a_2, a_3), against the X^{2/(n−1)} = X^{2/3} the proof claims. ### Bhargava–Shankar–Wang, "Squarefree values of polynomial discriminants II", Forum of Mathematics, Pi (2025) (`PAPER-BHARGAVA-SHANKAR-WANG-25`)

- **Error** at Proof of Proposition A.1, p.53 (ν_1), Proposition A.1, p.53, and Theorem 1, p.2 (n = 4) (it affects a stated result). The source says `ν_1(n, p) = "(1 − p^{−1})²(1 − (−p)^{−n})(1 + p)^{−1} if n ≥ 4, p ≠ 2"; α_4(p) = "(1 − 1/p)²(1 + 2/p − 2/p⁴ + 1/p⁵)"; Theorem 1: "(3/8)∏_{p>2}(1 − 1/p)²(1 + 2/p − 2/p⁴ + 1/p⁵) ≈ 21.18% if n = 4".`; it should be `ν_1(n, p) = (1 − p^{−1})²(1 − (−p)^{2−n})/(1 + p) for n ≥ 3; for n = 3 this is the printed p^{−1}(1 − p^{−1})². Then α_4(p) = (1 − 1/p)²(1 + 1/p)(1 + 1/p − 1/p²), the same as for n ≥ 5. The density in Theorem 1 for n = 4 is (3/8)∏_{p>2}(1 − 1/p)²(1 + 1/p)(1 + 1/p − 1/p²) ≈ 20.83%, the n ≥ 5 value.`. ν_0(n, p) + ν_1(n, p) must be the density of p² ∤ Δ among monic polynomials, which is Yamamura's λ_n(p) = 1 − (3p^{n−1} − p^{n−2} + (−1)^n(p − 1)²)/(p^n(p + 1)) (Part I, verified there by exhaustive counts). Solving gives the corrected ν_1. For n = 4, p = 3 the printed ν_1 gives ν_0 + ν_1 = 566/729, against λ_4(3) = 62/81 = 558/729. Exhaustive counts of binary quartics mod p² give α_4(3) = 176/243 and α_4(5) = 2784/3125. These equal the corrected formula and differ from the printed one (1600/2187, 69856/78125). The error enters only through the ν_1(4, p) term of subset 1. For n ≥ 5 the terms ν_1(n) + ν_1(n − 1)/p combine to (1 − 1/p)²/p with either exponent, so those formulas are right. So are α_2, α_3 and β_n (all checked by exhaustive counts). The PARI values are 0.2082756 (corrected) against 0.2117949 (printed). Recorded as `PAPER-BHARGAVA-SHANKAR-WANG-25/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Forum Math. - **Misprint** at Various, pp.3–51. The source says `p.3: Poonen's density "should equal ζ_X(m + 1)". Theorem 3.7: "Let m > 0 be a squarefree integer". (20): "∏_{1≤i<j≤g+1} t_i/t_j" and "∏_{α∈U′}". Proof of Proposition 4.8: "contained in U_1". Proof of Proposition 3.9 (b_n even): "{(b_n/2)e_1 − e_n, e_2, …}". (32): "Δ(f) ≤ H(f)^{2n−2−κ}". Lemma 6.7: "#G_{B_0}(F_p) = #O(b_0)(F_p) = 2p^{(n²+n)/2}(…)". (48): "∫_{s∈T_1}". (58): "s_n ≥ X^{1/2−δ}". Lemma 6.21: "δ < max(η, 1)/1355g⁶" and "b_{g+2,n+2}". §6.5: "O_ε(m^{2n−ε}) binary n-ic forms mod m²". Corollary 6.27: "For a positive integer N ≥ 3". Proof of Theorem 5.1: "L_1 ≤ ⋯ ≤ L_n".`; it should be `"ζ_X(m + 1)^{−1}"; "an odd squarefree integer"; "∏_{1≤i<j≤g} t_i/t_j" and a product over U_0 ∩ U′; "U′"; "(b_n/2)e_1 − e_{n+1}"; "|Δ(f)| ≤ H(f)^{2n−2−κ}"; "#G_{B_0}(F_p) = p^n·#O(b_0)(F_p)"; "∫_{s∈T_2}"; "Y^{1/2−δ}"; "min(η, 1)" and "b_{g+2,n+1}"; "O_ε(m^{2n+ε})"; "n ≥ 3"; "L_1 ≤ ⋯ ≤ L_r".`. Each correction is forced by the surrounding text:
- the intro's own next line has ζ_{P¹}(3)^{−1};
- the construction of σ_m needs 2 invertible mod m;
- for n = 3 the true product in (20) is empty while the printed one is t_1/t_2, and only the corrected form makes (19)·(20) omit exactly the pairs (i ≥ g + 1, j = n), as the text says (checked symbolically for g ≤ 3);
- with e_n the listed vectors do not span Z^{n+1};
- Lemma 6.1 counts |Δ| small;
- the stabilizer of B_0 contains the unipotent p^n, and the final count uses it;
- (48) integrates over T_2;
- Y is the scaling parameter;
- Lemma 6.21 assumes min;
- B is (n + 1) × (n + 1);
- the resulting bound X^{n+1}/m^{2−ε} needs m^{2n+ε};
- n is the degree;
- L has r entries. Recorded as `PAPER-BHARGAVA-SHANKAR-WANG-25/E10`; looked for an existing correction in: Forum Math. - **Gap** at Proof of Theorem 7.1, pp.51–52; proof of Theorem 3, p.53. The source says `"W_m(Σ) denote the set of elements f ∈ V(Z) such that m² | Δ(f), and such that the reduction of f modulo p² belongs to Σ_p for every p | N. Note that W_m(Σ) is a union of γ(Σ, N, m) := N^{2n+2}m^{2n+2}∏_{p|m}(#Σ_p/p^{2n+2} − α_n(Σ, p)) translates of m²N²V(Z)." Proof of Theorem 3: "applying Theorem 7.1, we see that ≫ X^{(n+1)/(2n−2)} integral elements in B_X have discriminant less than X".`; it should be `Inclusion–exclusion over m coprime to N imposes nothing at p | N. So W_m(Σ) must also require p² ∤ Δ(f) (resp. R_f maximal at p) for p | N, or Σ_p must be assumed to lie in that set. Then W_m(Σ) consists of N^{2n+2}∏_{p|N}α_n(Σ, p)·m^{2n+2}∏_{p|m}(1 − α_n(p)) classes modulo N²m², which gives the main term α_n(Σ)(2X)^{n+1}. Theorem 3 needs the same sieve over the scaled region B_X instead of the height box. The proof carries over, with Theorem 5 bounding the tails in the enclosing box and Proposition 4.1 the main term.`. The printed γ lacks the factor ∏_{p|N}#Σ_p/p^{2n+2}, and its main term Σ_mμ(m)∏_{p|m}(1 − α_n(p)) equals ∏_{p∤N}α_n(p), not α_n(Σ). Theorem 7.1 is stated only for {H(f) < X}. Theorem 6 (N = 1) is unaffected. Recorded as `PAPER-BHARGAVA-SHANKAR-WANG-25/E11`; looked for an existing correction in: Forum Math. - **Error** at Proposition 3.6 and proof, pp.11–12 (it affects the proof). The source says `"Proposition 3.6. We have det(A^top(A^top)^t) det(B^top(B^top)^t) | Δ as elements in Z[W_0]." Proof: "Suppose (A, B) ∈ W_0(C) with det(B^top(B^top)^t) = 0. Then B^top does not have full rank."`; it should be `Over C, det(MM^t) = 0 does not imply rank M < g (for M = (1, i), MM^t = 0), so the Nullstellensatz argument fails and the statement is false. What the argument does prove: Δ vanishes on the complex locus where B^top (or A^top) has rank < g. So Δ lies in the ideal generated by the g × g minors of B^top (a prime determinantal ideal), and likewise for A^top. On bounded sets this gives |Δ| ≪ Σ|minors| ≪ det(B^top(B^top)^t)^{1/2}, by Cauchy–Binet, and that suffices for Lemma 6.25 with the exponent doubled.`. Counterexample for n = 3 (g = 1): A = [[0, 0, 1], [0, 0, 0], [1, 0, 1]] and B = [[0, 1, 1], [1, 1, 0], [1, 0, 0]] lie in W_0(Z). Then f_{A,B} = −y(x² − 3xy + y²) and Δ = 5, while det(A^top(A^top)^t)det(B^top(B^top)^t) = 1·2 = 2 ∤ 5. At random integral points the claimed divisibility held in only 6 of 37 cases for n = 3 and 0 of 40 for n = 5. Δ vanished at all 12 random points with rank-1 B^top for n = 5. - **Error** at Lemma 6.25 and proof, p.44; its use in Proposition 6.26, pp.45–47, and Theorem 6.24 (it affects the proof). The source says `"Let (A, B) ∈ (YD × YD) ∩ W_0(R) be such that Δ(A, B) > X^{2n−2−κ} … Then det(B^top(B^top)^t) ≫ Y^{2(g+1)−(n+1)κ}. Proof. Let (A′, B′) = Y^{−1}(A, B) … Since |Δ(A, B)| > X^{2n−2−κ}, we have |Δ(A′, B′)| > X^{−κ}." In Proposition 6.26: "|Δ(A_0, B_0)| = |Δ(A, B)| > X^{2n−2−κ} since (A, B) ∈ L(1)."`; it should be `Here W = W_{n+1}, so f_{A,B} is an (n + 1)-ic form and Δ(A, B) is homogeneous of degree 2n(n + 1). Then |Δ(A′, B′)| = X^{−2n}|Δ(A, B)| > X^{−2−κ} only, so the printed conclusion does not follow. Repair: - discard, besides V^{Δ small}, the forms with |f(0, 1)| < H(f)^{1−κ/(2n−2)} (O(X^{n+1−κ/(2n−2)}) of them), and work in dyadic height ranges; - then for (A, B) ∈ L(1), Δ(A, B) = Δ(xf) = Δ(f)f(0, 1)² ≫ X^{2n−κ″} with κ″ = κn/(n − 1); - with the corrected Proposition 3.6 (E2), det(B_0^top(B_0^top)^t) ≫ Y^{2(g+1)}X^{−2κ″}; - so |q| ≪ Y^{(g+1)²}X^{κ″}∏_{i≤g+1}L_i in (66), and Theorem 6.24 holds with X^{n+1+κ″} in place of X^{n+1+κ/2}. In §6.5 (κ = (2n − 2)/(88n⁶)) this changes ξ_n in Theorem 5(c) and Corollary 6.27 from 1/(88n⁵) to 1/(44n⁵). The saving η_n = 1/(88n⁶) and Theorems 1–4 and 6 are unchanged.`. For (A, B) ∈ L(1), f_{A,B} = xf and Δ(A, B) = Δ(f)f(0, 1)² (§3.5). Only |Δ(f)| > H(f)^{2n−2−κ} is known, and f(0, 1) can be ±1, so |Δ(A, B)| can be as small as X^{2n−2−κ}. The scaling step treats Δ as if it had degree (2n − 2)(n + 1), the degree of Δ(f) for the n-ic f. Example: n = 4, W_5; the binary quintic discriminant has degree 8 in coefficients of degree 5, so Δ(tA, tB) = t^{40}Δ(A, B) = X^8 for t = Y. The printed conclusion needs |Δ(A, B)| > X^{2n−κ}. - **Error** at Theorem 6.6 and its proof, pp.29–33 (it affects the proof). The source says `"Theorem 6.6. We have I^main_X(L(1)) = O(X^{n+1−1/(4n)+ε})." Proof: "Combining (47) and (48) and choosing δ = (n + 3/2)/(n² + n + 1) yields N(L(1), L, Z, X) ≪_ε X^{n+1−(n−2)/(2n²+2n+2)+ε}."`; it should be `The proof gives I^main_X(L(1)) = O(X^{n+1−θ_n+ε}) with θ_n = min{1/(4n), (n − 2)/(2n² + 2n + 2)}, which is 1/(4n) for n ≥ 6 but 2/42 = 1/21 < 1/16 for n = 4. §6.5 needs only a saving above 1/(88n⁶), so Theorem 5(c) is unaffected.`. Proposition 6.9 gives 1/(4n), but the Z ⊂ M part gives (n − 2)/(2n² + 2n + 2). For n = 4 the latter is 1/21 (δ = 11/42, balancing (47) and (48) at Y^{−5/21}). So the stated exponent is not reached for n = 4, and nothing else in the proof improves it. Recorded as `PAPER-BHARGAVA-SHANKAR-WANG-25/E4`; looked for an existing correction in: Forum Math. - **Error** at Lemma 7.2 and proof, pp.52–53; proof of Theorem 3, p.53. The source says `"Lemma 7.2 … Suppose the corresponding rank-n rings R_f and R_{f*} are isomorphic. Then f(x, y) and f*(x, y) are SL_2(Z)-equivalent. Proof. … By the uniqueness of Minkowski-reduced bases, φ must map the basis elements 1, ζ_1, …, ζ_{n−1} for R_f to the corresponding basis elements 1, ζ*_1, …, ζ*_{n−1}."`; it should be `Minkowski-reduced bases are unique at best up to the signs of their vectors, so φ(ζ_i) = ±ζ*_i. The conclusion is only that f* is GL_2(Z)-equivalent to f, e.g. f*(x, y) = f(−x, y). In the proof of Theorem 3, choose B so that no two of its elements are GL_2(Z)-equivalent (GL_2(Z) also acts discretely). The bound is unchanged.`. Counterexample for n = 3: f = x³ − x²y + xy² + y³, an S_3 cubic (disc −44), is strongly Minkowski-reduced. An exhaustive search gives the reduced basis 1, ζ_1, ζ_2 with unique minima up to sign. f*(x, y) = f(−x, y) = −x³ − x²y − xy² + y³ has the same lattice R_{f*} = R_f, since ζ*_i = ±ζ_i, so it is strongly Minkowski-reduced too. But f* is not SL_2(Z)-equivalent to f. Otherwise f would have a stabilizer element δ of determinant −1, and −δ would be a nontrivial automorphism of R_f in the twisted GL_2(Z)-action, which is impossible for an order in a non-Galois cubic field. Part I's Lemma 5.1 has the same sign gap (its E10). Recorded as `PAPER-BHARGAVA-SHANKAR-WANG-25/E5`; looked for an existing correction in: Forum Math. Math. 2022) was compared where Part II relies on it.
- **Gap** at §3.4, p.12; §6, p.25 (W^{(2)}_m for odd m only); Theorem 5(b)–(c), pp.3–4. The source says `"For an odd squarefree integer m > 0, let W^{(2)}_m = W^{(2)}_{m,n} denote the set of integer n-ic binary forms whose discriminants are weakly divisible by p² for every prime factor p of m." Theorem 5 takes the union over all squarefree m > M.`; it should be `Add that W^{(2)}_m = ∅ for even m: the discriminant of an integral binary form is ≡ 0 or 1 mod 4 (Stickelberger), so 2 | Δ(f + 2g) forces 4 | Δ(f + 2g), and divisibility by 4 is never weak. The unions in Theorem 5(b)–(c) are then over odd m, as the proofs assume.`. The constructions σ_m (choice of r with m | 2rc_0 + b_1, the even-n set W^gen_m) need m odd, and the paper never says why even m can be dropped. The fact is used, without comment, only in subset 3 of the proof of Proposition A.1. Checked: Δ mod 4 ∈ {0, 1} for all binary forms mod 4 of degree n ≤ 5. - **Gap** at §6, (31) and the sentence after (35), p.25. The source says `"V(Z)^red := {f ∈ V(Z) : Gal(f(x, 1)) ≠ S_n}" and "The number of elements in V(Z)^red having height less than X was bounded by O(X^n) in [22, Theorem 1]."`; it should be `Kuba [22] counts reducible forms only. The irreducible forms with Galois group ≠ S_n need a separate power-saving bound, e.g. by the large sieve as in Gallagher's argument (O_ε(X^{n+1/2+ε})) or by Bhargava's work on van der Waerden's conjecture [6], which the bibliography lists but the text never cites. Any saving larger than 1/(88n⁶) suffices for §6.5.`. The S_n hypothesis is used in Lemma 6.23 (no factorization of g(x, 1) over quadratic fields) and in the definition of W^gen_m. So the count of V(Z)^red must include irreducible non-S_n forms, which [22] does not cover. Recorded as `PAPER-BHARGAVA-SHANKAR-WANG-25/E7`; looked for an existing correction in: Forum Math. - **Gap** at Theorems 1, 2 and 6 for n = 2; Theorem 5 and Corollary 6.27 (n ≥ 3). The source says `"For n = 2, classical methods in sieve theory yield the answer." Theorems 1 and 2 are stated for n ≥ 2; Theorem 5 and Corollary 6.27 for n ≥ 3.`; it should be `Add the n = 2 tail estimate. For N ≠ 0, #{(a_0, a_1, a_2) ∈ [−X, X]³ : a_1² − 4a_0a_2 = N} ≪_ε X^{1+ε}, since for each a_1 with a_1² ≠ N the product a_0a_2 = (a_1² − N)/4 has ≪ X^ε factorizations, and at most two a_1 have a_1² = N. Summing over N = m²k with m > M and 0 < |k| ≪ X²/m² gives O_ε(X^{3+ε}/M), which is what Theorem 7.1 needs.`. Theorem 7.1's proof uses Corollary 6.27, which is proved only for n ≥ 3, so the n = 2 cases of Theorems 1, 2 and 6 are asserted by reference to "classical methods" without proof. - **Gap** at §3.4, (8), p.12. The source says `"Then just as shown in [11, §3.2], there exists an SL_2(Z)-change of variable such that f((x, y)γ) has the form m²b_0x^n + mb_1x^{n−1}y + ⋯ + b_ny^n for some integers b_0, …, b_n and where m and b_0 are coprime."`; it should be `Part I (for monic polynomials) gives the divisibility but not the coprimality (m, b_0) = 1, which the choice of r needs. Repair, for each odd p | m: - move the double root to (1 : 0), so p | a_0, a_1 and p ∤ a_2, and p² | a_0 by the Appendix congruence; - apply (x, y) ↦ (x, pt·x + y), which changes a_0/p² mod p by (a_1/p)t + a_2t² with a_2 ≢ 0; - this quadratic has at most two roots mod p ≥ 3, so some t gives p³ ∤ a_0; - combine the primes by CRT.`. Coprimality is used to solve m | 2rc_0 + b_1 with c_0 = b_0, and it is not automatic: a weakly divisible f can have p³ | a_0, e.g. a_0 = 0. ### Bhatt et al., "Globally +-regular varieties and the minimal model program for threefolds in mixed characteristic", Publications Mathématiques de l'IHÉS (2023) (`PAPER-BHATT-ETAL-23`)

- **Error** at Lemma 2.56, second assertion, p.101 of the published version; the same wording is arXiv v1 Lemma 2.37 (p.20), v2 Lemma 2.50 (p.26) and v3 Lemma 2.56 (p.29) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"More generally, let D be a fixed divisor and let A be a semiample Q-Cartier Q-divisor such that A ∼_Q M + Λ, where M is an effective Cartier divisor with no exceptional curve of Y → X in its support, and −δD ≤ Λ ≤ δD for δ > 0. Take x ∈ F ∩ M of multiplicity k ∈ Z_{>0} in M. Then ε_sa(A; x) converges to k when δ → 0."`; it should be `Only a lower bound holds. For every rational γ with 0 < γ < k there is δ₀ > 0, depending only on f, D and γ, such that ε_sa(A; x) = ε(A; x) ≥ k − γ for all δ < δ₀ and all such A, M, Λ and x. Equivalently, liminf_{δ→0} ε_sa(A; x) ≥ k, uniformly. This is exactly what the printed proof establishes; there is no upper bound.`. Counterexample with Λ = 0. Let k be a field of characteristic p > 0, X = A²_k, and f: Y → X the blow-up of the origin with exceptional curve E = F. Let M be the sum of the strict transforms of the two axes. Then M ∼ −2E, so M is effective, Cartier, globally generated over X (O_Y(−E) = m·O_Y) and has no exceptional component. Let x = E ∩ (strict transform of the y-axis), so mult_x M = 1 = k. Blow up x, with exceptional curve G and strict transform E′. Then E′² = −2, G² = −1, E′·G = 1 and π*M − tG ∼ −2E′ − (2+t)G, which has degree 2 − t on E′ and t on G. Hence ε(M; x) = 2. At t = 2 the ideal (x, y²) pulls back to O(−E′ − 2G) (charts x = ab, y = b, then a = c, b = cd, giving c²d, and a = ef, b = f, giving f²). So π*M − 2G ∼ −2E′ − 4G is globally generated and ε_sa(M; x) = 2. Taking A = M and Λ = 0 (any D), ε_sa(A; x) = 2 for every δ, not converging to k = 1. The proof only shows that π*A − (k − γ)E is nef. The consumers need only the lower bound: Proposition 8.15 (p.173) uses the first assertion ε_sa(M; x) ≥ 1, which is correct, and Claim 8.21 (p.178) needs ε_sa > 2 for k = 3. So the paper's results are unaffected. Checked on the page image and by exact arithmetic. Noted by the extraction (diagnostic A, gap-seshadri-limit); verified and proved in the corrected form by this continuation. Recorded as `PAPER-BHATT-ETAL-23/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Crossref metadata for 10.1007/s10240-023-00140-8: no update, correction or erratum relation, and no work that updates this DOI (queried 2026-09-22); Springer article page and Publ. - **Misprint** at Proposition 6.28, p.154 of the published version (and arXiv v3 Proposition 6.28, p.71). The source says `"Let Δ ≥ 0 be a Q-divisor on X such that (X, Δ) is globally +-regular (completely purely globally +-regular over R, resp.). Suppose that Δ_Y ≥ 0, where K_Y + Δ_Y = f*(K_X + Δ). Then (Y, Δ_Y) is globally +-regular (globally +-regular, resp.)."`; it should be `The conclusion in the resp. case is "completely purely globally +-regular over R", with the pure boundary S_Y the strict transform of S and B_Y = Δ_Y − S_Y. This is what arXiv v2 (Proposition 6.28, p.64) prints, and arXiv v1 (Proposition 6.22, p.48) prints "purely ... purely".`. The printed resp. conclusion is false. Take R = k[[t]] with k of characteristic p, X = Y = Spec R, f = id and Δ = S = V(t). Then (X, S) is purely globally +-regular. For a finite normal R′ ⊇ R with uniformizer s and ramification e, the map R → s^{1−e}R′ splits because R′ is R-free and 1 ∉ t·s^{1−e}R′ = sR′. But (X, S) is not globally +-regular: already for the identity cover, a splitting of O_X → O_X(S) = t^{−1}R would send 1 = t·t^{−1} into tR. The cited proof (Proposition 6.26, which applies because R is complete, together with Lemma 4.27 with B′ = B_Y) proves the pure conclusion. The consumer in Theorem 7.14 (p.165) uses the pure conclusion. In arXiv v3 the qualifier is visibly garbled as "( ly globally +-regular, resp.)", so the published wording is a typesetting slip. Checked on the page images of v3 p.71 and published p.154. Noted by the extraction (diagnostic B, gap-pure-birational); the version history was established by this continuation. Recorded as `PAPER-BHATT-ETAL-23/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: Crossref metadata for 10.1007/s10240-023-00140-8: no update, correction or erratum relation, and no work that updates this DOI (queried 2026-09-22); Springer article page and Publ. - **Misprint** at Proof of Lemma 4.28, p.128 of the published version (also arXiv v2 and v3). The source says `"Twisting by the pullback L⁺ to W⁺ of the line bundle O_Y(f*(M − (K_X + S + B))) (for some finite cover f: Y → W), and taking local cohomology, we obtain: ... The left zeros are due to Corollary 3.7 and the fact that L⁺ is the pullback of a big and semiample line bundle."`; it should be `Twist by the pullback of O_Y(f*(K_X + S + B − M)), the inverse of the big and semiample bundle f*(M − (K_X + S + B)). Corollary 3.7 then applies with b = −1 and kills H^{d−1}_m of it.`. Corollary 3.7 (p.107) gives H^i(RΓ_x RΓ(X⁺, L^b)) = 0 only for b < 0 and i < dim X, where L is big and semiample, so it gives nothing for the positive twist that is printed. With the inverse twist, H^d_m of O(−S_{W⁺}) twisted by f*(K_X + S + B − M) is dual to H⁰(O_Y(K_Y + S_Y + f*(M − K_X − S − B))), which is the expression in the statement of the lemma. The same line bundle L := O_{X⁺}(π*(K_X + S + B − M)) appears in the proof of Theorem 7.2 (p.156). The lemma is marked "a fact we will not use". Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction (diagnostic C, gap-adjoint-sign). - **Misprint** at Theorem 10.6, definition of β(1), p.216 of the published version (also arXiv v3); compare Theorem K, p.76, and the proof, pp.218–219. The source says `"β(v) = 393 if v = 1, 213v + 48 if v ≥ 2"`; it should be `β(1) = 373, as in Theorem K ("f(v) = 373 if v = 1"). The printed 393 gives a weaker statement, which the corrected one implies.`. The proof uses β(v) only through conditions (b), p ≥ d + 4, and (c), p > deg D, for primes p > β(v). For v = 1 it computes the fibre canonical degree (K + 10K)·10K = 110 ≤ 373 − 4. It computes the discriminant degree 3L² + 2L·K + c₂ with L = 10K as 319K² + 12χ ≤ 325K² + 48 = 373, using c₂ = 12χ − K² and the Noether bound χ ≤ (K² + 6)/2 + 1. - **Error** at Theorem 2.13 and Proposition 2.14, p.85 of the published version. The source says `"... and let T be a subscheme of X. Then there exists a proper birational morphism g: Y → X from a regular scheme Y such that both g^{−1}(T) and Ex(g) are divisors and Supp(g^{−1}(T) ∪ Ex(g)) is simple normal crossing."`; it should be `T must be a closed subscheme containing no irreducible component of X, that is, nowhere dense.`. For T = X, or for T containing a component of X, g^{−1}(T) contains a component of Y and is not a divisor. Every application in the paper uses a nowhere-dense T: log resolutions of pairs (T the support of the boundary), and inside the proof of Proposition 2.14 the union of the nowhere-dense blow-up centre Z with g′^{−1}(T) ∪ Ex(g′). - **Error** at Definition 2.19, p.88 of the published version. The source says `"coeff_E Fix(D) = min_{D′ ∈ |D|} coeff_E D′. Note that as D is Cartier the above coefficients are integers and hence the minimum exists."`; it should be `The minimum exists only when |D| ≠ ∅, so the definition needs that hypothesis.`. For D = O(−1) on P¹, |D| = ∅ and the minimum is over the empty set. Every use of Mob and Fix is in §8, on schemes Y and S′ that are projective and birational over the affine base Z = Spec R or over f(S). There H⁰(O(D)) = H⁰(g_*O(D)) ≠ 0 for every Cartier D, since g_*O(D) is a nonzero torsion-free coherent sheaf on an affine integral scheme. In addition, Setting 8.5 chooses K_X + S + B effective. Noted by the extraction (gap-empty-system). ### Bhatt–Mathew, "The arc-topology", Duke Mathematical Journal (2021) (`PAPER-BHATT-MATHEW-21`)

- **Misprint** at Remark 3.31 (arXiv v4, the version read). The source says `Proposition 3.29 and Lemma 3.27 imply that for every commutative ring A, there is a v-cover A → B such that each connected component of B is a valuation ring.`; it should be `Lemma 3.29 and Lemma 3.27 imply …`. The statement numbered 3.29 is 'Lemma 3.29' (the cardinality bound for absolutely integrally closed valuation rings, labelled boundcardaic in the source); the paper has no Proposition 3.29. The reference is generated by \ref{boundcardaic} with the environment name typed by hand. - **Misprint** at Proof of Corollary 6.18 (GAGA for rigid étale cohomology in the proper case), last sentence (arXiv v4, the version read). The source says `But this follows from formal glueing for étale cohomology (Proposition 6.4).`; it should be `(Theorem 6.4).`. The statement numbered 6.4 is 'Theorem 6.4' (formal glueing squares for arc-sheaves, labelled arcarithm); the paper has no Proposition 6.4. J. 170 (2021), no. 9): the DOI resolves to the Project Euclid landing page, which served no article text to an automated request on 22 September 2026, so the published text was not compared and these findings are recorded against arXiv v4; the second author's homepage at math.uchicago.edu/~amathew (the link given in the job), read 22 September 2026: the paper is listed with no erratum.
- **Gap** at Theorem 1.19 of the introduction (= Theorem 7.3), and Remark 7.4(2) (arXiv v4, the version read) (it affects a stated result). The source says `Fix a complete and algebraically closed nonarchimedean field K. Let A be a classical affinoid K-algebra of dimension d. Fix a prime ℓ and let F be an ℓ-power torsion étale sheaf on Spec(A). If A is smooth or if ℓ is not the residue characteristic of K, then H^i(Spec(A)_ét, F) = 0 for i > d.`; it should be `The disjunct 'if A is smooth' is not proved in the paper at this generality. Theorem 7.3, which Theorem 1.19 is labelled as, proves the vanishing above degree d only for ℓ prime to the residue characteristic; the smooth case is claimed only in Remark 7.4(2), only for constant coefficients, and only as an adaptation: 'The algebraization method used to prove Lemma 7.8 below can be adapted to prove this statement when A is smooth (in the sense of rigid spaces) and F is constant, thanks to [Elkik, Theorem 7 and Remark 2 on page 587].' Either the introduction should restrict the smooth case to constant coefficients and point at Remark 7.4(2), or the adaptation should be written out for arbitrary ℓ-power torsion sheaves.`. Theorem 7.3 as printed has two parts: (1) H^i = 0 for i > d + 1 for every torsion sheaf, and (2) H^i = 0 for i > d when F is ℓ-power torsion with ℓ prime to the residue characteristic. Neither mentions smoothness of A, and Remark 7.4 opens 'We would expect that part (2) of Theorem 7.3 holds for arbitrary torsion sheaves', listing the smooth constant-coefficient case as one of two special cases that are 'within reach'. No proof of the smooth case for non-constant ℓ-power torsion sheaves appears in Section 7, and the reduction of Theorem 7.3 to constant coefficients runs through Proposition 7.9 for the two cases actually stated there, not for the smooth case. ### Binyamini, "Point counting for foliations over number fields", Forum of Mathematics, Pi (2022) (`PAPER-BINYAMINI-22`)

- **Misprint** at Lemma 15 and proof, (34)–(35), p.11 (published version). The source says `"S(X ∩ B², ε) ≤ (2^m/c(m)) vol(X)·ε^{−2m}" … "vol(X ∩ B_p) ≥ c(n)(ε/2)^{2m}"`; it should be `S(X ∩ B², ε) ≤ (2^{2m}/c(m)) vol(X) ε^{−2m}, from vol(X ∩ B_p) ≥ c(m)(ε/2)^{2m}.`. X has dimension m, so the Lelong bound involves c(m), the volume of the unit ball of ℂ^m. Dividing vol(X) by c(m)(ε/2)^{2m} gives the factor 2^{2m}. Only O_n-constants change. Recorded as `PAPER-BINYAMINI-22/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2009.00892 (v1, 2 September 2020, the only version); Forum of Mathematics, Pi 10 (2022) e6 article page (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1017/fmp.2021.20: no update or correction relation.
- **Misprint** at §4.4, p.14 and proof of Proposition 22, p.15 (published version). The source says `"by a dimension-counting argument using Lemma 19, the codimension of π_Φ(Σ_Ṽ) is positive" … "Set Z = Clo π(Σ_{W̃_{k+1}}) … Z has positive codimension"`; it should be `Restrict to the part of Σ_Ṽ lying over M ∖ Σ_V (resp. over the p with dim(V ∩ L_p) < n − k): Z := Clo π_Φ(Σ_Ṽ ∖ π_M^{-1}(Σ_V)).`. Over points p ∈ Σ_V every Φ lies in Σ_Ṽ, so π_Φ(Σ_Ṽ) is all of M_D whenever Σ_V ≠ ∅. Lemma 19 and the target condition (44) concern only p ∉ Σ_V. Recorded as `PAPER-BINYAMINI-22/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2009.00892 (v1, 2 September 2020, the only version); Forum of Mathematics, Pi 10 (2022) e6 article page (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1017/fmp.2021.20: no update or correction relation.
- **Misprint** at Proof of Lemma 40, p.30, and §9.3, p.31 (published version). The source says `"This follows from δ(V^ws) = poly_n(δ_W) and equation (121)" … "Then according to Corollary 39, none of the positive-dimensional blocks …"`; it should be `δ(V^ws) = poly_n(δ_V); "according to Proposition 39".`. There is no W in Lemma 40, and §9.2 gives δ(V^ws) = poly_n(δ_V). The cited statement is Proposition 39. Recorded as `PAPER-BINYAMINI-22/E3`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2009.00892 (v1, 2 September 2020, the only version); Forum of Mathematics, Pi 10 (2022) e6 article page (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1017/fmp.2021.20: no update or correction relation.
- **Misprint** at §8.6, (108), p.27 (published version). The source says `"#A(1, h) = poly(δ_C, R̂, h)."`; it should be `#A(1, h) = poly(δ_C, log R̂, h).`. Theorem 3 depends on log R. Lemma 38 then takes R̂ = e^{poly(δ_C)}, and only log R̂ = poly(δ_C) gives the polynomial bound used in (112). Recorded as `PAPER-BINYAMINI-22/E4`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2009.00892 (v1, 2 September 2020, the only version); Forum of Mathematics, Pi 10 (2022) e6 article page (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1017/fmp.2021.20: no update or correction relation.
- **Misprint** at §8.6, p.27, and §10.3, p.34 (published version). The source says `"Setting A := ℝ² ∩ Φ(B̂² ∩ V)" (twice)`; it should be `A := Mat₂ₓ₂(ℝ) ∩ Φ(B̂² ∩ V) in §8.6 (Φ = U ∈ Mat₂ₓ₂), and A := (ℂ² × ℝ²) ∩ Φ(B̂² ∩ V) in §10.3 (Φ = (x, y, u₁, u₂) with real (u₁, u₂)).`. In both places Φ has four complex coordinates. The realness condition applies to the four entries of U, resp. to (u₁, u₂) only. Recorded as `PAPER-BINYAMINI-22/E5`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2009.00892 (v1, 2 September 2020, the only version); Forum of Mathematics, Pi 10 (2022) e6 article page (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1017/fmp.2021.20: no update or correction relation.
- **Misprint** at Theorem 8, p.21 (published version). The source says `"Let E be an elliptic curve defined over a number field K, and p ∈ A a torsion point of order n. Then n = poly_g([K:ℚ], h_Fal(E), [K(p):K])."`; it should be `p ∈ E(K̄); n = poly([K:ℚ], h_Fal(E), [K(p):K]).`. A and g belong to the abelian-variety version (143) of §10.4; for an elliptic curve g = 1. Recorded as `PAPER-BINYAMINI-22/E6`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2009.00892 (v1, 2 September 2020, the only version); Forum of Mathematics, Pi 10 (2022) e6 article page (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1017/fmp.2021.20: no update or correction relation.
- **Misprint** at Definition 49, (153), p.36 (published version). The source says `"∠L := max_{i=1,…,n} ‖a_j(t)‖/‖a₀(t)‖"`; it should be `∠L := max_{j=1,…,n} ‖a_j(t)‖/‖a₀(t)‖.`. The maximum index is j. Recorded as `PAPER-BINYAMINI-22/E7`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2009.00892 (v1, 2 September 2020, the only version); Forum of Mathematics, Pi 10 (2022) e6 article page (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1017/fmp.2021.20: no update or correction relation.
- **Error** at Proposition 51 and Theorem 9, Appendix A, pp.36–37 (published version) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Proposition 51. Suppose L is defined over a number field. Then inf_{φ ∈ Aut(ℙ¹)} ‖a₀(φ*L)‖ = e^{−poly_n(δ_L)}. Proof. … Now the operator L′ := L + 1 is also Fuchsian …"`; it should be `Assume in addition that L + 1 is Fuchsian. This holds when every finite zero of a₀ has order ≤ n and deg a₀ ≥ n, and in particular for the Legendre Picard–Fuchs operator (87), a₀ = λ(1−λ), n = 2, which is the only operator to which the paper applies Theorem 9. Under this normalization the proof of Proposition 51 is correct, and Theorem 9 follows as written.`. For a general Fuchsian operator, L + 1 need not be Fuchsian, and both statements fail. Take L = ∂_t, which is Fuchsian: its only singular point ∞ is regular singular (∂_t = −z(z∂_z), z = 1/t). Then φ(s) = κs gives φ*L = κ^{-1}∂_s and inf_φ ‖a₀(φ*L)‖ = 0. In Theorem 9 take b(t) = (t − t₀)/(2r) (|b| ≤ 1 on D^{1/2}) and f = (t − t₀)²/(4r), so j_z f(t₀) = 0 while |∂_z f(t₀ + r/2)| = r/4, unbounded in r. Here L + 1 = ∂ + 1 has an irregular singularity at ∞. Theorems 1–8 are unaffected. Recorded as `PAPER-BINYAMINI-22/E8`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2009.00892 (v1, 2 September 2020, the only version); Forum of Mathematics, Pi 10 (2022) e6 article page (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1017/fmp.2021.20: no update or correction relation.

### Boxer–Calegari–Gee, "Cuspidal cohomology classes for GL_n(Z)", Journal of the American Mathematical Society (2025) (`PAPER-BOXER-CALEGARI-GEE-25`)

- **Misprint** at Proof of Theorem 2.1, pp. 514–515 (published offprint; identical in arXiv v1–v3). The source says `"(see [BLGGT14, Thm. 2.4.2] for a restatement in the precise form we use here; in the notation of that statement, we are taking l = p, n = p − 1, S = {p}, μ = ε^{1−n}, H_τ = {0, 1, . . . , n − 1})" and "(taking F = Q, l = p, n = p − 1, r = ρ, and μ = ε^{1−n}δ^n_{F/F^+})"`; it should be `In both parameter lists read n = n, that is n = p − 1 or n = p − 2 as in the statement of Theorem 2.1.`. Theorem 2.1 and its proof treat n = p − 1 and n = p − 2 together ('Let n = p − 1 or p − 2'). With n = p − 1 the multiplier and weights would be those of the symplectic case only, and the orthogonal case n = p − 2, which gives n = 105 in Theorem 2.4, would not be covered. In arXiv v1 the proof had the same wording. Recorded as `PAPER-BOXER-CALEGARI-GEE-25/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2309.15944 v1 (27 Sep 2023), v2 (18 Oct 2023) and v3 (12 Sep 2024); the published offprint, J. Amer. Math. Soc. 38 (2025) 509–520 (Calegari's research page); G. Boxer's homepage copy WeightZero.pdf (17 Oct 2024); web search for an erratum or corrigendum (none found).
- **Misprint** at Proof of Theorem 2.1, p. 514. The source says `"Applying [Tho12, Thm. 10.1], we see that R_F is a finite O-algebra (see [BLGGT14, Thm. 2.4.2] for a restatement in the precise form we use here; ...)"`; it should be `[Tho12, Thm. 10.2]`. In [Tho12] §10, Theorem 10.1 is 'A minimal finiteness theorem' (crystalline components fixed at v | l) and Theorem 10.2 is 'An ordinary finiteness theorem' (ss-ordinary lifts at v | l). [BLGGT14] introduces its Theorem 2.4.2 as 'the next result is Theorem 10.2 of [Tho12]', and R_F is an ordinary deformation problem. The numbering is the same in Thorne's homepage copy and in arXiv 1107.5989v1. The citation of Thm 10.1 in the proof of Theorem 3.1, a crystalline problem with fixed components, is correct. Recorded as `PAPER-BOXER-CALEGARI-GEE-25/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2309.15944 v1 (27 Sep 2023), v2 (18 Oct 2023) and v3 (12 Sep 2024); the published offprint, J. Amer. Math. Soc. 38 (2025) 509–520 (Calegari's research page); G. Boxer's homepage copy WeightZero.pdf (17 Oct 2024); web search for an erratum or corrigendum (none found); J. Thorne, 'On the automorphy of l-adic Galois representations with small residual image', homepage copy and arXiv 1107.5989v1, §10.
- **Misprint** at Proof of Theorem 3.1, pp. 517–518. The source says `"By [Tho17, Thm. 7.1], Ind^{G_F}_{G_M}(θ ⊗ ρ|_{G_F}) is automorphic" and "It remains to show that we can apply [Tho17, Thm. 7.1, Prop. 7.2]."`; it should be `[Tho17, Thm. 5.1] (equivalently [Tho12, Thm. 7.1] in the form of [Tho17, Cor. 7.3]), together with [Tho17, Prop. 7.2].`. Section 7 of [Tho17] ('An erratum to [Tho12]') consists of Proposition 7.1 (Taylor–Wiles primes), Proposition 7.2 and Corollary 7.3; there is no Theorem 7.1, and Proposition 7.1 is not an automorphy statement. The automorphy lifting theorem with adequacy in the sense of Defn 2.20, which allows p | n as here (n = (k−1)p), is Theorem 5.1; Corollary 7.3 gives [Tho12, Thm 7.1] under that adequacy for odd l. [GHT17, §1] cites the same paper as '[60, Corollary 7.3]' and '[60, Theorem 5.1]' for these results. Prop. 7.2 and Prop. 2.21, cited nearby, match the author's copy. - **Misprint** at Proof of Theorem 3.1, p. 517 (published; identical in arXiv v2 and v3). The source says `"such that the representation s̄ := Ind^{G_F}_{G_M}(θ̄ ⊗ ρ̄|_{G_F}) is absolutely irreducible"; "We have a finite map R_F → R, taking a lifting ρ of ρ̄ to Ind^{G_F}_{G_M}(θ ⊗ ρ|_{G_F})."; "By [Tho17, Thm. 7.1], Ind^{G_F}_{G_M}(θ ⊗ ρ|_{G_F}) is automorphic"`; it should be `Ind^{G_F}_{G_M}(θ′ ⊗ ρ|_{G_M}) in the last two places, and ρ̄|_{G_M} in the definition of s̄ (where θ̄ = θ̄′, so s̄ itself is unchanged).`. R_F parametrizes deformations with multiplier ε^{1−(k−1)p}δ_{F/F^+} and regular Hodge–Tate weights 0, …, (k−1)p − 1 lying on the component of (ρ_{k−1,p} ⊗ ρ_{p,1})|G_{F_v}, and the paper chooses (Ind θ′)|G_{F_v} ∼ ρ_{k−1,p}. With θ′ the induced representation has multiplier ε^{p(2−k)}ε^{1−p} = ε^{1−(k−1)p} and weights {pa + b} = {0, …, (k−1)p − 1}. With θ, as printed, it has multiplier ε^{2−k}ε^{1−p} = ε^{3−k−p} and repeated weights {a + b}; for p = 79, k = 38 the exponents are −114 against the required −2922. The characters θ, θ′ live on G_M, so the representation inside Ind_{G_M} must be restricted to G_M. The automorphic seed is Ind(θ ⊗ Sym^{p−1}ρ_f|G_M), as in the proof of [BLGGT14, Prop. 4.1.1], where R := r ⊗ Ind θ pairs with R′ := r_{l,ι}(π) ⊗ Ind θ′; the paper's naming of θ and θ′ is the reverse, and the two printed occurrences follow the BLGGT pattern. - **Gap** at Proof of Theorem 2.1, p. 514; proof of Theorem 3.1, p. 517 (it affects the proof). The source says `"The remaining hypotheses of [BG19, Prop. 4.2.6] hold because the multiplier character ε̄^{1−n} is odd/even precisely when G_n is symplectic/orthogonal, and the Hodge–Tate weights 0, 1, . . . , n − 1 are pairwise distinct." and "By [BG19, Prop. 4.2.6], every irreducible component of R has Krull dimension at least 1 (the verification that H^0(Q, so_p^*(1)) = 0 is exactly as in the proof of Theorem 2.1)."`; it should be `For G_n = GO_n (n = p − 2 in Theorem 2.1, n = p in Theorem 3.1) add that ρ̄(c) is, up to sign, Sym^{n−1} of an odd two-dimensional involution, so its eigenvalues ±1 have multiplicities (n+1)/2 and (n−1)/2 and dim so_n^{ρ̄(c)} = ((n−1)/2)^2 = dim GO_n − dim B, which is 'discrete series and odd' in the sense of [BG19, Def. 4.2.1].`. [BG19, Def. 4.2.1] requires dim H^0(Gal_{F_v}, g^0) = dim G − dim B at every real place. For GSp_n an odd multiplier implies this (the ±1-eigenspaces of ρ̄(c) are Lagrangian). For GO_n an even multiplier only says ρ̄(c) ∈ O_n: an involution with eigenvalue multiplicities (a, n − a) has dim so_n^{ρ̄(c)} = a(a−1)/2 + (n−a)(n−a−1)/2, which equals ((n−1)/2)^2 only for a = (n ± 1)/2 (ρ̄(c) = ±1 gives n(n−1)/2). The proof of Theorem 3.1 does not mention oddness. The needed count holds here: (53, 52) for n = 105 and (40, 39) for n = 79. [BLGGT14, Thm. 2.1.1(4)] gives compatibility at v | l only when π_v has an Iwahori-fixed vector; BLGGT say they quote Caraiani's l = p theorem only in that case. In Theorem 2.1 this does no harm, because [BLGGT14, Thm. 2.4.1] gives automorphy of level prime to l for crystalline r.
