- **Error** at Remark 3.3, p. 518 (published; the same list is in arXiv v2 and v3, and arXiv v1 §3.1 has it as 'companion forms also exist for p = 139, 151, 173, 179, . . . in weights k with (p − 1, k − 1) = 1') (it affects a stated result). The source says `"and modular forms satisfying the hypotheses of Theorem 2.1 for p = 107, 139, 151, 173, 179, . . .."`; it should be `p = 107, 139, 173, 179, 191, 193, . . . (remove 151).`. For an ordinary f the shape ρ̄_f|G_{Q_p} ≅ ψ̄ ⊕ ψ̄^{−1}ε̄^{1−k} determines k mod p − 1 up to replacing k by p + 1 − k (the companion), and gcd(p − 1, k − 1) = gcd(p − 1, p − k) is the same for both. At p = 151 the only ordinary level-one eigensystems with ρ̄_f|G_{Q_p} semisimple are the companion pair in weights 52 and 100 ([CG13] table: 151, C[52]). This was confirmed by matching the characteristic polynomials of T_2 and T_3 on S_52 and on S_100 (twisted by ℓ^{99}) mod 151; no other pair (k, 152 − k) with 12 ≤ k < 76 matches, and the self-pair k = 76 = (p+1)/2 has (150, 75) ≠ 1. Since (150, 51) = (150, 99) = 3, hypothesis (2) of Theorem 2.1 fails at 151. (Theorem 3.1 does apply at 151, through the non-ordinary pair (60, 94).) The same search finds pairs with gcd 1 at 107, 139, 173, 179, 191 and 193. - **Gap** at Remark 2.5, p. 515 (it affects the proof). The source says `"Combining Theorem 2.4 with the descent result [CKPSS04, Thm. 7.2], we see that there is a globally generic, non-endoscopic, cuspidal automorphic representation for Sp_104/Q of level one and weight zero."`; it should be `Add the local step showing that the descended σ is unramified at every prime ℓ, given that its local lift π_ℓ is unramified (for example through Arthur's local classification for Sp_2n, which arXiv v1 of the paper offered as an alternative route to the descent).`. [CKPSS04, Thm. 7.2] describes the image of the global generic lift and says nothing about local components. [CKPSS04, §7.2] treats local lifts only through γ-factors and notes that local descent for SO_2n and Sp_2n was not yet available. So 'level one' for the representation of Sp_104, and with it H^*_cusp(𝒜_52, C) ≠ 0, is asserted without the needed local input. The conclusion is expected to hold. ### Bresciani, "On the birational section conjecture with strong birationality assumptions", Inventiones Mathematicae (2024) (`PAPER-BRESCIANI-24`)

- **Error** at §4, the paragraph after Corollary 15 on parabolic curves, v3 p.11 (published p.141), in arXiv 2108.13397v3 (15 September 2023, the final author version; PDF SHA-256 e23989fb…b722); published page numbers are those the extraction recorded from the open-access publisher PDF (SHA-256 77c20bc7…e148), which could not be re-fetched here (it affects the proof). The source says `"We say that a curve X is parabolic if it has genus 0 and the degree of X̄ ∖ X is at most 2. If X is proper of genus 0, then there is a unique Galois section, and it is geometric if and only if X = P¹. If X is parabolic affine, every Galois section of X is cuspidal (the only non-trivial case is settled by [Sch15, Theorem A]). Because of this, we can always assume that X is non-parabolic."`; it should be `The sentence is false as stated. What the later reductions need is a statement about the t-birationally liftable (or birationally liftable) sections of a parabolic affine X, which have to be treated directly, including the case where X̄ ∖ X is a single point of degree 2. The reductions to non-parabolic X rest on this: "We may assume that X is non-parabolic" in the proof of Lemma 16, and "it is immediate to check that t-b.l. sections are quasi-t-b.l." after Definition 18, where the identity map is not available for parabolic X.`. Counterexample. Let k = Q and X = P¹_Q ∖ V(T² − 2). Then X is affine, of genus 0, with boundary a single point of degree 2, so it is parabolic. The paper defines a Galois section to be cuspidal if it is associated with a k-rational point of X̄ ∖ X (§1). The boundary has no Q-rational point, so X has no cuspidal sections. But 0 ∈ X(Q), so X has a geometric section. Schmidt's theorem concerns the existence of rational points and splitting; it does not make a degree-2 boundary point rational. The paper's Theorems A–C are not claimed to fail here, only this justification of the reduction. Noted by the extraction (item 77, with the reduction gap in items 81, 96 and 112). - **Gap** at Proof of Lemma 26, v3 p.17 (published p.148), in arXiv 2108.13397v3 (15 September 2023, the final author version; PDF SHA-256 e23989fb…b722); published page numbers are those the extraction recorded from the open-access publisher PDF (SHA-256 77c20bc7…e148), which could not be re-fetched here (it affects the proof). The source says `"For every open subset U ⊂ X, denote by ι_U(s) the image of s in S_{U/k}, by hypothesis it is cuspidal. By [Sti12, Theorem 17 (2)], there exists a unique rational point x ∈ X̄(k) associated with ι_X(s) … For every open subset U ⊂ X, denote by P_{U,x} ⊂ S_{U/k} the packet of cuspidal sections associated with x. … Since ι_U(s) ∈ P_{U,x} for every U ⊂ X and s is the limit of the sections ι_U(s), we get that s is cuspidal associated with x too."`; it should be `By hypothesis each ι_U(s) is geometric or cuspidal, not necessarily cuspidal. Once the associated point x ∈ X̄(k) is found, restrict to the cofinal family of opens U with x ∉ U. For those, ι_U(s) is cuspidal at x, and S_{k(X)/k} = lim_U S_{U/k} may be computed along this family, so the conclusion follows.`. The hypothesis of the lemma is that every birationally liftable section is geometric or cuspidal. If x ∈ X, then for opens U containing x the section ι_U(s) is the geometric section at x, which is not in the cuspidal packet P_{U,x}. So "ι_U(s) ∈ P_{U,x} for every U ⊂ X" fails for those U, although the limit argument goes through on the cofinal subfamily. Noted by the extraction (item 127). - **Misprint** at Dependency Bresciani, Essential dimension and pro-finite group schemes, arXiv:1904.00789v2, Lemma 5.8 proof, last sentence p.20. The source says `banded by A`; it should be `The band is TA, the full Tate module, as in the lemma statement.`. The proof identifies the gerbe with the inverse limit of gerbes banded by A[n]; its band is therefore lim_n A[n]=TA. The semiabelian variety A is not that profinite band. Recorded as `PAPER-BRESCIANI-24/E7`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv:1904.00789v2, Lemma 5.8 statement and proof checked 2026-09-22; the statement already says TA. Only this author version is asserted to contain the slip.; Title/erratum web search and the author institutional publication list checked 2026-09-22; no separate correction located. Publication-version comparison remains open..

### Burungale–Kobayashi–Ota, "Rubin's conjecture on local units in the anticyclotomic tower at inert primes", Annals of Mathematics 194 (2021), no. 3 (`PAPER-BURUNGALE-KOBAYASHI-OTA-21`)

- **Error** at Lemma 5.2(ii), p.956, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 194 (2021), 943–966 (Caltech repository copy of the publisher PDF, SHA-256 77ff3290…790e) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"(ii) The number of supersingular points in X₀(N)(F_{p²}) that correspond to supersingular elliptic curves over F_{p²} with the Frobenius trace a_{p²} = 2p or −2p and the j-invariant different from 0 and 1728 is at least µ(⌊(p−1)/12⌋ − 3/2 + (−3/p) + ½(−1/p)). In particular, there is a supersingular point with a_{p²} = 2p or −2p in X₀(N)(F_{p²}) over X(1)(F_{p²})∖{0,1728} that is unramified for π if p ≡ 1 mod 12 or p ≥ 31."`; it should be `The "in particular" follows for p ≡ 1 mod 12 and for p ≥ 31 with p ≠ 47. At p = 47 the displayed lower bound is 0 and gives nothing. The existence of an unramified supersingular point at p = 47 comes instead from Lemma 5.2(iii), since 47 ≡ 2 mod 3.`. The conclusion needs the lower bound to exceed the number of ramification points, which (i) bounds by µ/6. At p = 47: ⌊46/12⌋ = 3, (−3/47) = −1 (47 ≡ 2 mod 3) and (−1/47) = −1 (47 ≡ 3 mod 4), so the bound is µ(3 − 3/2 − 1 − 1/2) = 0 < µ/6. A computation over all primes 31 ≤ p ≤ 400 finds 47 as the only failure, and beyond that the floor term dominates. The main theorem is unaffected: Lemma 5.2(iii) covers p = 47, and Rubin's own verification (Remark 2.3) covers small p. Found by the review REV-PAPER-BURUNGALE-KOBAYASHI-OTA-21. - **Misprint** at §3.0.2 and Proposition 3.3, pp.951–953; proof of Theorem 3.4, p.954, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 194 (2021), 943–966 (Caltech repository copy of the publisher PDF, SHA-256 77ff3290…790e). The source says `"Let ν ∈ X_l be a Hecke character of order l^m. We define ξ_ν as the image of z_{fl^m} …" and "Proposition 3.3. Let ν ∈ X_l be a character of order l^m", while the same construction uses K(fl^m p^{n+1}), "the conductor fl^m of ϕν" (proof of 3.3(3)) and "let l^m be the conductor" (p.954).`; it should be `Read l^m as the conductor of ν throughout §3.0.2–3.0.3 (so that ν factors through K(fl^m) and the elliptic unit z_{fl^m} applies), with the anticyclotomic layer L_m indexed accordingly.`. For l split in K, the l-part of the ring class group of conductor l^n is (Z/l^n)^×, of order (l−1)l^{n−1}, so an anticyclotomic character of exact order l^m (m ≥ 1) has conductor l^{m+1}, not l^m. The construction and the proofs use l^m as the conductor, so "order" is the slip; nothing else changes. The extraction silently used the conductor reading. ### Burungale–Tian, "A rank zero p-converse to a theorem of Gross–Zagier, Kolyvagin and Rubin", Annals of Mathematics 203 (2026), no. 1 (`PAPER-BURUNGALE-TIAN-26`)

- **Error** at Theorem 2.1 and footnote 3, p.4, in arXiv 2506.03465v2 (11 October 2025), the version read; the passage is the same in v1 (4 June 2025) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Theorem 2.1. Let the notation be as above. Then we have ξ(h²_Q) = ξ((h¹/z)_Q), an equality of ideals in Z_p[[G_{p∞f}]]⊗Q." Footnote 3: "The formulation in Theorem 2.1 is then identical with the one in [11, §7.2]."`; it should be `ξ((h¹/z)_Q) = ξ(h²_Q)·∏_{𝔩|f, 𝔩∤p} ξ(Z_p[[G_{p∞f}/D_𝔩]]_Q), D_𝔩 the decomposition group of 𝔩; equivalently ξ((h¹/z)_Q) = ξ(H²_Q) with H² = lim_{K'} H²(O_{K'}[1/pf], Z_p(1)), which is the module of Johnson-Leung–Kings §7.2. The printed equality holds when every prime factor of f lies above p, and after localizing at height-one primes on components ramified at every 𝔩|f with 𝔩∤p.`. z is generated by the elliptic units of the single modulus p^n f (Kato §15.5), whose projection to a character χ carries ∏_{𝔩|f,𝔩∤p}(1 − χ(𝔩)Frob_𝔩^{−1}) (Johnson-Leung–Kings Lemma 7.7); JLK accordingly pair it with H² over O_K[1/pf]. BT's h² is the class-group limit, which Kato (15.6.2) identifies at height one with H² over O_K[1/p]. By purity, 0 → H²(O[1/p]) → H²(O[1/pf]) → ⊕_{𝔩|f,𝔩∤p} Z_p[[G/D_𝔩]] → 0, and since D_𝔩 has Z_p-rank one each term is torsion but not pseudo-null. On the trivial-character component 1 − Frob_𝔩^{−1} is a non-unit even after inverting p, so the printed equality fails whenever f has a prime factor not above p, and footnote 3's "identical" is false for h². The same imprecision is in Kato's quotation of Rubin (Astérisque 295, Theorem 15.2(2)); Rubin's elliptic units include all moduli g|f. ### Cadoret–Hui–Tamagawa, "Geometric monodromy — semisimplicity and maximality", Annals of Mathematics 186 (2017), no. 1 (`PAPER-CADORET-HUI-TAMAGAWA-17`)

- **Misprint** at Published p.218, Lemma 5.2, sentence after the displayed finite étale trace; same omission in author GSS.pdf. The source says `H^w(U′,Λℓ)`; it should be `Replace this direct-summand target by H_c^w(U′,Λℓ).`. Both arrows in the immediately preceding restriction/trace composite have compact support. ### Calegari–Dimitrov–Tang, "The unbounded denominators conjecture", Journal of the American Mathematical Society (2025) (`PAPER-CALEGARI-DIMITROV-TANG-25`)

- **Misprint** at Lemma 4.5.11, second line, p.663 (published version; the same in arXiv v3–v4). The source says `“dim H¹(Γ(5), F₃)^{SL₂(F₅)} = 0,” and, for N = 3, 4, 5, “the same result holds even after considering the semisimplifications of these modules.”`; it should be `dim H¹(Γ(5), 𝔽₅)^{SL₂(𝔽₅)} = 0 (coefficients 𝔽₅), and likewise for its semisimplification.`. Diagram (4.5.10) requires ℓ | N, and the proof of Theorem 4.5.2 takes N = ℓ ∈ {3, 5}, so ℓ = 5 is the case used. As printed the statement is false: SL₂(𝔽₅) is perfect, so inflation–restriction for Γ(5) ⊴ SL₂(ℤ) makes restriction H¹(SL₂(ℤ), 𝔽₃) = Hom(ℤ/12, 𝔽₃) ≅ 𝔽₃ → H¹(Γ(5), 𝔽₃)^{SL₂(𝔽₅)} injective (the character SL₂(ℤ) → SL₂(𝔽₃) → ℤ/3 stays nontrivial on Γ(5), which maps onto SL₂(𝔽₃)). Over 𝔽₃ the semisimplification claim also fails: H¹(Γ(5), ℚ) = 3 + 3′ + 5 as an A₅-module, and the 5-dimensional character, with Brauer character (5, 1, 0, 0) on the 3-regular classes, reduces mod 3 to 1 + 4. Over 𝔽₅ the lemma holds: H¹(SL₂(ℤ), 𝔽₅) = 0 and H²(SL₂(𝔽₅), 𝔽₅) = 0 (Lemma 4.5.5) give vanishing invariants, and 3, 3′, 5 reduce to the Brauer irreducibles 3, 3, 5 of PSL₂(𝔽₅), none trivial. The source says `“There is a natural surjection π : ⟨E, Γ(N)⟩ ∩ Γ0(p) → B whose kernel is Γ(Np).”`; it should be `For N > 2 the kernel of reduction mod p on ⟨E, Γ(N)⟩ ∩ Γ₀(p) is ⟨E, Γ(N)⟩ ∩ Γ(p), which contains Γ(Np) with index 2. Restrict to Γ(N) ∩ Γ₀(p), where the kernel is Γ(Np): Lemma 4.4.4 holds there, and it gives ⟨G ∩ Γ(Np), A⁻¹GA ∩ Γ₀(p) ∩ Γ(N)⟩ = Γ(N) ∩ Γ₀(p); adjoining E gives (4.4.9) as printed. Twisting π by the sign x ≡ ±I mod N works equally.`. Some x ∈ SL₂(ℤ) has x ≡ −I mod N and x ≡ I mod p (SL₂(ℤ) → SL₂(ℤ/Np) is onto). It lies in ⟨E, Γ(N)⟩ ∩ Γ₀(p) and in ker π, but not in Γ(Np). So, as written, ker(f₁, π) = G ∩ Γ(p), and the deduction yields (4.4.9) only with G ∩ Γ(p). That is weaker than the (4.4.10) used in the proof of Theorem 4.3.1, where f(pτ) is known to be invariant only under G ∩ Γ(2Np). The proof of Lemma 4.4.4 already works on Γ(N) ∩ Γ₀(p): (f₁, f₂) stays surjective there, and π(γ) still generates the unipotent radical. So the corrected deduction goes through, and Theorems 4.3.1–4.3.2 stand. The source says `“By Lemma 5.2.6, the set {z ∈ D(0, 1) : |FN(z)| > e^M + 1} is contained in S(M, N)”`; it should be `… is contained in s·S(M, N), the image of S(M, N) under the order-2N rotation s of Lemma 5.2.6.`. On that set |1 − F_N^N| ≥ |F_N|^N − 1 > e^{MN}, so it is disjoint from S(M, N). By (5.2.7) it lies in s·S(M, N). Since s is a Euclidean rotation of D(0,1) about 0, s·S(M, N) also avoids the closed disc of radius r, and the conclusion stands. arXiv v1 (“contained in the rotation under s of the set S(M, N)”) and v3 (“contained in sS(M, N)”) had it right; the s was lost in v4. In the same proof the Shimizu bound is quoted for γ̃ ∈ Γ̃_N but used for γ ∈ Φ_N; Φ̃_N is discrete and contains t̃_N, so Shimizu's lemma gives the same bound. ### Canning–Larson–Payne, "Extensions of tautological rings and motivic structures in the cohomology of the moduli spaces of stable curves", Forum of Mathematics, Pi (2024) (`PAPER-CANNING-LARSON-PAYNE-24`)

- **Misprint** at First sentence of the proof of Theorem 1.5(3), Section 6.2 (arXiv v3; the published version at doi:10.1017/fmp.2024.24 has the same text). The source says `We now show that H_k(M̄_{g,n}) is tautological for even k ≥ 14.`; it should be `for even k ≤ 14.`. Theorem 1.5(3) states that RH^*(M̄_{g,n}) contains H_k(M̄_{g,n}) for even k ≤ 14, and the proof itself uses the opposite inequality two sentences later: 'When g ≥ 3, in all of these cases with k ≤ 14 we know that M_{g,n} has the CKgP'. The proof is correct with k ≤ 14; with k ≥ 14 it would assert the statement in degrees where it is open (Conjecture 1.8 concerns even k ≤ 20) and where the table of known CKgP cases does not apply. Recorded as `PAPER-CANNING-LARSON-PAYNE-24/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: the arXiv listing and submission history of arXiv:2307.08830, read 22 September 2026: v1 (17 July 2023), v2 and v3 (25 October 2024), the version read, described by the authors as the final version to appear in Forum of Mathematics, Pi; there is no later version; the published version, doi:10.1017/fmp.2024.24 (Forum of Mathematics, Pi 12 (2024), open access on Cambridge Core), read 22 September 2026: the article carries no erratum, and the sentence recorded below appears there in the same form.

On an n-dimensional abelian variety, cycles of dimensions r_1 and r_2 have codimensions n − r_1 and n − r_2. Their intersection has codimension 2n − r_1 − r_2, that is, dimension r_1 + r_2 − n. - **Error** at §7.1–7.2, the series Ψ^{[m]}, Lemma 7.4 and the definitions of Log^t and Exp^t, pp.23–24, in arXiv 2102.01055v2 (10 February 2021, the latest arXiv version, 39 pp.; PDF SHA-256 d7398c4b…67f7); the published article, Invent. Math. 234 (2023), 1197–1250 (54 pp., substantially revised), was not accessible and was not collated, so this finding concerns v2 (it affects the proof). The source says `"Given an integer m, let Ψ^{[m]} = (Ψ^{[m]}_1, ..., Ψ^{[m]}_n) be the power series expansion of the morphism [m] : A → A of multiplication by m in terms of t." "From [11] III.5.3 Proposition 2(i) we get Lemma 7.4. The terms of degree less than m in Ψ^{[m]}_j vanish for each j = 1, ..., n." "Log^t_j(y) = Σ_{m≥1} ((−1)^m/m) · Ψ^{[m]}_j(y), Exp^t_j(x) = Σ_{m≥1} (1/m!) · ψ^{[m]}_j(x)", with ψ^{[m]}_j the homogeneous part of degree m of Ψ^{[m]}_j.`; it should be `Take Ψ^{[m]} to be the m-th finite difference of the multiplication maps, Ψ^{[m]} = Σ_{i=0}^{m} (−1)^{m−i} C(m,i) [i], the series of Bourbaki III.5.3. Take Log^t_j(y) = Σ_{m≥1} ((−1)^{m−1}/m) Ψ^{[m]}_j(y). With these changes Lemma 7.4 is Bourbaki's statement. Lemma 7.2 still holds, as Ψ^{[m]} is an integral combination. Exp^t is the exponential, and the proofs of Lemmas 7.5–7.7 go through as written.`. As defined, Ψ^{[m]} is multiplication by m, whose linear term is m·t_j, so Lemma 7.4 fails already for the additive formal group and m = 2. Then Log^t_j has linear term Σ_m (−1)^m y_j, which does not converge coefficientwise. The printed Exp^t is also wrong in general. For the one-dimensional formal group with logarithm f(t) = t + at² + bt³, the printed Exp^t (degree-m parts of [m], weighted by 1/m!) has cubic coefficient 6a² − 4b, while the true exponential f^{−1} has 2a² − b; the finite differences give the correct 2a² − b, checked by exact computation to degree 4. The sign: with finite differences, Σ ((−1)^{m−1}/m) Ψ^{[m]} equals the logarithm f, while the printed (−1)^m/m gives −f, which is not inverse to Exp^t as Lemma 7.7(ii) asserts. The bounds used later (|b_{j,α}| ≤ ‖α‖^{[K:Q_p]}, m!c_{j,α} ∈ R) hold for the corrected series, so the main results are unaffected. Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction (gap G4, items formal-low-degree-source, formal-log-source and formal-exp), which proposed a different repair; the finite-difference reading is given here. The source says `"If assumption (ii) in Theorem 9.1 holds, then there is an ample divisor H′ on A′ such that deg_{H′}(X′) = deg_H(X), cdeg_{H′}(X′) = cdeg_H(X), and deg((H′)³)_{A′} = deg(H³)_A."`; it should be `deg((H′)^n)_{A′} = deg(H^n)_A.`. A has dimension n ≥ 3 in Theorem 9.1(ii), whose threshold involves deg(H^n). The top self-intersection is H^n; H³ is the n = 3 case. Noted by the extraction (gap G5, item good-geometry). - **Gap** at Proof of Lemma 9.7, p.31, in arXiv 2102.01055v2 (10 February 2021, the latest arXiv version, 39 pp.; PDF SHA-256 d7398c4b…67f7); the published article, Invent. Math. 234 (2023), 1197–1250 (54 pp., substantially revised), was not accessible and was not collated, so this finding concerns v2 (it affects the proof). The source says `"Thus, we can take v_1, v_2 ∈ ker(f) ∩ R^n which are K-linearly independent. After scaling, we can assume that v_1, v_2 ∈ R^n are primitive. If there are b_1, b_2 ∈ R^× with b_1v_1 + b_2v_2 ∈ pR^n ⊆ R^n then there are c_1, c_2 ∈ R such that b_1v_1 + b_2v_2 = ϖc_1v_1 + ϖc_2v_2 which is not possible since v_1, v_2 are K-linearly independent. Hence the images of v_1, v_2 in k^n are k-linearly independent."`; it should be `Take v_1, v_2 to be two members of an R-basis of the saturated lattice ker(f) ∩ R^n. That lattice is a direct summand of R^n of rank n − 1 ≥ 2, so any basis of it reduces to k-linearly independent vectors.`. The step from b_1v_1 + b_2v_2 ∈ ϖR^n to b_1v_1 + b_2v_2 ∈ ϖ(Rv_1 + Rv_2) assumes that Rv_1 + Rv_2 is saturated in R^n. For v_1 = e_1 and v_2 = e_1 + ϖe_2 in R³, both are primitive and K-independent, but v_2 − v_1 = ϖe_2 and their reductions coincide. The lemma itself holds by the corrected choice, so nothing later changes. Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction (gap G5, item primitive-proof-source). ### Česnavičius, "Purity for the Brauer group", Duke Mathematical Journal (2019) (`PAPER-CESNAVICIUS-19`)

- **Gap** at Proposition 2.2 and its proof, p.4, in arXiv 1711.06456v4 (1 December 2018, the latest version; PDF SHA-256 a62a12bb…8709); the author's copy of 27 November 2018 on his homepage has the same text; the published text, Duke Math. J. 168 (2019), 1461–1486, was not collated (it affects the proof). The source says `"Proposition 2.2. For a finite, flat map R → R′ of local rings, an open subscheme V ⊂ Spec R, and an affine, smooth R-group scheme G, if (1) Γ(Spec R, O) ≅ Γ(V, O) via pullback; and (2) every G-torsor is trivial over R; then the following pullback is injective: H¹_et(V, G) ↪ H¹_et(V_{R′}, G). (2.2.1)" Proof: "… every element of X(V) lifts to (Res_{R′/R}(G_{R′}))(V), so, by [Gir71, III.3.2.2], the map H¹_et(V, G) → H¹_et(V, Res_{R′/R}(G_{R′})) (2.2.2) is injective."`; it should be `State Proposition 2.2 for commutative G, which covers both of its uses (Proposition 2.3, with G = Res_{R′/R}(T_{R′})/T, and Corollary 2.4, with G = T). For noncommutative G, supply the missing step: for every G-torsor P over V, the twisted map (^P Res_{R′/R}(G_{R′}))(V) → (^P X)(V) must also be surjective.`. Write H = Res_{R′/R}(G_{R′}) and X = H/G. Surjectivity of H(V) → X(V) shows that the fibre of H¹(V, G) → H¹(V, H) over the trivial class is trivial. When G is commutative this is injectivity, because the map is a group homomorphism. For a noncommutative G, H¹ is only a pointed set. The fibre through the class of a torsor P is the orbit set of (^P H)(V) acting on (^P X)(V), for the twists by P (Giraud III.3.2; cf. Serre, Galois Cohomology, I.5.5, Cor. 2). The proof checks only the untwisted case. The twisted forms ^P X live only over V, so hypothesis (1) and the affineness of X give no control of their V-points. If P extends to R it is trivial by (2), so the problem is exactly the torsors on V that do not extend. Proposition 2.2 is used only in the proofs of Proposition 2.3 (G = Res_{R′/R}(T_{R′})/T) and Corollary 2.4 (G = T, an R-torus), where G is commutative, so the main theorems are unaffected. Noted by the extraction (gap G-NONABELIAN, items h1-descent and h1-abelian), which did not claim the statement false; neither is it claimed false here. Recorded as `PAPER-CESNAVICIUS-19/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Duke Mathematical Journal: Crossref record of DOI 10.1215/00127094-2018-0057, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum or other update is registered; arXiv 1711.06456: v1 (17 November 2017), v2 (24 November 2017), v3 (17 January 2018), v4 (1 December 2018, the latest); the author's copy https://www.imo.universite-paris-saclay.fr/~kestutis.cesnavicius/brauer-purity.pdf (dated 27 November 2018, SHA-256 4187331e…97a1), checked 22 September 2026: Proposition 2.2 and its proof are unchanged, and no erratum is linked; web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found.

### Chang–Chen–Mishiba, "On Thakur's basis conjecture for multiple zeta values in positive characteristic", Forum of Mathematics, Pi (2023) (`PAPER-CHANG-CHEN-MISHIBA-23`)

- **Misprint** at Section 2.2, the definition of the power sums, just before Definition 2.2.2 (published version: Section 2.2, before Definition 2.4); present in arXiv v2 and in the published version. The source says `S^{ζ}_{d}(s) := Σ_{a ∈ A_{+,d}} 1/a^{d} ∈ k`; it should be `S^{ζ}_{d}(s) := Σ_{a ∈ A_{+,d}} 1/a^{s} ∈ k`. The exponent must be s, not d: S^ζ_d(s) is the power sum of exponent s over the monic polynomials of degree d. With the printed exponent the symbol would not depend on s at all, and Remark 2.2.12 — which states Carlitz's identity 1/L_d^s = Σ_{a ∈ A_{+,d}} 1/a^s and concludes 𝔏^{Li}_d(s) = 𝔏^ζ_d(s) for 1 ≤ s ≤ q — uses the exponent s. The definition of ζ_A(s) in Section 1.2 likewise sums a^{-s} over monic a. Checked against the published text on Cambridge Core, which prints the same formula. Recorded as `PAPER-CHANG-CHEN-MISHIBA-23/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: the arXiv listing and submission history of arXiv:2205.09929, read 22 September 2026: v1 (20 May 2022) and v2 (11 July 2022), the version read, whose comment is 'added Section 5.4, corrected a few typos'; there is no later preprint version; the published version, doi:10.1017/fmp.2023.26 (Forum of Mathematics, Pi 11 (2023), open access on Cambridge Core), read 22 September 2026: the article carries no erratum, and each finding below was compared with the published text.
- **Misprint** at Remark 1.2.5 (published: Remark 1.6), the displayed upper bound; present in arXiv v2 and in the published version. The source says `dim_{w} Z_{w} ≤ d'_{w}, ∀ w ≥ 1`; it should be `dim_{k} Z_{w} ≤ d'_{w}, ∀ w ≥ 1`. Z_w is a k-vector space and its dimension is taken over k everywhere else in the paper (Conjecture 1.2.2 writes dim_k Z_w = d'_w, and Corollary 1.2.7 writes dim_k Z_{v,w}); 'dim_w' is not defined. Checked against the published text on Cambridge Core, which prints the same subscript. Recorded as `PAPER-CHANG-CHEN-MISHIBA-23/E3`; looked for an existing correction in: the arXiv listing and submission history of arXiv:2205.09929, read 22 September 2026: v1 (20 May 2022) and v2 (11 July 2022), the version read, whose comment is 'added Section 5.4, corrected a few typos'; there is no later preprint version; the published version, doi:10.1017/fmp.2023.26 (Forum of Mathematics, Pi 11 (2023), open access on Cambridge Core), read 22 September 2026: the article carries no erratum, and each finding below was compared with the published text.

### François Charles, "Birational boundedness for holomorphic symplectic varieties, Zarhin's trick for K3 surfaces, and the Tate conjecture", Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), no. 2 (`PAPER-CHARLES-16`)

- **Error** at Corollary 2.8 and its proof, pp.499–500, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 487–526 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 3425f269…40e2) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"If p > 0, we can find nonnegative integers λ and t such that 0 < λ ≤ v² and |disc(NS(X))| = p^t λ |disc(NS(M_H(v)))|." Proof: "… we have a natural injection of lattices with torsion cokernel Zv ⊕ (v^⊥ ∩ N(X)) ↪ N(X). Since the discriminant of Zv ⊕ v^⊥ is v² disc(v^⊥ ∩ N(X)), this implies by the same argument that |disc(N(X))| ≤ v² |disc(v^⊥ ∩ N(X))|."`; it should be `λ need not be an integer. With I = [N(X) : Zv ⊕ (v^⊥ ∩ N(X))], one has |disc NS(X)| = p^t (v²/I²) |disc NS(M_H(v))| with t ≥ 0. So λ = v²/I² is a positive rational number with λ ≤ v², and I divides v² because v is primitive. For p ∤ v², for example v² = 2 and p ≥ 3 as in the applications, λ is still a p-adic unit, which is what the later arguments use.`. Take v = (1, 0, −1), the Mukai vector of ideal sheaves of length-two subschemes, so M_H(v) = X^[2]. It satisfies condition (C): it is primitive, of rank 1, v² = 2, and gcd(1, 0, −1) = 1. Then v^⊥ ∩ N(X) = NS(X) ⊕ Z(1, 0, 1) with (1, 0, 1)² = −2, so |disc(v^⊥ ∩ N(X))| = 2|disc NS(X)|. The proof's first step gives |disc(v^⊥ ∩ N(X))| = p^r |disc NS(M_H(v))|. Hence |disc NS(X)| = ½ p^r |disc NS(M_H(v))|, which is not of the form p^t λ with λ a positive integer when p is odd. The proof passes from the equality disc(Zv ⊕ K) = I² disc N(X) to an integer multiplier without controlling I, here I = 2. The inequality stated for p = 0 is correct. Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction (items 34–35). Recorded as `PAPER-CHARLES-16/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2016/184-2/p04 and the Crossref record of DOI 10.4007/annals.2016.184.2.4, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum or update; arXiv 1407.0592: v1 (2 July 2014) and v2 (24 August 2014, the latest); F. Charles's publication page https://www.math.ens.psl.eu/~charles/, checked 22 September 2026: it links an erratum for Invent. Math. 194 (2013) only, not for this paper; web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Error** at Proposition 2.9, p.500, as used in the proof of Theorem 2.10, p.503, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 487–526 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 3425f269…40e2) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Proposition 2.9. Let d be a positive integer, and let Λ be a rank 2 positive definite sublattice of Λ_2d. There exists a positive integer N and nonzero integers a, b such that if m is any positive integer satisfying (i) m = 1[N]; (ii) m is prime to a and b, and both a and b are quadratic residues modulo m; then there exists a primitive embedding of Λ into Λ_2md." Proof: "In the setting of the proposition, the primitive embedding of Λ into Λ_2d corresponds to a tuple (V, W, γ, t)."`; it should be `Assume Λ is a primitive sublattice of Λ_2d. In the proof of Theorem 2.10, choose the lattice Λ containing v, w with v² = 2, v·w = 1 to be primitive. For example, in Λ_2d = ⟨2d⟩ ⊕ U with basis h, e, f, take the span of v = e + f and w = kh + e. It is primitive, since (x, y, z) ↦ (z, y − z) retracts it, and its Gram matrix [[2, 1], [1, 2dk²]] is positive definite.`. The proof starts from a primitive embedding Λ ⊂ Λ_2d, which the statement does not provide, and the statement fails for non-primitive Λ. In Λ_2 = ⟨2⟩ ⊕ U, the sublattice Λ generated by 2h and 2(e + f) has Gram matrix diag(8, 8). Take m = 1, which satisfies (i) and (ii) for every N, a, b. A primitive embedding of Λ into Λ_2 would give a 2-dimensional subspace of Λ_2/2Λ_2 = F_2³ on which the half-norm Q(x, y, z) = x² + yz vanishes, since Λ has all half-norms even. The nonzero zeros of Q are (0,0,1), (0,1,0) and (1,1,1), and each pairwise sum has Q = 1, so no such plane exists. Noted by the extraction (items 38–41), which also gave the primitive choice above. The source says `"By Riemann-Roch, for any p and q as above, the morphism H⁰(C, 3L|_C) → 3L|_C/(3L|_C − D(p) − D(q)) is surjective. Indeed, the degree of 3L|_C − D(p) − D(q) is 3r − 4 > r = deg(ω_{C/k}); note that r is even, so that r > 1."`; it should be `Pass to 2L before this step, as the proof already allows ("up to replacing L by 2L"), so that r = L² ≥ 4 and 3r − 4 > r holds.`. 3r − 4 > r holds only for r > 2. "r is even, so that r > 1" leaves r = 2, the double-plane case, where 3r − 4 = r and the surjectivity argument as written does not apply. The proposition only claims the existence of some L′ with bounded invariants, so doubling L is allowed. The source says `"Let N_{α/r}(X) be the preimage of N(X) by T_{−α/r}. This is the group denoted by CH_{α/r}(X, Z) in [LMS14]. By [LMS14, Lemma 3.3.3], we have (4.1) N_{α/r}(X) = {(ar, D + aα, cω) | a, c ∈ Z, D ∈ NS(X)}."`; it should be `Define N_{α/r}(X) directly as the lattice (4.1), the twisted Mukai lattice CH_{α/r}(X, Z) of [LMS14], and drop "the preimage of N(X) by T_{−α/r}".`. With T_{−α/r}(x) = x ∪ e^{−α/r} and e^{−α/r} = (1, −α/r, α²/(2r²)), one computes T_{−α/r}(ar, D + aα, cω) = (ar, D, (c − (α·D)/r − aα²/(2r))ω). Its last coordinate is not an integer in general, for α ∈ H²(X, Z_ℓ(1)) primitive and r = ℓ^n. So the lattice (4.1) is not the preimage of N(X). Lemma 4.2, Proposition 4.3 and §4.2 compute with the explicit lattice (4.1), so the results are unaffected. Noted by the extraction (§4.1 conventions, items 93–106). ### William Y. Chen, "Nonabelian level structures, Nielsen equivalence, and Markoff triples", Annals of Mathematics 199 (2024), no. 1 (`PAPER-CHEN-24`)

- **Error** at §5.6, p. 85, Proposition 5.6.1(a) (arXiv v2). The source says `There is a unique unramified point in f^{−1}(0). Every other point is ramified with index 3, so |f^{−1}(0)| = (p(p+3)−3)/3 if p ≡ 1 mod 4, (p(p−3)−3)/3 if p ≡ 3 mod 4`; it should be `|f^{−1}(0)| = 1 + (deg f − 1)/3 = (p(p+3)+2)/3 for p ≡ 1 mod 4 and (p(p−3)+2)/3 for p ≡ 3 mod 4.`. The printed expression is never an integer: deg f = |X*(p)| = p(p±3) ≡ p² ≡ 1 (mod 3) for every prime p ≠ 3, so 3 never divides deg f − 3. The sentence preceding the display gives the correct count, one unramified point together with (deg f − 1)/3 points of ramification index 3. Computing the γ₀-orbits on X*(p) directly confirms it: for p = 5, 7, 11, 13, 17, 19, 23 the fibre has 14, 10, 30, 70, 114, 102, 154 points and γ₀ has exactly one fixed point in each case, matching (deg f + 2)/3 = 14, 10, 30, 70, 114, 102, 154 and not (deg f − 3)/3 = 37/3, 25/3, 85/3, 205/3, 337/3, 301/3, 457/3. Recorded as `PAPER-CHEN-24/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: the arXiv listing of 2011.12940 on 2026-09-22: two versions (25 November 2020 and 19 June 2021) and the author's comment, which announces a revision but no erratum; Crossref for 10.4007/annals.2024.199.1.5 and for works correcting it on 2026-09-22: no corrigendum; the Annals of Mathematics listing for volume 199 (2024), no. 1 on 2026-09-22: no erratum linked.
- **Misprint** at §5.6, p. 85, Proposition 5.6.1(b), first sentence (arXiv v2). The source says `There are precisely two unramified points in π^{−1}(1728) if p ≡ 1, 7 mod 8, and no unramified points otherwise.`; it should be `f^{−1}(1728), the fibre of the forgetful map f : M_p → M(1) named in the statement; π is not defined in this proposition.`. The rest of the proposition, and part (a), use f for the map M_p → M(1); π is used elsewhere in the paper for the universal admissible cover C → E. The displayed formula in (b) is for |f^{−1}(1728)| and is correct: computing the γ₁₇₂₈-orbits on X*(p) gives 20, 15, 44, 104, 171, 152, 231 for p = 5, 7, 11, 13, 17, 19, 23, with 0, 2, 0, 0, 2, 0, 2 fixed points, matching the four cases of the display and the claim about p ≡ 1, 7 mod 8. Recorded as `PAPER-CHEN-24/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: the arXiv listing of 2011.12940 on 2026-09-22: two versions (25 November 2020 and 19 June 2021) and the author's comment, which announces a revision but no erratum; Crossref for 10.4007/annals.2024.199.1.5 and for works correcting it on 2026-09-22: no corrigendum; the Annals of Mathematics listing for volume 199 (2024), no. 1 on 2026-09-22: no erratum linked.

The source says `'We have a natural inclusion ρ: R^{r′} → R^r sending e_l to (1/√m_l) Σ_{i∈P_l} e_i … This embedding is an isometry … and it follows from Formula (2.4.4) that we have β_Q^F(ρ(x), ρ(y)) = β_{Q′}^F(x, y)'; Corollary 2.9: '… and if these inequalities hold we have μ_{Q′}^F = μ_Q^F'; Output: 'For j = 2, …, m, set x_j = (1/√m_j) Σ ϖ … Then for the element x = t_1 (1/√m)(π_1 + ⋯ + π_m) + Σ_{i=2}^r t_i x_i of RΠ_alg we have C^{F_ℓ}(x, x) ≤ β_Q^{F_ℓ}(t, t) < 0'`; it should be `With β_Q as printed in (2.4.4) (the factor 1/m_i on F̂(i/4π); this is what the authors' code computes), β_Q(ρx, ρy) = β_{Q′}(Dx, Dy) with D = diag(√m_l); μ_Q < 0 ⟺ μ_{Q′} < 0 still holds, but μ_{Q′} = μ_Q does not. The inequality C(x, x) ≤ β_Q(t, t) holds for x = (t_1/m)(π_1 + ⋯ + π_m) + Σ_{i=2}^r (t_i/m_i) Σ_{ϖ ↦ i} ϖ (weights 1/m, not 1/√m); and j runs over 2, …, r. Alternatively, replace (2.4.4) by β_Q(e_i, e_j) = F̂(i/4π) e_i·e_j − √(m_i m_j)(J_F(U_i·U_j) + F̂(0) δ_i δ_j (1 − ε(U_i·U_j))/4), for which the printed statements hold.`. One block of size 2 (r = 2, r′ = 1, K = J_F(U·U) + its e⊥ term): β_Q = ((A − K, −K), (−K, A − K)) has μ_Q = A − 2K for K > 0, while β_{Q′} = A/2 − K, so μ_Q = 2μ_{Q′}. The worker ported the authors' formulas (without_GRH.gp, testfin.gp), checked the port against seven of their certificate values (six digits), and evaluated the displayed elements. In Section 2.4.6 the printed C-values −0.173, −0.198, −0.147 are exactly β_Q(t, t), that is C(x′, x′) for the weights t_1/2; the displayed x (weights t_1/√2) have C ≈ −0.293, −0.321, −0.251. In Section 4.1 the displayed x (ℓ = 8.75) has C(x, x) ≈ +0.078 > 0 (every root number ε(U·U_j) involved is +1, so no e⊥ term enters): it is not a counterexample to Proposition 2.2. Its t is the least eigenvector of the printed Gram matrix (eigenvalue ≈ −0.0198, which is C(x′, x′)); the printed −0.0023 matches neither value. Every certificate in the paper is a negative value of β_Q as printed, which gives C(x′, x′) < 0 for the corrected x′, so Theorems 3–7, Proposition 4.1 and Lemma 4.3 are unaffected; the false statements are the equality in Corollary 2.9 and the inequality claimed in the Output of Algorithm 2.4.5. - **Misprint** at Theorem 1.11 and the paragraph before it, p.5, in arXiv 2311.15300v1 (26 November 2023, the only arXiv version; PDF SHA-256 7261083b…c103); the published text, Annals of Mathematics 204 (2026), 545–601 (revision of 8 October 2025), was not collated and may differ. Assume Conjectures 9.1 and 9.3; alternatively, assume π admits a parametrization as in [Laf18]."`; it should be `"Conjectures 9.1 and 9.5".`. (9.3) is a displayed decomposition, introduced with "for the moment we assume there is such an action, and that it gives rise to a decomposition". The numbered conjecture is Conjecture 9.5 (Lafforgue), whose part (1) asserts "There is a decomposition as in (9.3)". Noted by the extraction. Recorded as `PAPER-CIUBOTARU-HARRIS-26/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics: Crossref record of DOI 10.4007/annals.2026.204.2.3, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum or update registered; arXiv 2311.15300: v1 (26 November 2023) is the only version; web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Misprint** at Proof of Theorem 8.5, case (2) (G^∨ = Sp(2n)), p.29, in arXiv 2311.15300v1 (26 November 2023, the only arXiv version; PDF SHA-256 7261083b…c103); the published text, Annals of Mathematics 204 (2026), 545–601 (revision of 8 October 2025), was not collated and may differ. The source says `"We can use the standard representation of G^∨ to conclude that ν_i = 0 for all i odd. The remaining nonzero points are ν_j = ½ω^j_{⌊r_j/2⌋}, when j is odd and r_j ≥ 3, and all of these points are half-integral for G^∨."`; it should be `"… when j is even and r_j ≥ 3 …"`. The theorem's statement (1) lists ℜ(s) = ½h^∨ + ½Σ_{j even, r_j≥3} ε_j ω^j_{⌊r_j/2⌋}. The centralizer is Z(O^∨) = ∏_{i odd} Sp(r_i) × ∏_{j even} O(r_j), so the so(r_j) factors that carry the points are those with j even. The preceding sentence has just shown ν_i = 0 for i odd. Noted by the extraction. Recorded as `PAPER-CIUBOTARU-HARRIS-26/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics: Crossref record of DOI 10.4007/annals.2026.204.2.3, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum or update registered; arXiv 2311.15300: v1 (26 November 2023) is the only version; web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found.

(5) G^∨ = E_8: (a) O^∨ = D_6: … n_v(17) = n_u(17) = 0, n_v(16) = 2 > 1 = n_v(16)."`; it should be `n_v(9) = n_u(9) = 0 in (3)(a); n_v(9) = 1 > 0 = n_u(9) in (4); n_v(16) = 2 > 1 = n_u(16) in (5)(a).`. Each item exhibits a weight i_0 where Property A fails, that is, n_u(i) = n_v(i) for i > i_0 but n_u(i_0) < n_v(i_0). The other items state exactly this with n_u on the right. As printed, "n_v(9) = 1 > 0 = n_v(9)" and "n_v(16) = 2 > 1 = n_v(16)" are self-contradictory, and "n_v(9) = n_v(9)" is vacuous. Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction. ### Jean-Marc Couveignes, "Enumerating number fields", Annals of Mathematics 192 (2020), no. 2 (`PAPER-COUVEIGNES-20`)

- **Misprint** at §3, p.493, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 192 (2020), 487–497 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 as recorded by the extraction); the same line is in arXiv 1907.13617v2. The source says `"This is a free Z-module 𝓛 ⊂ V_R of rank ℓ = C(d+r, d) − n. We set L = 𝓛 ⊗_Q R as the underlying R-vector space and L^⊥ as its orthogonal complement in V_R."`; it should be `L = 𝓛 ⊗_Z R, the real span of 𝓛 in V_R (equivalently (𝓛 ⊗_Z Q) ⊗_Q R).`. 𝓛 is defined one sentence earlier as a free Z-module, so a tensor product over Q is not defined for it; the intended object, used in the rest of the section, is its real span. ### Dasgupta–Kakde, "On the Brumer–Stark conjecture", Annals of Mathematics 197 (2023), no. 1 (`PAPER-DASGUPTA-KAKDE-23`)

- **Misprint** at Proposition 8.6, displayed formula, in arXiv 2010.00657v3 (5 September 2022; the authors' final version, source dated 14 May 2022) and in the 14 May 2022 PDF on M. Kakde's homepage, p.52. The February 2022 version on S. Dasgupta's homepage (p.53) does not have it. The published version, Annals of Mathematics 197 (2023), 289–388, could not be checked (paywalled).. The source says `"For a cusp [𝒜] ∈ C₀(𝔠, 𝔫), the normalized constant term c_𝒜(0, W_k(ψ_𝔓, 1)) equals 𝔱 · τ(ψ)/N𝔠₀^k · sgn(N(−c)) ψ(𝔠_𝒜) · L(ψ^{−1}, 1−k)/2^n · ∏_{𝔭|𝔓}(1 − ψ(𝔭)/N𝔭^k) · ∏_{𝔩∈J_𝔱}(1 − ψ(𝔩)) · ∏_{𝔩∈J_𝔱^c}(1 − N𝔩^k)."`; it should be `Delete the leading 𝔱: c_𝒜(0, W_k(ψ_𝔓, 1)) = τ(ψ)/N𝔠₀^k · sgn(N(−c)) ψ(𝔠_𝒜) · L(ψ^{−1}, 1−k)/2^n · ∏_{𝔭|𝔓}(1 − ψ(𝔭)/N𝔭^k) · ∏_{𝔩∈J_𝔱}(1 − ψ(𝔩)) · ∏_{𝔩∈J_𝔱^c}(1 − N𝔩^k).`. 𝔱 is an integral ideal (the auxiliary modulus of §8.1), not a scalar, so the printed product has no meaning. The proof computes the constant term as the displayed expression with Σ_{𝔪|𝔱} μ(𝔪) ∏_{𝔩∈J_𝔪^c} N𝔩^k ∏_{𝔩∈J_𝔪} ψ(𝔩) in place of the last two products, and evaluates that sum by the Möbius identity; no factor 𝔱 appears. The February 2022 version prints the formula without it (with 𝔮 for 𝔩). Checked on the page images of both author versions and in the arXiv v3 source (`\ft` at the start of the display). Noted by the extraction (item 221). Recorded as `PAPER-DASGUPTA-KAKDE-23/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2023/197-1/p05, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum is linked; the published text itself is paywalled; arXiv 2010.00657: v1 (1 October 2020), v2 (6 November 2020), v3 (5 September 2022, the latest); v3 has the slip; authors' pages: S. Dasgupta's homepage PDF (February 2022, no slip) and M. Kakde's homepage PDF (14 May 2022, has the slip); neither lists a correction; web search for an erratum, corrigendum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found.

### DeMarco–Krieger–Ye, "Uniform Manin-Mumford for a family of genus 2 curves", Annals of Mathematics 191 (2020), no. 3 (`PAPER-DEMARCO-KRIEGER-YE-20`)

- **Misprint** at §§2.1–2.2, pp.6–7 (arXiv v2; published Annals version not collated). The source says `"can be defined via the projection π and the iteration of f_t as h_{E_t}(P) := ½ ĥ_t(π(P))" … "where ‖(z, w)‖_v = max{|x|_v, |y|_v}"`; it should be `ĥ_{E_t}(P) := ½ ĥ_t(π(P)); ‖(z, w)‖_v = max{|z|_v, |w|_v}.`. The sentence defines the canonical height ĥ_{E_t} just introduced; the norm is of the vector (z, w), and x, y are not in scope. By (2.13)–(2.14) the mutual energy is minus the integral of log|z − w|; the value 0 (product formula) is unaffected. The limit is over the iterate n, as in (2.3); t is the fixed parameter. - **Misprint** at Proof of Theorem 1.5, p.37 (arXiv v2; published Annals version not collated). The source says `"We conclude by the product formula that max{h(s_n − t_n), h(s_n, t_n), h(s_n − 1, t_n − 1)} > (1/8) log(1/δ)."`; it should be `The bound should be (1/16) log(1/δ). Alternatively, in cases (2) and (3), state it for h(1/s_n) + h(1/t_n), resp. h(1/(s_n − 1)) + h(1/(t_n − 1)).`. In case (2), each place of M′_n has |s_n|_v < δ or |t_n|_v < δ. This gives h(s_n) + h(t_n) ≥ (1/8) log(1/δ), but for h(s_n, t_n) only (1/16) log(1/δ) when the small coordinate alternates between places. The argument only needs a bound tending to ∞ as δ → 0. Recorded as `PAPER-DEMARCO-KRIEGER-YE-20/E4`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 1901.09945 versions (v2, 3 December 2019, the post-referee version, is the latest); Annals of Mathematics 191 (2020), 949–1001 article page: no erratum listed; the typeset PDF was not accessible here, so the published text was not collated; Crossref metadata for 10.4007/annals.2020.191.3.5: no update or correction relation.
- **Misprint** at (7.5), p.41 (arXiv v2; published Annals version not collated). The source says `"Σ_{v∈M_K} −r_v log η_v ≤ 2 (h(t₁) + h(1 − t₁) + h(t₂) + h(1 − t₂)) − ½ log c(ε′)."`; it should be `The constant is −log c(ε′), not −½ log c(ε′).`. At archimedean v, η_v = c(ε′)·min_i min{…}, and Σ_{v|∞} r_v = 1, so the factor c(ε′) contributes −log c(ε′). The next display divides by 2|F| and has −½ log c(ε′)/|F|, which is what the corrected (7.5) gives, so nothing downstream changes. - **Misprint** at Proof of Theorem 7.1, p.41 (arXiv v2; published Annals version not collated). The source says `"We have for v ∈ M_K^∞ that −(µ_{t_i,v}, m_{F,η,v})_v + (µ_{t_i}, [F]_v)_v ≤ ε′ log max{|t_i|_v, |t_i|_v^{-1}, |t_i − 1|_v^{-1}} for i = 1, 2 by Proposition 4.4."`; it should be `The bound is ε′ max{log|t_i|_v, log|t_i|_v^{-1}, log|t_i − 1|_v^{-1}, 1}, as in Proposition 4.4. This adds ε′ (Σ_{v|∞} r_v = 1) to the next display, which is absorbed by choosing ε′ < ε/20.`. Proposition 4.4 includes 1 in the maximum; log max{|t|, |t|^{-1}, |t − 1|^{-1}} can be arbitrarily close to 0 (|t| = |t − 1| = 1), where the proposition gives only ε. Recorded as `PAPER-DEMARCO-KRIEGER-YE-20/E6`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 1901.09945 versions (v2, 3 December 2019, the post-referee version, is the latest); Annals of Mathematics 191 (2020), 949–1001 article page: no erratum listed; the typeset PDF was not accessible here, so the published text was not collated; Crossref metadata for 10.4007/annals.2020.191.3.5: no update or correction relation.
- **Error** at Proof of Theorem 7.1, p.41 (choice of η_v at finite places) (arXiv v2; published Annals version not collated) (it affects the proof). The source says `"At each non-archimedean place v of K, we set η_v := min{1, |t₁(t₁ − 1)|_v, |t₂(t₂ − 1)|_v}. … (µ_{t_i,v}, m_{F,η,v})_v = (µ_{t_i,v}, [F]_v)_v for this choice of η, because the potentials for µ_{t_i,v} are constant on disks of radius η_v."`; it should be `At places v | 2 take η_v := |2|_v · min{1, |t₁(t₁ − 1)|_v, |t₂(t₂ − 1)|_v}. The claim is then true: in the potentially good range the support of µ_{t,v} is a type II point of diameter |Δ_t|_v^{1/6} = |2|_v^{2/3}|t(t − 1)|_v^{1/3} ≥ |2|_v min{1, |t(t − 1)|_v}, and in the bad-reduction ranges of Proposition 3.5 smaller radii are harmless. (7.5) grows by Σ_{v|2} r_v log 2 = log 2, which is divided by 2|F| and absorbed into C(ε).`. The claim fails at v | 2 when |t_i(t_i − 1)|_v = 1. There η_v = 1, but µ_{t_i,v} is a point mass at ζ_{a,R} with R < 1. Indeed λ_{t,v}(0) = λ_{t,v}(ζ_{0,1}) = 0 and f_t(ζ_{0,1}) = ζ_{0,1/|4|}, so the support lies in a residue class of ζ_{0,1} other than that of 0. For x ∈ D(a,R), λ(ζ_{x,1}) − λ(x) = log(1/R) > 0, so the non-archimedean term of Proposition 7.3 is positive, not 0. Counterexample: take ω a primitive cube root of 1 and t₁ ≠ t₂ the roots of t² − (6ω² − 4)t − (3ω − 4) = 0 (discriminant 48ω(1 − ω) ≠ 0). Then ψ₃(ω, t_i) = 0, so x = ω ∈ π(E_{t₁}[3]) ∩ π(E_{t₂}[3]) ⊆ F. Also t₁t₂ = 4 − 3ω and (t₁ − 1)(t₂ − 1) = 15 + 3ω are 2-adic units, so |t_i(t_i − 1)|_v = 1 and η_v = 1. The reduction is supersingular (|j(t_i)|_v ≤ |2|^8), so the 3-torsion reduces to non-identity points and x ∈ D(a_i, R_i). The bound therefore acquires a positive term that is not divided by |F|, which would break the small-height case of Theorem 8.1. With the corrected η_v the equality holds and all later statements stand. Recorded as `PAPER-DEMARCO-KRIEGER-YE-20/E7`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 1901.09945 versions (v2, 3 December 2019, the post-referee version, is the latest); Annals of Mathematics 191 (2020), 949–1001 article page: no erratum listed; the typeset PDF was not accessible here, so the published text was not collated; Crossref metadata for 10.4007/annals.2020.191.3.5: no update or correction relation.
- **Gap** at Proof of Theorem 1.9, §9.4, p.47 (arXiv v2; published Annals version not collated) (it affects the proof). The source says `"Restricting to the points x ∈ j_P(X)(Q̄), so that π(Φ(x)₁) = π(Φ(x)₂) in ℙ¹, the theorem now follows from Theorem 8.1."`; it should be `The identity π(Φ(x)₁) = π(Φ(x)₂) holds on j_Q(X) for the Weierstrass point Q of Proposition 9.1. For another Weierstrass point P, j_P(X) = j_Q(X) + T with T = [Q − P] ∈ J(X)[2]. Since L_X is symmetric, ĥ_{L_X} is a quadratic form whose bilinear pairing kills torsion, so ĥ_{L_X}(x + T) = ĥ_{L_X}(x), and the count for P equals the count for Q. This is the analogue of the step written out for Theorem 1.1 in §9.3.`. For P ≠ Q, Φ(j_P(X)) = Φ(j_Q(X)) + Φ(T) is a 2-torsion translate of the lift of the diagonal, and π(Φ(x)₁) ≠ π(Φ(x)₂) in general, so the displayed restriction does not apply as written. Recorded as `PAPER-DEMARCO-KRIEGER-YE-20/E8`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 1901.09945 versions (v2, 3 December 2019, the post-referee version, is the latest); Annals of Mathematics 191 (2020), 949–1001 article page: no erratum listed; the typeset PDF was not accessible here, so the published text was not collated; Crossref metadata for 10.4007/annals.2020.191.3.5: no update or correction relation.

### DeMarco–Mavraki–Ye, "Bounded geometry for PCF-special subvarieties", Forum of Mathematics, Pi (2026) (`PAPER-DEMARCO-MAVRAKI-YE-26`)

- **Misprint** at Proof of Proposition 3.1, p.10 (published version). The source says `"If ℓ = 1, then we are done, since k = r + 1 and T_bif^{∧(r+1)} ∧ [Y] in M_d^cm."`; it should be `"… and T_bif^{∧(r+1)} ∧ [Y] ≠ 0 in M_d^cm."`. The sentence asserts the non-vanishing obtained from [GOV, Lemma 6.8] in the preceding line; the relation symbol is missing. Recorded as `PAPER-DEMARCO-MAVRAKI-YE-26/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2405.17343 versions (v3, 10 January 2026); Forum of Mathematics, Pi 14 (2026) e4 article page (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1017/fmp.2026.10024: no update or correction relation.
- **Misprint** at Proof of Lemma 4.1, p.17 (published version). The source says `"M_i = (ρ_ε/ρ)^{i−1}(M₁ + 1) + (1/ρ^i) Σ_{j=1}^{i−2} ρ_ε^j."`; it should be `M_i = (ρ_ε/ρ)^{i−1}(M₁ + 1) + (1/ρ^{i−1}) Σ_{j=1}^{i−2} ρ_ε^j.`. From M₂ = (ρ_ε/ρ)(M₁ + 1) and M_{i+1} = ρ_ε/ρ^i + (ρ_ε/ρ)M_i one gets M₃ = ρ_ε/ρ² + (ρ_ε/ρ)²(M₁ + 1), whereas the printed formula gives ρ_ε/ρ³. The corrected tail is ≤ (ρ_ε/ρ)^{i−1}/(ρ_ε − 1), so M_i → 0 still holds. Recorded as `PAPER-DEMARCO-MAVRAKI-YE-26/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2405.17343 versions (v3, 10 January 2026); Forum of Mathematics, Pi 14 (2026) e4 article page (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1017/fmp.2026.10024: no update or correction relation.
- **Misprint** at §5, pp.19–20 (definition of Σ^r_{λ,ε}) (published version). The source says `"Σ^r_{λ,ε} := {f ∈ X_λ ∩ M_d(Q̄) : ĥ_crit(f) < (ε/(ℓ+1)) max{1, h_Ch(f)}} ⊂ X_λ"`; it should be `max{1, h_Ch(λ)}.`. h_Ch is a height on the Chow variety, evaluated at the parameter λ of the cycle X_λ as in Theorem 1.8 and in the curve case (p.19); f is a point of M_d. Recorded as `PAPER-DEMARCO-MAVRAKI-YE-26/E3`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2405.17343 versions (v3, 10 January 2026); Forum of Mathematics, Pi 14 (2026) e4 article page (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1017/fmp.2026.10024: no update or correction relation.
- **Gap** at Proof of Theorems 1.1 and 1.2, §5, pp.19–20 (complex parameters) (published version) (it affects the proof). The source says `"… and to Σ_{λ,ε} := {f ∈ C_λ ∩ M_d(Q̄) : ĥ_crit(f) < ε/(ℓ+1)} ⊂ C_λ for λ ∈ U(ℂ) ∖ U(Q̄) … repeating this procedure provides uniform bounds on the Zariski closures of Σ^r_{λ,ε′} for all ε′ < ε and of X_λ ∩ PCF_d for all λ ∈ U(ℂ)."`; it should be `For λ ∈ V(ℂ) ∖ V(Q̄), X_λ ∩ PCF_d can contain flexible Lattès maps not defined over Q̄. These do not lie in Σ_{λ,ε}, whose points are in M_d(Q̄). Since X_λ ∩ PCF_d ∖ M_d(Q̄) ⊂ L_d (Thurston rigidity, §2.2), add the components of X_λ ∩ L_d. By Bézout they number at most D·deg L_d plus the number of components of L_d, with degrees ≤ max{deg L_d, 1}, uniformly in λ. The theorems are unaffected; only N and B change.`. The count in §5 bounds only algebraic points of small critical height, but Theorem 1.1 is stated for arbitrary complex X and all PCF points. Flexible Lattès maps are PCF and form a curve L_d ⊂ M_d in square degrees whose points with transcendental j-invariant are not defined over Q̄. So for d a square and transcendental λ, a curve C_λ can meet L_d in PCF points outside Σ_{λ,ε}. Recorded as `PAPER-DEMARCO-MAVRAKI-YE-26/E4`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2405.17343 versions (v3, 10 January 2026); Forum of Mathematics, Pi 14 (2026) e4 article page (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1017/fmp.2026.10024: no update or correction relation.
- **Misprint** at Proof of Theorem 1.8, (4.3), p.14 (published version). The source says `"h_{M₁}(λ₀, t₁, …, t_{ℓ+1}) = Σ_{i=1}^ℓ h_crit(f_i) + Σ_{j=1}^r ĥ_{f_{λ₀,t_{ℓ+1}}}(c_j(λ₀, t_{ℓ+1}))" (and likewise for M₂)`; it should be `h_{M₁} = κ Σ_{i=1}^ℓ ĥ_crit(f_i) + Σ_{j=1}^r ĥ_{f_{λ₀,t_{ℓ+1}}}(c_j(λ₀, t_{ℓ+1})), where κ > 0 is the number of marked tuples containing a given critical point in a given slot, summed over slots.`. L = ⊗_c ⟨L_Φ|_{Γ_c}⟩ is a tensor product over all marked tuples c for f₁,…,f_ℓ, so its height counts each critical point of each f_i many times. Only the vanishing at PCF points and the comparison with Σ ĥ_crit are used, so nothing downstream changes. Recorded as `PAPER-DEMARCO-MAVRAKI-YE-26/E5`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2405.17343 versions (v3, 10 January 2026); Forum of Mathematics, Pi 14 (2026) e4 article page (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1017/fmp.2026.10024: no update or correction relation.

The preceding paragraph asks for "an appropriate upper bound for (M·F^{·(d−1)})", and Siu's criterion needs exactly that. Gao's Theorem 1.3(ii) has a dimension threshold, and dim j_ε(C_S) = dim S + 1 because the curves are one-dimensional. The source says `"As in the proof of Theorem 1.2 we use h(ι(ρ(t))) ≤ c′h_{A_g,M}(t) + c″"`; it should be `"As after the proof of Theorem 1.1".`. The inequality is established in the remark following the proof of Theorem 1.1 (p.35); the sentence sits inside the proof of Theorem 1.2 itself. Recorded as `PAPER-DIMITROV-GAO-HABEGGER-21/E4`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2001.10276 versions (v3, 31 March 2021, is the latest); Annals of Mathematics 194 (2021) article page: no erratum listed; the typeset PDF was not accessible here, so the published text was not collated; Crossref metadata for 10.4007/annals.2021.194.1.4: no update or correction relation.
- **Misprint** at Proof of Theorem B.1, pp.43–46 (arXiv v3; published Annals version not collated). The source says `"We will proceed the proof with six dévissage steps." … "Seventh dévissage: … It remains to prove the height inequality (B.1) with the extra hypotheses (i) - (v) listed above using Theorem 1.6. … (Hyp) is just (iv) and (v)."`; it should be `There are seven dévissage steps. The extra hypotheses are (i)–(vi), and (Hyp) is (v) and (vi) (principal polarization and level-ℓ structure).`. The list has items (i)–(vii), with (iv) "isogenous to a principally polarized scheme", (v) "principal polarization" and (vi) "level ℓ-structure", and the text has headings First to Seventh. (Hyp) is defined on p.5 as a principal polarization plus a level-ℓ structure. Recorded as `PAPER-DIMITROV-GAO-HABEGGER-21/E5`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2001.10276 versions (v3, 31 March 2021, is the latest); Annals of Mathematics 194 (2021) article page: no erratum listed; the typeset PDF was not accessible here, so the published text was not collated; Crossref metadata for 10.4007/annals.2021.194.1.4: no update or correction relation.
- **Gap** at Proof of Theorem 1.4, p.36 (arXiv v3; published Annals version not collated) (it affects the proof). The source says `"By the Northcott property and Torelli's Theorem, there are up-to Q̄-isomophism only finitely many C_Q̄'s … such that h(ι([Jac(C_Q̄)])) < c1. By applying Raynaud's result on the Manin–Mumford Conjecture to each one of these finitely many curves separately, we obtain Theorem 1.4."`; it should be `For each of the finitely many curves C a bound for #((C − P₀) ∩ Jac(C)_tors) uniform in P₀ ∈ C(ℚ̄) is needed, because Theorem 1.4 quantifies over all P₀. For g ≥ 3 it follows from Raynaud's theorem for the surface C − C ⊆ Jac(C): its torsion lies in finitely many points and elliptic torsion cosets, and a coset t + E pulls back to a curve in C × C with no component C × {P₀}, so each P₀ meets it in boundedly many points. For g = 2, C − C = Jac(C), and a uniform Manin–Mumford for the translates C − P₀ is required, e.g. Kühne's later uniform Manin–Mumford theorem (arXiv:2101.10272), which proves Theorem 1.4 without the dependence on d.`. Raynaud's theorem gives, for each embedding C − P₀ ⊆ Jac(C), a finite set, with no control of its size as P₀ varies over the infinitely many points of C(ℚ̄). The statement is true, but the displayed step does not prove it. ### Dittmann–Pop, "Characterizing finitely generated fields by a single field axiom", Annals of Mathematics 198 (2023), no. 3 (`PAPER-DITTMANN-POP-23`)

- **Misprint** at §3, definition of Pfister forms, p.6, in arXiv 2012.01307v2 (27 April 2023, the final author version, SHA-256 f9f26f7d…2c1f); the published Annals text (198 (2023), 1203–1227) was not collated. The source says `⟨⟨a_i⟩⟩ ⊗ … ⟨⟨a_1⟩⟩ ⊗ ⟨⟨a_0]]`; it should be `In characteristic 2 the factors ⟨⟨a_j⟩⟩ (j ≥ 1) are the bilinear Pfister forms ⟨1, −a_j⟩_b of [EKM, 9.B], and q_a is their product with the quadratic form ⟨⟨a_0]]: a nonsingular quadratic form of dimension 2^{i+1}.`. With ⟨⟨a⟩⟩ read as the quadratic form x_1² − a x_2² just defined, the tensor product in characteristic 2 is not defined (quadratic forms do not multiply there), and that form is totally singular, being (x_1 + √a x_2)². The reference [EKM, 9.B] has the bilinear factors. Noted by the extraction (item pfister-two). - **Misprint** at §5, proof of Proposition 5.1, Case 2 (char K = 0), pp.16–17, and the proof of Lemma 5.3, p.17, in arXiv 2012.01307v2 (27 April 2023, the final author version, SHA-256 f9f26f7d…2c1f); the published Annals text (198 (2023), 1203–1227) was not collated. The source says `w ∈ W_T`; it should be `In Case 2 and Lemma 5.3, W_T should denote {w ∈ D^e_{K|κ} : T ⊂ O_w}, the full-rank geometric flags containing T, not the rank-one divisors of p.16.`. For a rank-one geometric prime divisor w the residue field κw has transcendence degree e − 1 over κ, so it is not a number field and the finite primes P_fin(κw) used next do not apply; the construction needs the rank-e flags just introduced. The source says `Then ζ is an e-tuple of roots of unity`; it should be `Choose ζ in the torus, with all coordinates nonzero and ḡ(ζ) ≠ 0 (possible because ḡ is a nonzero polynomial over the infinite field κv^alg); then each coordinate is a root of unity of order prime to char(κv) and has a Teichmüller-type lift.`. - **Error** at arXiv v2, Proposition 3.2(3), Case 2 Claim, p.7 (it affects the proof). The source says `cd(E′) ⩽ dim(E′) + 1`; it should be `Bound the nonreal base change E′k′_v directly by cd_2(k′_v)+trdeg(E′/k′)≤dim(E′)+1, using the nonreal local-field theorem and the transcendence-degree inequality.`. E′ can be formally real, for example Q. Its absolute Galois group has an order-two subgroup and cd_2(E′)=∞, so the printed intermediate bound is false. The intended vanishing after nonreal base change has the corrected proof input nonreal-local-cd-bound; its general source proof remains explicitly open. - **Gap** at arXiv v2, final deduction, p.18 (it affects the proof). The source says `[AKNS, Proposition 2.28]`; it should be `Apply the finite-language QFA theorem after adjoining total inversion to the field language, and then eliminate that definable function. Retain the existing inverse-language-bridge item.`. A finitely generated field need not be a finitely generated ring: Q is not. In the finite field language with total inversion, it is finitely generated as a structure; the inverse graph has a unique value for every element. The prior extraction identified this omitted bridge. The AKNS original reading is inherited, not newly reread. - **Misprint** at Kuhlmann–Novacoski, arXiv:1311.6155 PDF p.11, Theorem 1.2 proof. The source says `σ_iη ≠ η for 1 ≤ i ≤ n`; it should be `Exclude the identity embedding from both nonfixing ranges (choose σ_1=id and use 2≤i≤n, and 2≤i≤k for the residue embeddings).`. The identity fixes η. Nonidentity residue embeddings separate the nonzero generator, and the remaining embeddings send it to residue zero. This preserves the primitive-generator argument. Recorded as `PAPER-DITTMANN-POP-23/E8`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv https://arxiv.org/abs/1311.6155, publisher https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jalgebra.2014.07.004 and title/author correction searches, 2026-09-22; no correction located. Finding scoped to the downloaded author PDF; published text not collated..
- **Misprint** at arXiv v2, Lemma 3.9(2) proof, p.11. The source says `q_a`; it should be `The open set V_{E|F} is a neighborhood of the valuation w, not of the quadratic form q_a.`. V_{E|F} is a subset of Val_F, and the chosen point is w∈V_a. The immediately preceding argument supplies that neighborhood. ### Duke–Imamoḡlu–Tóth, "Geometric invariants for real quadratic fields", Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), no. 3 (`PAPER-DUKE-IMAMOGLU-TOTH-16`)

- **Misprint** at §5, "Spectral expansion", the display after (5.2), p.961, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 949–990 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 a67de715…5f61); the author preprint of 29 July 2016 on W. Duke's page has the same text there. The source says `"Clearly E(z, s) is an eigenfunction of Δ = −y^{−2}(∂²_x + ∂²_y) with eigenvalue λ = s(1 − s)."`; it should be `Δ = −y²(∂²_x + ∂²_y).`. The hyperbolic Laplacian is −y²(∂²_x + ∂²_y). Only with y² is it Γ-invariant and does Δ(y^s) = s(1 − s)y^s hold, which the stated eigenvalue of E(z, s) needs. The paper's own (7.10), p.972, and the Stokes computation after it use the correct operator. Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction (Corrections, item 1). - **Gap** at DIT16 publisher Appendix A pp983–985, proof of(A.1); page images checked2026-09-22 (it affects the proof). The source says `“the Taylor series coefficients of both sides agree up to order 2”`; it should be `Use f=t^sH with H analytic (indeed entire), justify the endpoint integration and differentiated integrals, then derive c₁=0 and c_n=−c_{n−2}/[n(n+2s−1)]. This determines the solution, including s=1/2 and c₀=0.`. For nonintegral complex s the integral generally is not an ordinary Taylor series at0, and its ODE has a singular coefficient t⁻². A regular-point ODE uniqueness argument from two ordinary Taylor coefficients is therefore not the missing justification. Items162–167 and118 give the explicit entire-H argument, endpoint majorants and cutoff integration by parts. This is a proof-completion qualification, not a counterexample to Lemma7. - **Error** at Theorem 4, p.965, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 949–990 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 a67de715…5f61); the author preprint of 29 July 2016 on W. Duke's page has the same text there (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Then there exists a unique nonzero F(z) with weight 1/2 for Γ_0(4) with Fourier expansion F(z) = Σ b(n) W_{sgn(n)/4, ir/2}(4π|n|y) e(nx), such that for any pair of co-prime fundamental discriminants d′ and d, we have (5.16) 12√π |D|^{3/4} b(d′) \overline{b(d)} = ⟨φ, φ⟩^{−1} Σ_A χ(A) {…}. Here ⟨F, F⟩ = … = 1 and the value of b(n) for a general discriminant n = dm² … is determined by means of the Shimura relation …"`; it should be `F is unique only up to a constant factor of absolute value 1. The line spanned by F is unique, and F itself becomes unique once one also requires, for example, b(d_0) > 0 for one fundamental discriminant d_0 with b(d_0) ≠ 0.`. If F satisfies the conditions, so does uF for every complex u with |u| = 1. Indeed ⟨uF, uF⟩ = 1, the Shimura relation is linear in b, and b(d′)\overline{b(d)} is unchanged since |u|² = 1. Taking u = −1 gives a second such F. The conjugate in (5.16) was checked on the page image. Existence and the identity (5.16) are unaffected. The proof, via Proposition 6 in §10, produces F as a member ψ of a chosen orthonormal basis B_r, which fixes it only up to such a unit. Noted by the extraction (Corrections, item 5). - **Misprint** at §5, the numerical example after Theorem 4, p.966; also Proposition 6 and its proof, pp.981–982, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 949–990 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 a67de715…5f61); the author preprint of 29 July 2016 on W. Duke's page has the same text there. The source says `p.966: "we take the first occurring even Hecke-Maass form with eigenvalue λ = 190.13154731 ··· = 1/2 + r², where r/2 = 6.889875675 ···". Proposition 6, p.981: "For any even Hecke-Maass cusp form φ for Γ with Laplace eigenvalue 1/2 + r²". The same "1/2 + r²" appears again after (10.4) on p.981 and twice on p.982.`; it should be `1/4 + r² in each place.`. The paper writes every eigenvalue as λ = 1/4 + r² in (5.6), p.962, and Theorem 4 and Proposition 6 use the weight-1/2 Whittaker parameter ir/2 that belongs to 1/4 + r². The numbers confirm it. r = 2 × 6.889875675 = 13.77975135 gives 1/4 + r² = 190.131547…, the printed λ, whereas 1/2 + r² = 190.381547…. Noted by the extraction (Corrections, item 2). - **Misprint** at Proof of Proposition 1, (6.2), p.968, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 949–990 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 a67de715…5f61); the author preprint of 29 July 2016 on W. - **Gap** at Proposition 2, (6.6), and its proof, pp.968–969, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 949–990 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 a67de715…5f61); the author preprint of 29 July 2016 on W. Duke's page has the same text there (it affects the proof). The source says `"Weyl(⟨φ, φ⟩^{−1}φ, χ) ≪_ε r^C |D|^{13/28+ε}, (6.6) where … φ is any even Hecke–Maass cusp form with Laplace eigenvalue 1/4 + r²." Proof: "Part (6.6) of Proposition 2 follows straight from Theorem 4 and Theorem 5 of [12]."`; it should be `State (6.6) for the unit vector u = φ/‖φ‖: Weyl(u, χ) ≪_ε r^C |D|^{13/28+ε}. It follows from Theorem 4 and [12, Theorem 5] together with the Rankin–Selberg size of ‖φ‖ for the normalization a(1) = 1. It is also what the proof of Theorem 2 uses, since c(φ)⟨φ, φ⟩^{−1}φ = ⟨f, u⟩u.`. The forms φ are normalized by a(1) = 1 in (5.7), p.962, and the Shimura relation in Theorem 4 forces this too. [12, Theorem 5] (Duke 1988, p.85, read on the page image) bounds the coefficients of a spectral weight-1/2 form with eigenvalue 1/4 + t² by |λ|^A ch(πt/2)|n|^{−2/7+ε}. Here t = r/2, so Theorem 4 gives |Weyl(⟨φ, φ⟩^{−1}φ, χ)| ≪ r^C cosh²(πr/4)|D|^{3/4−2/7+ε}. That bound has an exponential factor, which (6.6) omits. The factor cannot simply be dropped. For a(1) = 1, Rankin–Selberg unfolding gives ⟨φ, φ⟩ ≍ L(1, sym²φ)/cosh(πr), so ⟨φ, φ⟩^{−1}φ is about e^{πr/2} times the unit vector u. As printed, (6.6) would therefore make the Weyl integrals of u exponentially small in r, which the cited results do not give. For u the two exponentials cancel: Weyl(u, χ) = ‖φ‖ Weyl(⟨φ, φ⟩^{−1}φ, χ) ≪ L(1, sym²φ)^{1/2} r^C |D|^{13/28+ε}, and L(1, sym²φ) ≪ r^ε. The coefficient bound "c(φ) ≪ |r|^{−A}" used for Theorem 2 on p.969 holds for u, so Theorem 2 stands. Noted by the extraction ("The spectral norm gate"). Recorded as `PAPER-DUKE-IMAMOGLU-TOTH-16/E6`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2016/184-3/p08, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum is linked; W. - **Misprint** at §7, the definition of Hecke's L-function, p.970, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 949–990 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 a67de715…5f61); the author preprint of 29 July 2016 on W. Duke's page has the same text there. The source says `"Hecke's L-function for a character χ of Cl⁺(K) is given for Re(s) > 1 by L(s, χ) = Σ_a χ(a)N(a)^{−s} = ∏_p (1 − N(p)^{−s})^{−1}"`; it should be `L(s, χ) = Σ_a χ(a)N(a)^{−s} = ∏_p (1 − χ(p)N(p)^{−s})^{−1}.`. As printed, the product is the Dedekind zeta function of K, not L(s, χ). Multiplicativity of χ puts χ(p) in each Euler factor. Duke's page has the same text there. The source says `"… such that for d a fundamental discriminant and d′ any discriminant such that D = d′d is not a square, we have T(φ, χ) = 12π^{1/2} D^{3/4} b(d′) \overline{b(d)}"`; it should be `T(φ, χ) = 12π^{1/2} |D|^{3/4} b(d′) \overline{b(d)}, as in (5.16).`. ### Esnault–Groechenig, "Rigid connections and F-isocrystals", Acta Mathematica (2020) (`PAPER-ESNAULT-GROECHENIG-20`)

- **Misprint** at Published §2.1 p.108 and Definition 2.3 p.110. The source says `“integrality condition”; ∇∈Θ_Z(U)`; it should be `Use 'integrability condition' for θ∧θ=0 and ∇²=0; the local vector-field argument of ψ∇ is δ (the paper's ∂), not the connection symbol ∇.`. These are type/terminology slips: Θ_Z consists of vector fields, whereas ∇ is the connection. No mathematical conclusion changes. Recorded as `PAPER-ESNAULT-GROECHENIG-20/E10`; looked for an existing correction in: Acta journal DOI page and official PDF; YMSC Acta 225 issue entry, checked 2026-09-22.; Esnault publication page item 126 and linked author PDF; no attached EG20 corrigendum found in this search.; arXiv:1707.00752 submission history v1–v4; latest v4 is dated 1 June 2020. This is not proof of novelty..
- **Error** at Published Theorem 2.17 pp.117–119, especially p.119 root descent; author version pp.13–14 (it affects a stated result). The source says `“By uniqueness of solutions”`; it should be `For arbitrary, possibly nonreduced S, require the rank-r reduced characteristic invariant computed by local Morita reduction to equal a; prove its independence and descent. The weaker conditions of support on Z'_a and characteristic polynomial a^(p^d) do not recover a. Restricting S to reduced schemes repairs Frobenius injectivity but is insufficient for the later infinitesimal argument.`. Let R=k[ε]/ε² in characteristic p≥5 and Z an elliptic curve with nonzero differential ω. In the trace-free rank-three Hitchin base take a(λ)=λ³+εω²λ. It differs from a₀=λ³, but a^p=a₀^p and a(0)=0. The 𝒟-module attached to the rank-three canonical zero-p-curvature connection is supported on the zero section, hence on Z'_a; its pushforward has rank 3p and polynomial λ^(3p)=a^p. It satisfies the printed right-hand conditions but reconstructs the zero-invariant connection, not one with invariant a. Thus the canonical BNR essential-image characterization is insufficient, as well as the stated root-uniqueness inference. This does not provide a counterexample to Theorems 1.4 or 1.6. Recorded as `PAPER-ESNAULT-GROECHENIG-20/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: Acta journal DOI page and official PDF; YMSC Acta 225 issue entry, checked 2026-09-22.; Esnault publication page item 126 and linked author PDF; no attached EG20 corrigendum found in this search.; arXiv:1707.00752 submission history v1–v4; latest v4 is dated 1 June 2020. It does not explicitly supply the strengthened nonreduced reduced-characteristic condition; investigate the historical correction during independent review..
- **Gap** at Published Proposition 3.5 proof p.127, truncation to T_t^(m−1) (it affects the proof). The source says `“but not through”`; it should be `Supply a valuation-controlled infinitesimal deformation whose Hitchin invariant exceeds the rigid-component length bound while remaining OV-admissible. Do not infer exact order m−1 solely by truncating an arbitrary nonconstant family.`. The nonconstant map A¹→A¹ given by u=t^m does not factor through any finite infinitesimal neighborhood, but becomes the zero map on Spec k[t]/t^m. Therefore the claimed survival of a highest nonzero Hitchin jet is not a formal consequence of the preceding choice of T. A proof choosing and bounding the order, or a different deformation argument, is needed. This is an inference counterexample, not a counterexample to Proposition 3.5. - **Gap** at Published Definition 5.2 p.139 and Lemma 5.6 p.141 (it affects a stated result). The source says `“continuous representations”`; it should be `Add the fixed-determinant condition to the deformation functor used with the fixed-determinant moduli, or explicitly separate it from the unrestricted functor and prove the required comparisons.`. For Γ=π₁ of a positive-genus complex projective curve and the trivial rank-one residual character, the fixed-determinant character moduli is a point. Unrestricted characters nevertheless deform along the pro-p part of Γ^ab into 1+tF_p[[t]]. Their completed local character ring is not Artinian. Thus the functor as literally defined cannot equal the fixed-determinant completion invoked in Lemma 5.6. The global convention may indicate the intended repair, but it must be made explicit in an interface. - **Gap** at Published Proposition A.2 proof p.154, passage from restriction kernel to zero-section support (it affects the proof). The source says `“has support”`; it should be `Replace the support inference by an actual relative Cartier/PD or Brauer-class calculation proving equality of m*𝒟 and r*𝒟 in the relevant Brauer group. Vanishing when t=1 is not support on the cotangent zero-section.`. Locally θ=ξ dx and t=1+ε with ε^p=0, so m*θ−r*θ=εξ dx. It restricts to zero at ε=0 but remains nonzero after inverting ξ. Thus it is not supported at ξ=0. The two thickenings in the printed step are different. This disproves the support inference, not the asserted equality of Brauer classes; Theorem A.4 and Corollary A.7 remain source targets with this proof gate. - **Gap** at Published Theorem 6.1 proof p.146, generic vanishing deduction (it affects the proof). The source says `“infinitely many prime numbers”`; it should be `Run the good-prime construction on every relevant nonempty arithmetic open (or after inverting a coefficient of the nonzero section) to prove density sufficient to force the Kodaira–Spencer map to vanish.`. Infinitely many closed points of different residue characteristics need not be dense in a positive-dimensional arithmetic base: (p,t) for all primes p in Spec Z[t] all lie in V(t). A nonzero section t vanishes there. Cassels's embedding construction may furnish the required strengthened choice, but infinitude alone is not the stated deduction. - **Misprint** at Published Remark 2.16(a) p.116 and tautological-section notation pp.116–117 (it affects a stated result). The source says `“vector space”`; it should be `The Hitchin coefficients parametrize the affine space of MONIC TRACE-FREE degree-r characteristic expressions, not the full vector space of degree-r sections. For Z'=P¹ the stated trace-free Hitchin base is zero, whereas the full homogeneous degree-r section space still contains k·λ^r. It therefore cannot be the claimed vector space. ### Weibo Fu, "Sharp bounds for multiplicities of Bianchi modular forms", Annals of Mathematics 200 (2024), no. 1 (`PAPER-FU-24`)

- **Gap** at Equation (2) and the paragraph after it, p.2, and Corollary 1.3, p.3; compared with Marshall 2012, §2.1, equation (4), in arXiv 2201.11190v2 (15 February 2024, SHA-256 d71e9d3f…2614), the version the extraction read; the published Annals text (200 (2024), 123–152) was not collated (it affects the proof). The source says `"… the Eichler–Shimura isomorphism summarized as dim_C H_c^{r1+r2}(Y(K_f), W_k) = 2^{r1} dim_C S_k(K_f) (2) in [Mar12]." "This space corresponds to the first compactly supported cohomology …" Corollary 1.3: "dim_C H^n_c(Y(K_f), W_k) ∼_{K_f} k" for n = 1, 2.`; it should be `In [Mar12] H_c denotes cuspidal cohomology (inside ordinary cohomology), which is what (2) describes. For compactly supported cohomology, Corollary 1.3 needs a boundary estimate: for c cusps and n = 1, 2, |dim H^n_c − dim H^n| ≤ 3c (a cusp-torus Koszul computation with dimensions (1,2,1)), with the trivial coefficient system treated separately.`. Fu attributes (2) to Marshall but reads Marshall's H_c as compactly supported cohomology. The difference is a boundary term bounded in terms of the number of cusps, so the two-sided growth in Corollary 1.3 survives, but the paper does not supply that step. Converted from the extraction's older-form finding S1 ("source-convention mismatch; boundary proof required"): finding: Marshall defines H_c as cuspidal cohomology inside ordinary cohomology, whereas Fu describes it as compactly supported. Marshall also states the displayed dimension identity for nontrivial coefficient systems. Resolution recorded there: Use the cuspidal injection for the upper bound. For the full compact-support interpretation retain an explicit boundary long-exact-sequence calculation and Poincare-Lefschetz comparison, treating the trivial coefficient separately. The two-sided Bianchi result uses the specified base-change lower-bound level class. The continuation gives a cusp-torus Koszul argument with dimensions(1,2,1) and resulting |dim H_c^i−dim H^i|<=3c for i=1,2 and c cusps; its geometric and chain-comparison imports remain explicit. Items: cuspidal-support-dictionary, bianchi-boundary-estimate, bianchi-cohomological-sharpness. - **Error** at §4, equation (16), p.13, in arXiv 2201.11190v2 (15 February 2024, SHA-256 d71e9d3f…2614), the version the extraction read; the published Annals text (200 (2024), 123–152) was not collated. The source says `"We let D_r(G, K) := completion of D(G, K) with respect to the norm ‖·‖_r. As a K-Banach space, D_r(G, K) = {Σ_α λ_α b^α | λ_α ∈ K, sup_{α∈N^d} |λ_α| r^{|α|} < ∞}. (16)"`; it should be `The completion consists of the series with |λ_α| r^{|α|} → 0. The space with sup < ∞ is the larger Banach space (Schneider–Teitelbaum's D_{<r}-type space).`. Example: r = p^{−1/p} and coefficients c_{pj} = p^{−j} give a bounded weighted sequence of norm 1 that does not tend to zero, so it is not in the completion. The Fréchet algebra D(G,K) = lim_r D_r(G,K) is the same with either description, so the later arguments are unaffected; the proof of Theorem 5.7 cites (16) for a density statement that holds for the completion. Converted from the extraction's older-form finding S2 ("explicit definition correction"): finding: The norm-completion description of D_r needs weighted coefficients tending to zero. Equation (16) gives only a boundedness condition; Schneider-Teitelbaum distinguishes the larger bounded space D_<r. Resolution recorded there: Keep D_r and D_<r separate. For example r=p^(-1/p) and coefficients c_(pj)=p^(-j) give a bounded weighted sequence of value 1 which does not tend to zero. At every radius simultaneously the two descriptions give the same Frechet space, but not at one fixed radius. Items: distribution-radius-completion, frechet-stein.
