- **Gap** at Proof of Lemma 11.1, p.969, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 189 (2019), 945–978 (public copy of the published PDF at bimsa.net, SHA-256 1eab0797…a0ab) (it affects the proof). The source says `"We partition X_{d,d} into subsets X^i_{d,d} consisting of all pairs (φ, ψ) satisfying Stab φ_i = Stab φ_{i+1}, where φ_i denotes φ up to the i-th order, and i is the smallest such integer. Note that this is indeed a partition since each time Stab φ_i ≠ Stab φ_{i+1} the dimension of Stab φ_{i+1} is at least 1 smaller than that of Stab φ_i."`; it should be `A strict inclusion Stab φ_{i+1} ⊊ Stab φ_i of algebraic subgroups need not lower the dimension. So the choice m = d = dim G, and the claim that the X^i_{d,d} cover X_{d,d}, need a further argument that bounds the number of dimension-preserving drops uniformly, for example through the component groups or via Scanlon's finite-order orbit results.`. A proper closed subgroup of the same dimension is a union of fewer connected components of the same identity component. Along the chain of stabilizers, such drops can occur without any drop in dimension, and the argument counts only dimension drops. The lemma asserts only that some m = m(G, q, k) exists; the proof's value m = d is not established. Noted by the extraction (the report's check 4). Recorded as `PAPER-MOK-PILA-TSIMERMAN-19/E4`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2019/189-3/p07 and the Crossref record of DOI 10.4007/annals.2019.189.3.7, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum or update; arXiv 1711.02189: v1 (6 November 2017), v2 (25 November 2017), v3 (20 September 2018, the latest); web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Error** at Theorem 11.3 and its proof, p.971, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 189 (2019), 945–978 (public copy of the published PDF at bimsa.net, SHA-256 1eab0797…a0ab) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Theorem 11.3. Let k be a positive integer, r ≥ 2, and m as above. Consider a map (w, u, v) : Δ^k → Ω̂ × X × J^n_r X. If the image of (w, u, v) lands in V_{k,m} × V_{n,r} and the image of Tv lands in W_{n,r}, and if v restricts to u, then there exists g ∈ G(C) such that (w, u, v) = L(w, g, r)." Proof: "… Thus we may write g(t) = gn(t) for a function n(t) ∈ N. However, since v restricts to u, it follows that n(t) is the identity function."`; it should be `"v restricts to u" is not defined, and read as the zeroth-order condition used in the proof, namely that the value of v is u, the theorem is false. A first-order compatibility must be added: du equals the first-order part of v applied to dw, as holds for every L(w, g, r), or the full chain-rule compatibility of the jet v along w with the jets of u. The proof's last step must use it.`. Work locally on a modular curve (n = k = 1) with Γ torsion-free, near a point where q′ ≠ 0. Put w(t) = 2t, u(t) = q(t) and v(t) = J_r q(id_r(t)). Then:
- The m-jets of u are those of q ∘ (z ↦ z/2) ∘ w, so they lie in V_{k,m}.
- v lies in V_{n,r}, and Tv lies in W_{n,r}, since v moves along the leaf through the identity.
- The value of v at t is q(t) = u(t).
By Theorem 11.2, any g with u = q ∘ g ∘ w is z ↦ γ(z/2) with γ ∈ Γ. For such g, J_r(q ∘ g) at w(t) = 2t has first derivative q′(t)/2, while v has q′(t), so (w, u, v) ≠ L(w, g, r) for every g. In the proof, the leaf decomposition of v gives g(t) = τ_{−t}, the translation, with trivial leaf constant and n(t) = τ_{−t} ≠ id, although the zeroth-order condition holds. Every L(w, g, r) satisfies du = v_1 dw, which fails here (q′ ≠ 2q′). The same wording is in arXiv v3. The Ax–Schanuel theorems themselves are not claimed to fail. Noted by the extraction (item triple-characterization-obligation), which gave this example. Recorded as `PAPER-MOK-PILA-TSIMERMAN-19/E5`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2019/189-3/p07 and the Crossref record of DOI 10.4007/annals.2019.189.3.7, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum or update; arXiv 1711.02189: v1 (6 November 2017), v2 (25 November 2017), v3 (20 September 2018, the latest); web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Gap** at §12.4, direct proof of Theorem 9.1 from Theorem 12.1, pp.974–975, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 189 (2019), 945–978 (public copy of the published PDF at bimsa.net, SHA-256 1eab0797…a0ab). The source says `"On the other hand, since r has full rank, by our non-degeneracy assumption we may find a map φ : Ω → Δ^k such that (z, r, s, ...) ∘ φ is the identity, and thus the two fields are equal." and "(z, x, y) is a uniformized locus of rank k = rank(z) = dim U."`; it should be `Obtain the field equality C(z, r, s, …; x, R, S, …) = C(z, r, s, …; x, q_i^{(ν)} ∘ z) by linear algebra. The matrix r(t) is invertible, so ∂q_i/∂z_p(z(t)) = Σ_j R^i_j(t)(r(t)^{−1})^j_p, and the higher derivatives of q at z(t) are recovered recursively from S, s, r, and so on. Do not identify rank(z) with dim U; the final dimension count uses dim(z).`. Along a k-dimensional locus U of non-degenerate jets, the base point z(t) can be constant, or of rank less than n, while the formal first-order coordinates r(t) vary. So no φ with z ∘ φ = id need exist, and rank(z) can be smaller than dim U. Full rank of the formal matrix r is what makes the linear recovery work. The concluding inequality dim Zcl(A, B) − dim Zcl(A) ≥ dim(A, B) − dim A only needs dim(z). §12.4 is a second proof of Theorem 9.1, which is proved directly in §10, so no result depends on it. Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction (the report's check 6). Recorded as `PAPER-MOK-PILA-TSIMERMAN-19/E6`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2019/189-3/p07 and the Crossref record of DOI 10.4007/annals.2019.189.3.7, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum or update; arXiv 1711.02189: v1 (6 November 2017), v2 (25 November 2017), v3 (20 September 2018, the latest); web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found.

### Richard–Yafaev, "Generalised André-Pink-Zannier conjecture for Shimura varieties of Abelian type", Publications Mathématiques de l'IHÉS (2025) (`PAPER-RICHARD-YAFAEV-25`)

- **Misprint** at Proof of Theorem 4.7, number field case, p.263 (published version). The source says `"It follows that, when ℓ > max{M_A; M(A, K, d)} the map Ẑ[U′] → Z → Z_ℓ = 𝔽_ℓ[U′(ℓ)] is surjective."`; it should be `ℓ > max{M_A; M′(A, K, d)}.`. M(A, K, d) is the constant being defined, as a product over ℓ ≤ max{M_A; M′(A, K, d)}. The step uses the Masser–Wüstholz constant M′, as in assertion (2). Recorded as `PAPER-RICHARD-YAFAEV-25/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2111.11216 versions (v4, 20 October 2023); Publications Mathématiques de l'IHÉS 141 (2025), 249–331 (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1007/s10240-025-00154-4: no update or correction relation.
- **Misprint** at Proof of Proposition 4.3, p.259 (published version). The source says `"Let U′ ≤ U(p) be of index [U′ : U(p)] ≤ D."`; it should be `[U(p) : U′] ≤ D.`. U′ is a subgroup of U(p), and Definition 2.1(2) bounds the index of U′ in U(p). Recorded as `PAPER-RICHARD-YAFAEV-25/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2111.11216 versions (v4, 20 October 2023); Publications Mathématiques de l'IHÉS 141 (2025), 249–331 (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1007/s10240-025-00154-4: no update or correction relation.
- **Misprint** at §5.3.1, (22), p.273 (published version). The source says `"and its inverse image in U_p by U_p† := π_p^{-1}(U_p)."`; it should be `U_p† := π_p^{-1}(U(p)†) ⊆ U_p.`. The sentence defines the inverse image of U(p)†. π_p^{-1}(U_p) is not meaningful since π_p is defined on U_p. The proof of Proposition 5.5 uses x_i ∈ U_p with π_p(x_i) ∈ U(p)† and ker π_p ⊆ U_p†. Recorded as `PAPER-RICHARD-YAFAEV-25/E3`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2111.11216 versions (v4, 20 October 2023); Publications Mathématiques de l'IHÉS 141 (2025), 249–331 (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1007/s10240-025-00154-4: no update or correction relation.
- **Misprint** at Proof of Corollary 5.8 and definition of H_Y, p.280 (published version). The source says `"because H_{v′}(φ) ∈ p^ℤ, we have H_{v′}(φ) ≥ p² as soon as H_p(φ) ∉ {1; p}. It follows H_v(φ) ≥ H_{v′}(φ)/p ≥ H_{v′}(φ)^{1/2} ≥ H_p(φ)^{1/(2·c(ρ))}." … "H_Y(φ) = max{1/p; ‖φ(Y₁)‖; …; ‖φ(Y_k)‖}"`; it should be `"as soon as H_{v′}(φ) ∉ {1; p}", and the final exponent is c(ρ)/2, as in the statement. In H_Y the last index is l.`. The hypothesis of Corollary 5.8 is on H_{v′}. With (37), H_{v′} ≥ H_p^{c(ρ)}, one gets H_{v′}^{1/2} ≥ H_p^{c(ρ)/2}. The Y-tuple is Y₁,…,Y_l (Proposition 5.5). Recorded as `PAPER-RICHARD-YAFAEV-25/E4`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2111.11216 versions (v4, 20 October 2023); Publications Mathématiques de l'IHÉS 141 (2025), 249–331 (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1007/s10240-025-00154-4: no update or correction relation.
- **Misprint** at Proof of Proposition 5.5, claim (30), p.275 (published version). The source says `"We use that f := supp Z(M^der)(𝔽̄_p) < +∞."`; it should be `f := sup_p #Z(M^der)(𝔽̄_p) < +∞.`. f is a uniform bound on the order of the finite group Z(M^der)(𝔽̄_p), used to raise to the power f!. Recorded as `PAPER-RICHARD-YAFAEV-25/E5`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2111.11216 versions (v4, 20 October 2023); Publications Mathématiques de l'IHÉS 141 (2025), 249–331 (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1007/s10240-025-00154-4: no update or correction relation.
- **Gap** at Lemma 5.12 and its proof, pp.285–287 (published version) (it affects the proof). The source says `"Let m ∈ M(𝔽̄_p) such that m̂ := ad_M(m) belongs to Z_{M^ad}(ad_M(U)). … Since #F | e, by construction of U[e], we have φ_m(U[e]) = {1}. Equivalently m ∈ Z_M(U[e]). This proves the Lemma with U′ = U[e] and c(N) = e^{k(N)}."`; it should be `The argument proves the inclusion Z_{M^ad}(ad_M(U)) ⊆ Z_M(U[e])/Z(M). This is what the applications use, and it suffices there. In Lemma 5.11 the Tate hypothesis gives Z_M(V[e]) = Z(M), hence Z_{M^ad}(ad_M(V)) = 1 and so Z_{M^ad}(Û) = 1. Either restate Lemma 5.12 as this inclusion, or supply a separate argument for the equality on U′.`. The lemma asserts Z_{M^ad}(ad_M(U′)) = Z_M(U′)/Z(M) for U′ = U[e]. That needs, for every m with ad_M(m) centralising ad_M(U[e]), that m centralises U[e]. The proof takes m centralising the image of the larger group U. Then φ_m is a homomorphism U → F killed by U[e]. For m only centralising ad_M(U[e]), φ_m is a homomorphism U[e] → F, and U[e] need not lie in its kernel, because homomorphisms of U[e] to F need not extend to U. The inclusion needed for the stated equality is therefore not established. Recorded as `PAPER-RICHARD-YAFAEV-25/E6`; looked for an existing correction in: arXiv 2111.11216 versions (v4, 20 October 2023); Publications Mathématiques de l'IHÉS 141 (2025), 249–331 (open access): no correction notice; Crossref metadata for 10.1007/s10240-025-00154-4: no update or correction relation.

### Olivier Schiffmann, "Indecomposable vector bundles and stable Higgs bundles over smooth projective curves", Annals of Mathematics 183 (2016), no. 1 (`PAPER-SCHIFFMANN-16`)

- **Misprint** at Lemma 2.2, the labels of its three parts; in arXiv v2, the version read (the published version could not be consulted). The source says `\item[i)] $Coh(X)$ is a Krull-Schmidt category … \item[ii)] Let $M$ be indecomposable. Then $k_M:=\text{End}(M)/rad(\text{End}(M))$ is a field. \item[ii)] Let $M,M'$ be distinct indecomposables. Then any composed map $M \to M' \to M$ lies in $rad(\text{End}(M))$.`; it should be `The third part should be labelled iii).`. The lemma has three parts and the second and third are both labelled ii). The mislabelling makes the references to it ambiguous: the proof of Lemma 2.3 says 'It follows from Lemma 2.2, ii) that U is a nilpotent ideal in End(M)', and the property used there is the third one, that a composite through a different indecomposable lies in the radical, not the second one, that the endomorphism ring of an indecomposable is local modulo its radical. Recorded as `PAPER-SCHIFFMANN-16/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: the arXiv listing and submission history of arXiv:1406.3839, read 22 September 2026: v1 (15 June 2014) and v2 (4 October 2014), the version read, whose comment records that some corollaries on the number of irreducible components of the stable global nilpotent cone were added; there is no later preprint version; the published version, Annals of Mathematics 183 (2016), no. 1, 297–362: the journal page and its PDF are reachable, but no PDF text extraction is available in this environment, so the published text could not be read and it is not known whether these slips were corrected between October 2014 and publication; a search for an erratum or corrigendum attached to the published article: none found.
- **Misprint** at Proposition 5.1, the displayed identity; in arXiv v2, the version read (the published version could not be consulted). The source says `G^{\geq 0}_{r_s, \ldots, r_1}(z_s, \ldots, z_1;w)=X_{r_1, \ldots, r_s}(z_s, \ldots, z_1;w) \cdot A^{\geq 0}_{r_s, \ldots, r_1}(z_r, \ldots, z_1;w)`; it should be `G^{≥0}_{r_s,…,r_1}(z_s,…,z_1;w) = X_{r_s,…,r_1}(z_s,…,z_1;w)·A^{≥0}_{r_s,…,r_1}(z_s,…,z_1;w)`. Two slips in the same formula. The index of the factor X is written in the reverse order, X_{r_1,…,r_s}, whereas the definition given four lines below in the same proposition reads X_{r_s,…,r_1}(z_s,…,z_1;w) = Exp(…); and the first argument of A^{≥0} is written z_r, whereas A^{≥0}_{r_s,…,r_1} takes the s variables z_s,…,z_1, as its own definition on the intervening line states. There is no variable z_r in the statement: r denotes Σ_i r_i elsewhere in the paper, and s is the number of variables. Recorded as `PAPER-SCHIFFMANN-16/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: the arXiv listing and submission history of arXiv:1406.3839, read 22 September 2026: v1 (15 June 2014) and v2 (4 October 2014), the version read, whose comment records that some corollaries on the number of irreducible components of the stable global nilpotent cone were added; there is no later preprint version; the published version, Annals of Mathematics 183 (2016), no. 1, 297–362: the journal page and its PDF are reachable, but no PDF text extraction is available in this environment, so the published text could not be read and it is not known whether these slips were corrected between October 2014 and publication; a search for an erratum or corrigendum attached to the published article: none found.
- **Misprint** at the list of examples following Corollary 1.10; in arXiv v2, the version read (the published version could not be consulted). The source says `We list below the values $A_{g,r,d}(0)$ for $r \leq 4$: $A_{g,1,d}(0)=1, \quad A_{g,2,d}(0)=\binom{g}{1}, \quad A_{g,3,d}=4\binom{g}{2}+\binom{g}{1}, \quad A_{g,4,d}=32\binom{g}{3}+20\binom{g}{2}+\binom{g}{1}.$`; it should be `A_{g,3,d}(0) = 4C(g,2) + C(g,1) and A_{g,4,d}(0) = 32C(g,3) + 20C(g,2) + C(g,1).`. The sentence introducing the list says that it gives the values A_{g,r,d}(0), and the first two entries are written with the argument 0; the third and fourth drop it. Without the argument the entries would assert that the counting polynomials themselves are these integers, which is false already for r = 1, where A_{g,1,d} = ∏_{i=1}^{2g}(1 − α_i) while A_{g,1,d}(0) = 1. The sentence after the list, that A_{g,r,d}(0) is a polynomial in g of degree r − 1, confirms the reading. Recorded as `PAPER-SCHIFFMANN-16/E3`; looked for an existing correction in: the arXiv listing and submission history of arXiv:1406.3839, read 22 September 2026: v1 (15 June 2014) and v2 (4 October 2014), the version read, whose comment records that some corollaries on the number of irreducible components of the stable global nilpotent cone were added; there is no later preprint version; the published version, Annals of Mathematics 183 (2016), no. 1, 297–362: the journal page and its PDF are reachable, but no PDF text extraction is available in this environment, so the published text could not be read and it is not known whether these slips were corrected between October 2014 and publication; a search for an erratum or corrigendum attached to the published article: none found.
- **Misprint** at Corollary 1.6 and Corollary 1.9 i), the summation indices in the description of the Frobenius eigenvalues; in arXiv v2, the version read (the published version could not be consulted). The source says `Corollary 1.6: $\lambda=\prod_j \sigma_j^{n_j}, \qquad \sum n_i=n$. Corollary 1.9 i): the Frobenius eigenvalues in $H^i(\Lambda^{st}_{r,d}, \overline{\mathbb{Q}_l})$ all of the form $\prod_j \alpha_j^{l_j}$ with $\sum_i l_j=i$.`; it should be `Σ_j n_j = n in Corollary 1.6, and Σ_j l_j = i in Corollary 1.9 i).`. In both statements the eigenvalue is a product over j of powers with exponents indexed by j, so the constraint is on the sum of those exponents over j. Corollary 1.6 writes Σ n_i, introducing an index i that appears nowhere else in the statement; Corollary 1.9 i) writes Σ_i l_j, summing over i a quantity that does not depend on i, and the letter i is already in use as the cohomological degree on the same line. Corollary 1.9 i) also carries an unmatched parenthesis in the exponent of the displayed Poincaré dual, q^{2(1+(g−1)r^2}, which should read q^{2(1+(g−1)r²)}. Recorded as `PAPER-SCHIFFMANN-16/E4`; looked for an existing correction in: the arXiv listing and submission history of arXiv:1406.3839, read 22 September 2026: v1 (15 June 2014) and v2 (4 October 2014), the version read, whose comment records that some corollaries on the number of irreducible components of the stable global nilpotent cone were added; there is no later preprint version; the published version, Annals of Mathematics 183 (2016), no. 1, 297–362: the journal page and its PDF are reachable, but no PDF text extraction is available in this environment, so the published text could not be read and it is not known whether these slips were corrected between October 2014 and publication; a search for an erratum or corrigendum attached to the published article: none found.

### Stefan Schröer, "There is no Enriques surface over the integers", Annals of Mathematics 197 (2023), no. 1 (`PAPER-SCHROER-23`)

- **Misprint** at Lemma 1.1, p.5, in arXiv 2004.07025v3 (9 August 2022, SHA-256 ae6481f2…6d61), whose text after the first page agrees with the author's third revised version of 19 July 2022 (the journal's revision date); the typeset Annals text (197 (2023), 1–63) was not obtained. The source says `"Lemma 1.1. The group scheme Num_{X/k} is constant if and only if there is an integer d ≥ 0 with the following property: For every invertible sheaf L on X̄ there is a numerically trivial invertible sheaf N on X̄ such that L^{⊗d} ⊗ N is the pullback of some invertible sheaf on X."`; it should be `"an integer d ≥ 1".`. With d = 0 the property holds trivially (take N = O), so the "if" direction would fail: P¹_C, viewed as a proper R-scheme, has X̄ = P¹_C ⊔ P¹_C with Num = Z² and complex conjugation exchanging the two factors, so Num_{X/R} is not constant. Noted by the extraction (item 2). Recorded as `PAPER-SCHROER-23/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2023/197-1/p01, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum is linked; arXiv 2004.07025: v1 (15 April 2020), v2 (20 May 2020), v3 (9 August 2022, the latest); web search for an erratum or corrigendum, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Misprint** at Proof of Proposition 12.3, p.43, in arXiv 2004.07025v3 (9 August 2022, SHA-256 ae6481f2…6d61), whose text after the first page agrees with the author's third revised version of 19 July 2022 (the journal's revision date); the typeset Annals text (197 (2023), 1–63) was not obtained. The source says `"As in the preceding proof, the two-section F′ gives a splitting Pic(Y_K) = Pic⁰(Y_K) ⊕ 2Z, we get an identification Pic(Y_K) = Num(Y)/S, …"`; it should be `"… an identification Pic⁰(Y_K) = Num(Y)/S", as in the proof of Proposition 12.2 (p.42).`. After the splitting, the quotient of Num(Y) by the vertical classes and the degree-two class is the degree-zero part; the full Pic(Y_K) has degree image 2Z. The parallel sentence in the proof of Proposition 12.2 has Pic⁰(Y_K). Noted by the extraction (report item 9). Recorded as `PAPER-SCHROER-23/E10`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2023/197-1/p01, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum is linked; arXiv 2004.07025: v1 (15 April 2020), v2 (20 May 2020), v3 (9 August 2022, the latest); web search for an erratum or corrigendum, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Error** at Proposition 3.1, pp.10–11, in arXiv 2004.07025v3 (9 August 2022, SHA-256 ae6481f2…6d61), whose text after the first page agrees with the author's third revised version of 19 July 2022 (the journal's revision date); the typeset Annals text (197 (2023), 1–63) was not obtained (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Proposition 3.1. Suppose the ground field k = F_q is finite and that the group scheme Num_{S/k} is constant. Then Γ(S_ā) → Γ(S_a) is a graph isomorphism respecting edge labels, and the implications (i)⇒(ii)⇔(iii) hold among the following three conditions: (i) The geometric fiber S_ā is reducible. (ii) The closed point a ∈ B is a rational point. (iii) The irreducible components of the schematic fiber S_a are birational to P¹."`; it should be `(ii)⇔(iii) requires the geometric fiber to be singular (as in the applications, e.g. Proposition 3.2, which assumes C_red singular).`. For S = E × P¹ → P¹ over F_q with E an elliptic curve, Num_{S/k} is constant, but the fiber over a rational point a is the smooth elliptic curve E, which is not birational to P¹: (ii) holds and (iii) fails. Noted by the extraction (item 14). Recorded as `PAPER-SCHROER-23/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2023/197-1/p01, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum is linked; arXiv 2004.07025: v1 (15 April 2020), v2 (20 May 2020), v3 (9 August 2022, the latest); web search for an erratum or corrigendum, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Misprint** at §5, the group schemes of rank two, p.14, in arXiv 2004.07025v3 (9 August 2022, SHA-256 ae6481f2…6d61), whose text after the first page agrees with the author's third revised version of 19 July 2022 (the journal's revision date); the typeset Annals text (197 (2023), 1–63) was not obtained. The source says `"Each triple (L, a, b) where L is an invertible R-module and a ∈ L, b ∈ L^{⊗−1} are elements with a ⊗ b = 2R yields a finite flat group scheme G^L_{a,b}, characterized by G^L_{a,b}(A) = {f ∈ L ⊗_R A | f^{⊗2} = a ⊗ f}, with group law f_1 ⋆ f_2 = f_1 + f_2 + b ⊗ f_1 ⊗ f_2."`; it should be `f_1 ⋆ f_2 = f_1 + f_2 − b ⊗ f_1 ⊗ f_2, as in Oort–Tate.`. With L = R = Z, a = 1, b = 2 (the constant group Z/2Z, as the text says next) and f_1 = f_2 = 1, the printed law gives 4, and 4² ≠ 1·4, so it is not in G; the minus sign gives 0, the group law of {0, 1}. For a = 2, b = 1 (μ₂) the minus sign matches (1 − f)(1 − g) = 1 − (f + g − fg). Noted by the extraction (item 51). Recorded as `PAPER-SCHROER-23/E3`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2023/197-1/p01, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum is linked; arXiv 2004.07025: v1 (15 April 2020), v2 (20 May 2020), v3 (9 August 2022, the latest); web search for an erratum or corrigendum, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Gap** at Proof of Proposition 5.5, p.16, in arXiv 2004.07025v3 (9 August 2022, SHA-256 ae6481f2…6d61), whose text after the first page agrees with the author's third revised version of 19 July 2022 (the journal's revision date); the typeset Annals text (197 (2023), 1–63) was not obtained (it affects the proof). The source says `"One the other hand, using Hensel's Lemma, any F_2^alg-valued point for Y → S can be extended to an A-valued point. Again with Proposition 5.4 we conclude that a family of canonical covering X ⊗ A → Y ⊗ A exists, whence X_F has good reduction over A, contradiction." (Here X_F is the generic fiber of the canonical covering constructed over Z[1/2], and A = W(F_2^sep), F = Frac A.)`; it should be `Compare the two covers: over F they are canonical covers of the same Enriques surface and may differ by a twist by a class in H¹(F, μ₂) = F^×/F^{×2}. One way to close the gap: construct the local cover over Z₂ (using a Z₂-point, which exists since #Y(F₂) = 25 by Corollary 7.3), write the difference over Q₂ as (−1)^e 2^a 5^b, remove (−1)^e 2^a by twisting the Z[1/2]-cover by the étale torsor s² = (−1)^e 2^a, and 5^b by twisting the local cover by the unramified torsor Z₂[z]/(z² − z − 1); then both covers agree over Q₂ and give a smooth proper model everywhere, contradicting Fontaine.`. Canonical coverings are unique only up to such twists, so good reduction of the local cover over A does not by itself give good reduction of X_F. The extraction supplies the four-twist repair sketched in the correction (report, "Four-twist repair"). The main theorem is not in doubt. Recorded as `PAPER-SCHROER-23/E4`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2023/197-1/p01, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum is linked; arXiv 2004.07025: v1 (15 April 2020), v2 (20 May 2020), v3 (9 August 2022, the latest); web search for an erratum or corrigendum, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Misprint** at Corollary 7.2, p.21, in arXiv 2004.07025v3 (9 August 2022, SHA-256 ae6481f2…6d61), whose text after the first page agrees with the author's third revised version of 19 July 2022 (the journal's revision date); the typeset Annals text (197 (2023), 1–63) was not obtained. The source says `"Suppose that X is a surface with b_1 = 0 and b_2 = ρ. If the local system Num_{X/k} is constant, we have Card X(F_p) = 1 + b_2 q + q²."`; it should be `"Card X(F_q) = 1 + b_2 q + q²".`. The ground field is F_q (as in Proposition 7.1, which the proof applies); the count is of F_q-points. Noted by the extraction (item 79). - **Gap** at Proof of Proposition 9.2, p.24, in arXiv 2004.07025v3 (9 August 2022, SHA-256 ae6481f2…6d61), whose text after the first page agrees with the author's third revised version of 19 July 2022 (the journal's revision date); the typeset Annals text (197 (2023), 1–63) was not obtained (it affects the proof). The source says `"If r ≥ 2, we actually have C_i ≃ P¹, and the canonical map Γ(C̄) → Γ(C) is a graph isomorphism respecting edge labels, again by Proposition 3.1. Choose a component C_i corresponding to a terminal vertex of Γ(C). Then there is exactly one component C_j intersecting C_i, which has (C_i · C_j) ≤ 2. In turn, from the three rational points in C_i = P¹ at least one is contained in the regular locus of C_red."`; it should be `For multiplicative fibers I_r with r ≥ 3 the dual graph is a cycle and has no terminal vertex; take any rational component, which meets the others in two points, and use the third point of P¹(F₂). For additive fibers, whose dual graph is a tree, choose a terminal component of multiplicity one.`. The argument as written applies a terminal-vertex choice to every fiber type, which fails for cycles. The conclusion still holds by the modified choice. Noted by the extraction (report §"Source precision", item 11). Recorded as `PAPER-SCHROER-23/E6`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2023/197-1/p01, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum is linked; arXiv 2004.07025: v1 (15 April 2020), v2 (20 May 2020), v3 (9 August 2022, the latest); web search for an erratum or corrigendum, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Error** at Proof of Proposition 9.3(v), p.25, in arXiv 2004.07025v3 (9 August 2022, SHA-256 ae6481f2…6d61), whose text after the first page agrees with the author's third revised version of 19 July 2022 (the journal's revision date); the typeset Annals text (197 (2023), 1–63) was not obtained (it affects the proof). The source says `"According to the definition of Mordell–Weil groups we have MW(J/P¹) = J(F) = Pic⁰_{Y_F/F}(F) = Pic⁰(Y_F), where F is the function field of the projective line. The latter equality holds because Br(F) = 0, by Tsen's Theorem."`; it should be `Here k = F₂, so F = F₂(t) and Br(F) ≠ 0: Tsen's theorem applies to function fields over algebraically closed fields. The equality Pic⁰_{Y_F/F}(F) = Pic⁰(Y_F) needs another argument, for instance passing to F_2^alg(t), where Tsen applies, and taking Galois invariants of the geometric Picard group.`. A concrete nonzero class: the cyclic algebra (F₄(t)/F₂(t), t), since norms from F₄(t) have even valuation at t = 0, so t is not a norm. Noted by the extraction (item 94), which proposes the repair. Recorded as `PAPER-SCHROER-23/E7`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2023/197-1/p01, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum is linked; arXiv 2004.07025: v1 (15 April 2020), v2 (20 May 2020), v3 (9 August 2022, the latest); web search for an erratum or corrigendum, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Error** at §10, p.29 (the argument that there is only one unstable fiber), in arXiv 2004.07025v3 (9 August 2022, SHA-256 ae6481f2…6d61), whose text after the first page agrees with the author's third revised version of 19 July 2022 (the journal's revision date); the typeset Annals text (197 (2023), 1–63) was not obtained. The source says `"Indeed, a fiber J_x is unstable if and only if c_4 ∈ F₂[T] vanishes at the point x ([14], Proposition 5.1), and in our situation c_4 = a_1^4 with deg(a_1) ≤ 1."`; it should be `A fiber is unstable (additive) if and only if Δ and c_4 both vanish at x; c_4(x) = 0 alone also holds for a smooth supersingular fiber.`. In characteristic 2, c_4 = a_1^4 vanishes at x exactly when the reduction is supersingular or additive. The argument only uses "unstable ⇒ c_4(x) = 0", which is true, to conclude that there is at most one unstable fiber, so nothing downstream changes. Noted by the extraction (report item 8). Recorded as `PAPER-SCHROER-23/E8`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2023/197-1/p01, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum is linked; arXiv 2004.07025: v1 (15 April 2020), v2 (20 May 2020), v3 (9 August 2022, the latest); web search for an erratum or corrigendum, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Misprint** at §10, p.31, in arXiv 2004.07025v3 (9 August 2022, SHA-256 ae6481f2…6d61), whose text after the first page agrees with the author's third revised version of 19 July 2022 (the journal's revision date); the typeset Annals text (197 (2023), 1–63) was not obtained. The source says `"If the semistable fiber is twisted, we have r_c ≤ 2 and n_c = 2r_c + 2. Now (11) becomes r_a + i + r_c = 11 and thus r_c ≥ 11 − i − r_a ≥ 7, and the argument is similar."`; it should be `"… and thus r_a ≥ 11 − i − r_c ≥ 7".`. Since r_c ≤ 2 and i ≤ 2, the equation r_a + i + r_c = 11 bounds r_a from below, not r_c (which is at most 2). Noted by the extraction (report item 8). ### Ananth Shankar–Arul Shankar–Tang–Tayou, "Exceptional jumps of Picard ranks of reductions of K3 surfaces over number fields", Forum of Mathematics, Pi (2022) (`PAPER-SHANKAR-SHANKAR-TANG-ETAL-22`)

- **Gap** at Lemma 5.3, Proposition 5.2, Theorems 5.7 and 5.8, pp.25–31 (it affects the proof). The source says `Proposition 5.2: "Let m ∈ Z_{>0} such that Z(m) ≠ ∅; and if b is odd, we further assume that for a fixed D, √(m/D) ∈ Z, as in Theorem 2.4." Lemma 5.3 likewise, with the proof "we conclude by [BK03, Theorem 4.11, (4.73), (4.74)], since both d_0 and k are independent of m." But: "Theorem 5.7. For every m representable by (L, Q) …" and "Theorem 5.8. For every positive integer m, we have the following bounds …", proved from Proposition 5.2.`; it should be `For b odd and m/D not a square, add this to the proof of Lemma 5.3. In [BK03, Thm. 4.11(ii)] (r odd), the m-dependence through d_0 = d_0(m) enters as log|4m/D_0²| − 2L′(χ_{D_0}, 3/2 − κ)/L(χ_{D_0}, 3/2 − κ). By the functional equation of L(s, χ_{D_0}), this is log m + 2L′(χ_{D_0}, κ − 1/2)/L(χ_{D_0}, κ − 1/2) + O(1): the log|D_0| terms cancel. The logarithmic derivative at κ − 1/2 ≥ 2 is bounded by |ζ′/ζ(2)|, and the χ_{d_0}-factors of σ_m(s) are bounded uniformly as in the proof of Proposition 5.2. So Lemma 5.3 (with O(1) uniform in m) and Proposition 5.2 hold for every m with Z(m) ≠ ∅. That is what Theorems 5.7–5.8 assert, and what Proposition 6.4 and Lemma 7.6 use.`. Theorem 5.8 is proved from equation (3.1), Proposition 3.2 and Proposition 5.2, so for b odd its proof covers only m ∈ D·(Q^×)². It is then applied to arbitrary integers:
- in Proposition 6.4 to M = Q(λ) for a difference λ of two lattice vectors ("Theorem 5.8(i) implies X^{b/2} ≪ −log|Q(λ_x)| ≪ A(M) ≪ M^{b/2} log M");
- in Lemma 7.6 to m = a_1(n)², a square but not in general D times a square.
Both feed Theorem 6.1 and Theorem 7.1, hence the main theorem for every odd b ≥ 3. For b even, d = (−1)^{r/2}det(L) does not depend on m and there is no gap. BK03 Theorem 4.11 was read in arXiv:math/0204100v2 (p.27): its r-odd formula contains log|4m/D_0²| and −2L′(χ_{D_0}, 3/2 − κ)/L(χ_{D_0}, 3/2 − κ). Recorded as `PAPER-SHANKAR-SHANKAR-TANG-ETAL-22/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Forum Math. Pi 10 (2022), e21, DOI 10.1017/fmp.2022.14: Crossref (checked 2026-09-22) records no update or correction relation, and a title search finds no correction notice; arXiv 1909.07473: v3 of 29 August 2022 ("Final version. To appear in Forum of Mathematics, Pi") is the latest version; the published text was read.
- **Error** at §7.3, construction of μ, p.43 (it affects the proof). The source says `"We define the a_i as follows: suppose that a_n ≠ 0; we define a_i = 0 for n + 1 ≤ i ≤ e^{e^{p^n}}, and we then define a_i = 1 for i = ⌈e^{e^{p^n}}⌉. This implies that Σ_{i≤n} a_ip^i ≡ μ mod p^N with N ≥ e^{e^{p^n}} – that is, there is an integer x_n of size O(p^n) that approximates μ mod p^N, with N double-exponential in the size of x_n. … the lattice Λ created this way must also have property 2."`; it should be `Make the gaps double-exponential in the discriminant rather than in x_n: after a_n ≠ 0, set a_i = 0 for n < i < exp(exp(C·p^{2c(n+1)})) with C depending only on L (so that exp(C·p^{2c(n+1)}) exceeds the discriminant of L_n below). Then property (2) of Lemma 7.11 holds with L_n = span_Z{e_j + x_nf_j}, and the example stands.`. Property (2) of Lemma 7.11 needs N_i ≥ e^{e^{D_i}}, where D_i is the discriminant of the approximating sublattice L_i. Here L_i = span_Z{e_j + x_nf_j : 1 ≤ j ≤ c}, with e_j, f_j orthogonal and Q positive definite. So D_i ≍ Π_j (Q(e_j) + x_n²Q(f_j)) ≥ x_n^{2c} ≥ p^{2cn}, because a_n ≠ 0 forces x_n ≥ p^n. The printed choice gives only N ≈ e^{e^{p^n}}, which is far below e^{e^{D_i}}, so the proof of Lemma 7.11 (m_i < e^{D_i} and (Y_Λ.Z(m_i)) > N_i ≥ e^{e^{D_i}} > e^{m_i}) does not apply. "Double-exponential in the size of x_n" is not double-exponential in D_i ≍ x_n^{2c}. The example is illustrative and not used for the main results. Recorded as `PAPER-SHANKAR-SHANKAR-TANG-ETAL-22/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: Forum Math. Pi 10 (2022), e21, DOI 10.1017/fmp.2022.14: Crossref (checked 2026-09-22) records no update or correction relation, and a title search finds no correction notice; arXiv 1909.07473: v3 of 29 August 2022 ("Final version. To appear in Forum of Mathematics, Pi") is the latest version; the published text was read.
- **Misprint** at §9.2, pp.45. The source says `"… A^+ is isogenous to B^{2^n}, where B is an abelian variety with dim B = 2^n." and, in the proof of Theorems 1.4 and 1.6, "Since s_B is not a scalar multiplication, ker(s_B − [Dm]) is a nontrivial simple factor of B_𝔓, and hence B_𝔓 is nonsimple."`; it should be `"dim B = 2^{n−1}"; "ker(s_B − [Dm]) contains a nonzero proper abelian subvariety of B_𝔓".`. dim A = 2^{b+1} (the paper's own 2^{2+3−1} = 16 for b = 3), so dim A^+ = 2^b = 2^{2n−1}, and A^+ ~ B^{2^n} forces dim B = 2^{n−1}. For b = 3, n = 2, B is the abelian surface (A^+ ~ B^4 of dimension 8), not a 4-fold. The PDF text layer shows the superscript n on 2 in both M_{2^n}(Q) and dim B = 2^n. The kernel of the non-scalar s_B − [Dm] (with (s_B − Dm)(s_B + Dm) = 0) has identity component a proper nonzero abelian subvariety, which need not be simple; nonsimplicity is all that is used. ### Shende–Tsimerman, "Equidistribution in Bun_2(P^1)", Duke Mathematical Journal (2017) (`PAPER-SHENDE-TSIMERMAN-17`)

- **Error** at v1 p.7 Proposition 2.3; final journal text not obtained (it affects a stated result). The source says `arbitrary positive d_i`; it should be `Require the weights to be nonzero in k for differential injectivity; the exact rank is the number of nonzero total weights.`. Multiplication by p has zero differential in characteristic p. Independent unweighted Abel tangent lines do not repair a zero scalar. W1 states the exact rank and overlap convention. The source says `O_ε(q^((n/2+ε)g))`; it should be `Use O_ε(q^(n/2+εg)); after division by #J use its actual asymptotic normalization.`. On the circle |t|=q^(−1/2), t^(−n) contributes q^(n/2), not q^(ng/2). The unnormalized count cannot take the subsequently printed tiny normalized error without dividing by #J. W2–W4: on C:y²=x⁵−x in characteristic 5, a nonempty open family P gives Q=τ(P²⁵), h⁰(P+Q)=1 and div(G_P)=5P+Q−6∞. The varying divisors 5P+Q all lie in |6∞|; their multiplicity-five point recovers P. ### Alexander Smith, "Algebraic integers with conjugates in a prescribed distribution", Annals of Mathematics 200 (2024), no. 1 (`PAPER-SMITH-24`)

- **Misprint** at Introduction, definition of λ_SSS, p.1, in arXiv 2111.12660v2 (16 March 2024, the latest arXiv version; PDF SHA-256 99b3855a…35a9); the published text, Annals of Mathematics 200 (2024), 71–122, was not collated. The source says `"Take λ_SSS to be the least real number such that, for any ε > 0, there are only finitely many totally positive algebraic integers α satisfying tr(α) < (λ_SSS − ε) deg(α)."`; it should be `"Take λ_SSS to be the greatest (the supremum of the) real numbers λ such that, for any ε > 0, there are only finitely many totally positive algebraic integers α with tr(α) < (λ − ε) deg(α)."`. If λ has the property, so does every smaller λ′, since fewer α satisfy the weaker inequality. So the set of such λ is downward closed and has no least element. The bounds that follow (Schur, Siegel, Smyth, 1.793145 ≤ λ_SSS ≤ 2) and Example 5.9 treat λ_SSS as the greatest such number. Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction (item 117). Recorded as `PAPER-SMITH-24/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics: Crossref record of DOI 10.4007/annals.2024.200.1.2, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum or update registered; the public publisher PDF returned 404 to the extraction; arXiv 2111.12660: v1 (24 November 2021) and v2 (16 March 2024, the latest); web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Misprint** at Proof of Proposition 2.5, after (2.14), p.11, in arXiv 2111.12660v2 (16 March 2024, the latest arXiv version; PDF SHA-256 99b3855a…35a9); the published text, Annals of Mathematics 200 (2024), 71–122, was not collated. The source says `"Since P_{n,μ}(x) = exp(−nU^{μ_n}(x)), we may exponentiate this inequality to find that (2.8) holds for sufficiently large C."`; it should be `|P_{n,μ}(x)| = exp(−nU^{μ_n}(x)).`. With U^{μ_n}(x) = −∫ log|x − t| dμ_n(t) and μ_n the normalized root-counting measure of the monic P_{n,μ}, one has −nU^{μ_n}(x) = log|P_{n,μ}(x)|. The polynomial P_{n,μ} takes negative values on R, so the absolute value is needed. Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction. Recorded as `PAPER-SMITH-24/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics: Crossref record of DOI 10.4007/annals.2024.200.1.2, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum or update registered; the public publisher PDF returned 404 to the extraction; arXiv 2111.12660: v1 (24 November 2021) and v2 (16 March 2024, the latest); web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Misprint** at Proposition 3.4, p.14, in arXiv 2111.12660v2 (16 March 2024, the latest arXiv version; PDF SHA-256 99b3855a…35a9); the published text, Annals of Mathematics 200 (2024), 71–122, was not collated. The source says `"For a nonnegative integer i satisfying i ≤ m, take λ_i to be the least real number so λ_i K contains at least i + 1 linearly independent integer polynomials."`; it should be `"… take λ_i to be the least nonnegative real number so λ_i K contains …" (the successive minima of K).`. K is centrally symmetric, so λK = |λ|K. Every sufficiently negative λ then has the property, and there is no least real number with it. Minkowski's second theorem, applied in the proof, concerns the successive minima λ_i ≥ 0. Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction. - **Misprint** at Notation 5.3, the estimate leading to (5.5), p.33, in arXiv 2111.12660v2 (16 March 2024, the latest arXiv version; PDF SHA-256 99b3855a…35a9); the published text, Annals of Mathematics 200 (2024), 71–122, was not collated. The source says `"Given any other Borel measure μ with support contained in a compact subset Σ of R … By Lemma 5.2, this is at most log((ε² + 2ε^{3/2} + ε)/(ε − ε²)) − ∫ log|z − x| dμ(x), and we can conclude that (5.5) U^{μ∗ν_ε}(z) − U^μ(z) ≤ log((ε² + 2ε^{3/2} + ε)/(ε − ε²)) ≤ Cε^{1/2}"`; it should be `The bound is μ(Σ) · log((ε² + 2ε^{3/2} + ε)/(ε − ε²)) − ∫ log|z − x| dμ(x), so (5.5) reads U^{μ∗ν_ε}(z) − U^μ(z) ≤ μ(Σ) · log(…) ≤ μ(Σ) · Cε^{1/2}.`. Integrating the bound of Lemma 5.2 for U^{ν_ε}(z − x) against dμ(x) multiplies the constant term by the total mass μ(Σ). The notation allows any finite Borel measure; the same page keeps the factor μ(Σ) in the Hölder estimate just above. The later applications, to μ_± in the proof of Proposition 2.5 on p.36, are sub-probability measures, for which the printed bound follows because the logarithm is positive. Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction (item 97). Recorded as `PAPER-SMITH-24/E4`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics: Crossref record of DOI 10.4007/annals.2024.200.1.2, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum or update registered; the public publisher PDF returned 404 to the extraction; arXiv 2111.12660: v1 (24 November 2021) and v2 (16 March 2024, the latest); web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Gap** at Proof of Theorem 5.11, pp.39–40, in arXiv 2111.12660v2 (16 March 2024, the latest arXiv version; PDF SHA-256 99b3855a…35a9); the published text, Annals of Mathematics 200 (2024), 71–122, was not collated (it affects the proof). The source says `The theorem defines λ_Smyth(Σ, F) using "a finite sequence a_1, …, a_N of positive numbers". The proof obtains a from the separation theorem: "we see that a_1, …, a_N must all be nonnegative", and then: "F(x) ≥ λ_N − N^{−1} + Σ_{i≤N} a_i log|Q_i(x)| for all x ∈ Σ unless x is a root of some Q_i with i ≤ N. For such an x, the inequality follows since the left hand side is finite and the right is −∞."`; it should be `Discard the Q_i with a_i = 0, so that the remaining coefficients are positive as the definition requires. At points x ∈ Σ that are roots only of discarded Q_i, the right side is then finite, and the inequality needs an argument. If x is a limit point of Σ, use continuity. If x is an isolated point of Σ, perturb, for example by giving the relevant Q_i a small positive coefficient at a small cost in λ.`. When a_i = 0 the term a_i log|Q_i(x)| is 0 · (−∞), not −∞. At a root x of such a Q_i that is not a root of any Q_j with a_j > 0, the right side is finite. The Dirac measure δ_x is not in M_N, since ∫ log|Q_i| dδ_x = −∞, so the separation inequality does not cover x, and the printed justification ("the right is −∞") fails. The definition of λ_Smyth also demands positive coefficients, which the separation argument does not supply. The conclusion λ(Σ, F) = λ_Smyth(Σ, F) is expected to survive the repair. Noted by the extraction (item 114), which left the perturbation as an outstanding step. ### Jacob Tsimerman, "The André-Oort conjecture for A_g", Annals of Mathematics 187 (2018), no. 2 (`PAPER-TSIMERMAN-18`)

- **Misprint** at §5, proof of Theorem 5.2, p.386, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 187 (2018), 379–390 (publisher PDF). The source says `"Define the subgroup H of ideal classes [I] in Cl(K*) such that there exists an element a ∈ K^× with t_{K,φ}(I) = (a) and Norm_{K/Q}(I) = aā for some a ∈ K^×." and later "Norm_{K/Q}(I) = r_{K,φ}(I) \overline{r_{K,φ}(I)} = aā O_K, so that aā/|O_K/I| is a totally positive unit", "aā/Norm_{K/Q}(I)".`; it should be `I is an ideal of the reflex field K*, so read N_{K*/Q}(I) for Norm_{K/Q}(I) and |O_{K*}/I| for |O_K/I|; the ideal-level identity is t_{K,φ}(I)·\overline{t_{K,φ}(I)} = N_{K*/Q}(I)·O_K (with t, not the class-group map r).`. The reflex norm t_{K,φ} sends ideals of K* to ideals of K, and t(I)·t(I)‾ = N_{K*/Q}(I)O_K; Norm_{K/Q} and O_K do not apply to an ideal of K*. Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction (item polarized-reciprocity-kernel). Recorded as `PAPER-TSIMERMAN-18/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2018/187-2/p02, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum is linked; arXiv 1506.01466: v1–v5 (5 December 2015 the last); v5 has the same regulator sentence and the same Proposition 2.2; the §5 passage of E1 appears only in the published version; web search for an erratum or corrigendum, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Gap** at Proposition 2.2 (p.382) and the proof of Theorem 4.2 (p.385), in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 187 (2018), 379–390 (publisher PDF). The source says `Proposition 2.2: "For a primitive CM type Φ, there are two elements in S(E, Φ) such that the lowest degree isogeny between them has degree at least |Disc(E)|^{1/4−o_g(1)}." Theorem 4.2 is stated for any A ∈ S(E, Φ), and its proof begins "Note that by Proposition 2.2, there are two elements A and B in S(E, Φ) such that the minimal isogeny between them is of degree at least |Disc(E)|^{1/4−o_g(1)}."`; it should be `State Proposition 2.2 as: for every A ∈ S(E, Φ) there is B ∈ S(E, Φ) such that every isogeny A → B has degree at least |Disc(E)|^{1/4−o_g(1)}. This is what its proof gives, and it is what the proof of Theorem 4.2 uses for the given A.`. The proof of Proposition 2.2 fixes an arbitrary A and counts: isogenies from A of degree n correspond to ideals of norm n, of which there are n^{o(1)}, while |S(E, Φ)| ≫ |Disc(E)|^{1/4−o(1)}. As printed, the proposition asserts only some pair, and the proof of Theorem 4.2 silently replaces the given A by that pair's first element. Noted by the extraction. Recorded as `PAPER-TSIMERMAN-18/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2018/187-2/p02, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum is linked; arXiv 1506.01466: v1–v5 (5 December 2015 the last); v5 has the same regulator sentence and the same Proposition 2.2; the §5 passage of E1 appears only in the published version; web search for an erratum or corrigendum, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Error** at §2.2, p.382, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 187 (2018), 379–390 (publisher PDF). The source says `"The Brauer–Siegel theorem [4], the fact that the regulators of E and E_0 are the same, and the fact that |Disc(E)| ≥ |Disc(E_0)|² imply that |S(E, Φ)| ≫_g |Cl(E)|/|Cl(E_0)| ≫_g |Disc(E)|^{1/4−o_g(1)}."`; it should be `The regulators agree up to a bounded factor: R_E = (2^{g−1}/Q)·R_{E_0}, where Q = [O_E^× : μ_E O_{E_0}^×] ∈ {1, 2} is the Hasse unit index. That suffices for the estimate.`. For a CM field of degree 2g the regulator ratio is 2^{g−1}/Q (e.g. Washington, Introduction to Cyclotomic Fields, Proposition 4.16), which is 1 only when g = 1, or g = 2 and Q = 2. The factor depends only on g, so the displayed bound is unchanged. Noted by the extraction. ### van Hoften, "Mod p points on Shimura varieties of parahoric level", Forum of Mathematics, Pi (2024) (`PAPER-VANHOFTEN-24`)

- **Misprint** at Published2024 §2.1 p6, definition of deperfection. The source says `with Y an algebraic space of finite presentation`; it should be `The finite-presentation condition is on Y0, the deperfection, not on Y.`. Recorded as `PAPER-VANHOFTEN-24/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Cambridge article DOI10.1017/fmp.2024.22 and article listing, accessed2026-09-22: no linked corrigendum found; https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.10496, latest listed v4 dated2024-09-03; targeted formula comparisons, not a complete version collation; https://polvanhoften2.github.io/, publication entry checked2026-09-22; no correction linked; Web searches2026-09-22: van Hoften Mod p points erratum correction dimension; Rong Zhou Mod p points appendix errata. - **Error** at Published2024 §2.4 after Lemma2.4.5 p24, sigma-conjugacy diagram (it affects the proof). The source says `"A field is locally finite if it is a subfield of F̄_p for some prime number p > 0. When the residue fields of S are not locally finite, we prove that L is EWM."`; it should be `"When the residue fields of S are perfect but not necessarily locally finite, we prove that L is EWM."`. Corollary 6.7, which this sentence summarizes, assumes that S is a mixed characteristic Dedekind scheme "with perfect residue fields of closed points" for the EWM conclusion, and proves semiampleness when those residue fields are moreover locally finite. The source says `"Lemma 2.1. Let f : X → Y be a finite map of integral proper schemes over a Noetherian base scheme S. Let L be a relatively nef line bundle on Y. Then L is relatively big over S if and only if f*L is relatively big over S. Proof. … Since (f*L)^{dim X} = (deg f)L^{dim Y}, the lemma follows."`; it should be `Assume that f is surjective, that is finite surjective, so that dim X = dim Y. The proof then applies.`. The formula (f*L)^{dim X} = (deg f)L^{dim Y} presupposes dim X = dim Y. Without surjectivity the "if" direction fails. Let S = Spec k, let Y = P¹_k, let X ⊂ Y be a closed point with f the inclusion (finite), and let L = O_Y. Then L is nef and not big, while f*L = O_X on the zero-dimensional X is big. The lemma is used in the proof of Proposition 5.2 for a component surjecting onto its image ("a surjective-onto-V integral component V′ ⊆ π^{−1}(V)"), where the corrected lemma suffices. Noted by the extraction (diagnostic 2, item finite-big). - **Misprint** at Definition of EWM, p.8, in arXiv 2002.11915v2 (23 January 2022, the accepted author version and the latest arXiv version; PDF SHA-256 5ab79212…a842); the published text, Annals of Mathematics 195 (2022), 655–705, was not collated (the public deposit timed out). The source says `"… we say that L is relatively EWM (endowed with a map) if there exists a proper S-morphism f : X → Y to an algebraic space Y proper over S such that an integral closed subscheme V ⊆ X is contracted (that is, dim V < dim f(V)) if and only if L|V is not relatively big."`; it should be `"… is contracted (that is, dim f(V) < dim V) …"`. A subvariety is contracted when its image has smaller dimension, and dim f(V) ≤ dim V always holds, so the printed inequality dim V < dim f(V) never holds. Read literally, the condition would force L|V to be relatively big for every integral V. The definition follows Keel [Kee99] (cf. [CT17, p.7]), where a contracted V has dim f(V) < dim V. Checked on the page image by the extraction. Noted by the extraction (diagnostic 1). The source says `"Lemma 6.8. Let π_i : D_i → Z_i be the map associated to the semiample line bundle L|D_i and let F be the generic fibre of π_i. Further, let C_i ⊂ D_i be the the reduction of the conductor of the normalisation p_i : D̄_i → D_i. Then C_i|F is a geometrically connected zero-dimensional scheme." The proof ends: "Hence, we get deg C_i|F ≤ deg Δ_{D_i}|F < 2."`; it should be `C_i|F is either empty or a single point of degree one over L = H⁰(F, O_F), in particular geometrically a single point. The application, that the relevant fibre meets the gluing locus in at most one point, needs only this.`. The degree bound deg C_i|F < 2 gives at most one point of degree one, but not that the scheme is nonempty. When D_i is normal the conductor is empty, and the empty scheme is not connected. The following argument, applying Proposition 5.3, uses only that the generic fibre meets the gluing locus in at most one point. Noted by the extraction (diagnostic 4). - **Gap** at Theorem 1.2, p.3, and the last assertion of Theorem 4.8, p.23, with its proof, pp.24–25, in arXiv 1702.04644v2 (13 July 2018, the latest version; its source file is Duke_Final_Revision.tex; PDF SHA-256 2eacf07f…e1bd); the published text, Duke Math. J. 168 (2019), 377–427, was not collated (it affects a stated result). The source says `lim_{q→∞} Ẽ^±/|H₂(G′,c)[q−1]|`; it should be `For G′ not good, replace lim by liminf: liminf_{q→∞, (q,|G′|)=1} Ẽ^±_{F_q(t),q^{2n}}(G,G′)/|H_2(G′,c)[q−1]| ≥ w_G n^{N_{G′}−1}, and likewise with E^± and a smaller constant in Theorem 1.2.`. The proof bounds the ratio from below for each large q. It keeps only the multidegrees n in a set 𝒞 (all n_i large, with prescribed parities) on which the number of Frobenius-fixed components is |H_2(G′,c)[q−1]|, and discards the other components as nonnegative. That gives a lower bound for the liminf. It does not show the limit exists: the discarded components with small n_i and their Frobenius-fixed counts may depend on q, and nothing in the argument controls them. The good cases, where the limits are computed exactly, are unaffected. In Theorem 1.2 the passage from Ẽ to E divides by the positive constants of §2.1, |Aut_{G′}(G)| and A(G,G′), so the lower bound transfers with a smaller constant. The proof's remark that "the second statements of both theorems are the same" should be read that way. Noted by the extraction (items 97, 141 and 143). The continuation makes the remaining obligation precise: by /307–/309 the ordinary limit exists iff the total normalized fixed-component profile is eventually constant. Stable parity fibers themselves require the obstruction test /300–/304; do not assume all stable fibers are independent of q without that test. No admissible-type counterexample is asserted. - **Error** at Definition 3.12 (from [EVW12, §8.4]), p.13; used again in the proof of Theorem 3.13, p.14, in arXiv 1702.04644v2 (13 July 2018, the latest version; its source file is Duke_Final_Revision.tex; PDF SHA-256 2eacf07f…e1bd); the published text, Duke Math. J. 168 (2019), 377–427, was not collated. The source says `tame quotient`; it should be `Let Γ be the maximal prime-to-p quotient of that tame quotient. This Γ is free pro-prime-to-p on δ_1, …, δ_k with δ_1⋯δ_kδ_∞ = 1. Since |F| is prime to p, φ factors through it.`. In characteristic p the tame fundamental group of P¹ minus a finite set S is not pro-prime-to-p. Grothendieck's comparison (SGA 1, XIII, 2.12, which the paper cites) describes only its maximal prime-to-p quotient as the prime-to-p completion of the topological fundamental group. For example, for p ≥ 5 the tame fundamental group of P¹ ∖ {0, 1728, ∞} has as quotients the groups PSL₂(F_ℓ), ℓ ≠ p, of modular covers X(ℓ) → X(1), ramified with indices 2, 3, ℓ, and these orders are divisible by p for suitable ℓ. The definition and the proof of Theorem 3.13 only use homomorphisms to F and its lift F̃_c, whose orders are prime to p. These factor through the prime-to-p quotient, so nothing changes. Noted by the extraction (item 56). - **Error** at Conjecture 5.1, p.26, in arXiv 1702.04644v2 (13 July 2018, the latest version; its source file is Duke_Final_Revision.tex; PDF SHA-256 2eacf07f…e1bd); the published text, Duke Math. J. 168 (2019), 377–427, was not collated (it affects a stated result). The source says `u∈μ_{Q(μ_{4|G̃′_c|})}`; it should be `Let u be a generator of μ_{Q(μ_{4|G̃′_c|})} (and h ∈ H_2(G′,c)[|μ_Q|]). Equivalently, stratify by the Hom-valued invariant u ↦ I(ρ,u). This matches Theorem 4.8, which takes u_q ∈ Ẑ(1)^×.`. By Lemma 3.11, u ↦ I_{F,π,K}(φ,u) is a homomorphism from μ_L to H_2(F,c)[|μ_K|], so I(ρ,1) = 1 for every ρ. With u = 1 and h ≠ 1 the count in the numerator is zero for every X, so the limit is 0, not 1, whenever H_2(G′,c)[|μ_Q|] ≠ 1. The paper has such cases. For Q = ℚ, where |μ_Q| = 2, its table in §8.2 lists the good pair G = A_4, G′ = ((C_2⁴) ⋊ C_3) ⋊ C_2 [227] with H_2(G′,c) = C_2, and G = A_5, G′ = A_5 ≀ C_2 with H_2(G′,c) = C_2. More generally, for u of order d the invariant only sees h in the image of μ_d. For a generator u, evaluation at u identifies the homomorphisms μ_L → H_2(G′,c)[|μ_Q|] with H_2(G′,c)[|μ_Q|], since |μ_Q| divides |μ_L|, which is the intended stratification. The braces in "H_2(G′,c){|μ_Q|}" are a misprint for the torsion subgroup H_2(G′,c)[|μ_Q|]. Noted by the extraction (items 99 and 144). ### Xinyi Yuan, "Arithmetic bigness and a uniform Bogomolov-type result", Annals of Mathematics 203 (2026), no. 1 (`PAPER-YUAN-26`)

- **Error** at Theorem 4.19, function-field case, p.101 (with the definition of maximal variation, §1.6, p.16); the same statement is Theorem 4.19 in arXiv v4, p.100, in the author manuscript dated 21 August 2024 (126 pp., the journal's revision date; SHA-256 b36f4860…813e) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Theorem 4.19. Let K be either a number field or a function field of one variable. Let S be a quasi-projective variety over K. Let π : X → S be a smooth relative curve over S of genus g > 1 with maximal variation. Let α be a line bundle on X of positive degree on fibers of π. Then the following morphisms satisfy the relative Bogomolov conjecture: (1) the S-morphism i_α^m : X^m_{/S} → J^m_{/S} for any m ≥ dim S + 1 …", where (§1.6) maximal variation means that the moduli morphism S → M_{g,R} is generically finite.`; it should be `For a function field K of a curve over k, the hypothesis must be maximal variation of a model over k (equivalently, as the proof says, bigness of the relevant adelic line bundles over k rather than over K), which excludes isotrivial families. With that hypothesis, or for K a number field, the statement stands.`. Counterexample to the function-field case: k algebraically closed of characteristic 0, K = k(t), S = Spec K, X = C₀ ×_k K for a smooth projective curve C₀/k of genus > 1, and α of degree 1. The moduli morphism Spec K → M_{g,K} is a point, hence generically finite, so X → S has maximal variation in the sense of §1.6. But every point of C₀(k) has canonical height 0 in the constant Jacobian, and these points are Zariski dense in X, so part (1) fails for m = 1 = dim S + 1; constant products give the same failure for the other parts. The proof itself notes that "we need the bigness of the adelic line bundles over k instead of over K", which a constant family does not have. The main uniform Bogomolov theorem excludes isotrivial families and is unaffected. Found by the extraction (items 185–189, 262–266). Recorded as `PAPER-YUAN-26/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2026/203-1/p02, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum is linked; arXiv 2108.05625: v1 (2021) to v4 (30 April 2024), all earlier than the manuscript read; no later version; X. Yuan's homepage http://faculty.bicmr.pku.edu.cn/~yxy/ (which links the manuscript): no correction listed; web search for an erratum or corrigendum, 22 September 2026: none found; the final typeset Annals text could not be accessed and was not compared.
- **Error** at §4.6.3, statement of the relative Bogomolov conjecture, p.100, in the author manuscript dated 21 August 2024 (126 pp., the journal's revision date; SHA-256 b36f4860…813e). The source says `"Let k be either Z or a field. Let K be a global field over k. Namely, K is a number field if k = Z; K is a function field of one variable over k if k is a field. … Recall that the relative Bogomolov conjecture of [DGH2, Conj. 1.2] asserts that, if dim Y < g, then there is a constant ε > 0 such that Y(L, ε) = {y ∈ Y(K̄) : ĥ_L(ι(y)) ≤ ε} is not Zariski dense in Y."`; it should be `Over function fields the statement needs the usual exclusion of the constant part (for instance, that A has trivial trace or that Y is not constant); [DGH2] formulates the conjecture over number fields.`. For a constant abelian scheme A = A₀ ×_k S and a constant subvariety Y generating it with dim Y < g (for example a constant curve of genus > 1 in its Jacobian), the constant points have canonical height 0 and are Zariski dense, so the conjecture as stated fails for function fields. No result depends on the conjecture itself; its function-field instances in Theorem 4.19 are E1. Found by the extraction (item 182). Recorded as `PAPER-YUAN-26/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2026/203-1/p02, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum is linked; arXiv 2108.05625: v1 (2021) to v4 (30 April 2024), all earlier than the manuscript read; no later version; X. Yuan's homepage http://faculty.bicmr.pku.edu.cn/~yxy/ (which links the manuscript): no correction listed; web search for an erratum or corrigendum, 22 September 2026: none found; the final typeset Annals text could not be accessed and was not compared.
- **Misprint** at §A.1, definition of admissible Green functions, p.102, in the author manuscript dated 21 August 2024 (126 pp., the journal's revision date; SHA-256 b36f4860…813e). The source says `"The smooth Green's function g_D is called admissible if the hermitian metric on O(D) defined by ‖1‖ = exp(−g_D) is admissible. The condition is equivalent to (i/π) ∂∂̄ g_D = dµ_Ar − δ_D as currents on C."`; it should be `(i/π) ∂∂̄ g_D = deg(D) dµ_Ar − δ_D.`. Admissibility of a metric on O(D) is defined just above as c₁(O(D), ‖·‖) = deg(D) dµ_Ar, and c₁ of ‖1‖ = exp(−g_D) is (i/π)∂∂̄ g_D + δ_D. The printed equation is the case deg D = 1, for instance D a point. Found by the extraction (item 192). Yuan's homepage http://faculty.bicmr.pku.edu.cn/~yxy/ (which links the manuscript): no correction listed; web search for an erratum or corrigendum, 22 September 2026: none found; the final typeset Annals text could not be accessed and was not compared.
- **Error** at §1.6, definition of stable curves, p.16, in the author manuscript dated 21 August 2024 (126 pp., the journal's revision date; SHA-256 b36f4860…813e). The source says `"The curve is called stable if it is semistable with arithmetic genus g > 1, and any rational irreducible component of C_k̄ intersects other irreducible components at three or more points."`; it should be `Count special points on the normalization: every rational component must have at least three points of its normalization lying over nodes, counting both branches of a self-node (the Deligne–Mumford condition). Equivalently, a smooth rational component meets the other components in at least three points, and a rational component with a self-node meets them in at least one.`. The printed condition ignores self-nodes. For example, a rational curve with one node attached at one point to a smooth genus-one curve is a stable curve of genus 2 in the usual sense, but its nodal rational component meets the other component in only one point. The paper uses stable reduction and the moduli of stable curves from the literature, so the intended (standard) notion is clear. Found by the extraction (item 1 and report §8). Recorded as `PAPER-YUAN-26/E4`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2026/203-1/p02, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum is linked; arXiv 2108.05625: v1 (2021) to v4 (30 April 2024), all earlier than the manuscript read; no later version; X. Yuan's homepage http://faculty.bicmr.pku.edu.cn/~yxy/ (which links the manuscript): no correction listed; web search for an erratum or corrigendum, 22 September 2026: none found; the final typeset Annals text could not be accessed and was not compared.

For A=[[p,−1],[0,p]], g=[[1,0],[1,1]], X=Ag, the corrected product is integral g while XA⁻¹ contains −1/p².
